Rupa Gosvami confirms that unparalleled beauty, pastimes, flute playing, and associates arc unique to Krsna and cannot be found even in Lord Narayana, not to mention great demigods like Lord Siva.

Thus, devasya echoes the words of the Rg Veda:

**"Madhava becomes especially beautiful alongside Radha, just as She becomes especially beautiful alongside Him. And together They outshine all others."**

Now we come to dhimahi, the key word in the cultivation (abhidheya) portion of the Brahma-gayatri, a cultivation whose course has already been well established in our study of the relationship portion.

That the word dhimahi appears in the first verse of Srimad-Bhagavatam identifies it as a marker to show that Vyasadeva's magnum opus contains and elaborates upon the Brahma-gayatri.

Moreover, dhimahi, whose meaning is "let us meditate," indicates that the type of cultivation Srimad-Bhagavatam elaborately describes, and Brahma-gayatri hints at, are one and the same.

Scripture assigns to dhimahi the meaning "we meditate" or "we hold within our mind," implying that those who are eager to understand the Supreme always think of and meditate upon Him.

By now it should be clear to the reader that the meditation defined by the word dhimahi is fixed upon the Lord. It is one of the nine limbs of devotional service, which Sri Prahlada also calls smaranam.

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu confirms that dhimahi means devotional service. The Lord says, "...'we meditate \[dhimahi\] on Him' indicates the execution of devotional service and the ultimate goal of life."

How and where is this meditation to be carried out? Motionless? In a secluded place? Hardly! In this age people's minds are too disturbed, their hearts too impure to effectively perform solitary meditation.

For this reason Srimad-Bhagavatam proclaims nama-sankirtana — active, congregational chanting of Krsna's names — to he the only effective way to remain always absorbed in thoughts of Krsna.

Meditation generally means to search for something internally with one's intelligence, but gayatri also impels us to perform a more active form of deliberation, even outside of the times we chant the mantra.

Since dhimahi also means bhakti, or using our senses in the Lord's service, Brahma-gayatri advises us to engage in the limbs of devotion throughout the day, as we constantly chant Krsna's names.

Lord Krsna and Caitanya Mahaprabhu both teach us to perform the ninefold process of devotional service in two ways: to benefit ourselves by practice and to benefit others through teaching.

Krsnadasa Kaviraja writes, "In this way, assuming the sentiment of a devotee, He \[Caitanya Mahaprabhu\] preached devotional service while practising it Himself," *apani acari bhakti karila pracara.*

The level of absorption in Krsna even beginners attain by preaching the Lord's glories and chanting His names in the company of Vaisnavas is attainable only by the most mature bhajananandis.

This revolutionary concept of active meditation is exclusive to the followers of Lord Caitanya, to whom the Lord guarantees His personal guidance and ultimate success in their efforts at perfection.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu told the brahmana Kurma,

*yare dekha, tarekaha ' krsna'-upadesa amara ajnaya guru hana tara ei desa*  
*kabhu nabadhibe tomara visaya-taranga punarapi ei thani pabe mora sanga*

**"Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Sri Krsna as they are given in the Bhagavad-gita and Srimad-Bhagavatam. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.**

**"If you follow this instruction, your materialistic life will not obstruct your spiritual advancement. Indeed, if you follow these principles, we will again meet here, or, rather, you will never lose My company."**

We may note that the plural form of the word dhimahi implies that the best (varenyam) form of meditation is that which is done congregationally. This is also the meaning of sankirtana.

In this sense dhimahi also implies that while meditating we remain aware of and use the things of this world, again defining the mood of nama-sankirtana, especially of preaching, to be one of yukta-vairagya.

I have based this statement on the following words of Jiva Gosvami: "The word dhimahi here means 'being fully aware of the inferior creation, we meditate on the Supreme, who is above all."

Who is that Supreme upon whom we meditate? Playful Radha and Krsna, who reside amongst the Vraja-vasis in Vrndavana, where the sound of the flute always reverberates through forests and vales.

The words of the gayatri reflect this ultimate picture of spiritual perfection, a picture we remember by hearing and glorifying it, a picture charmingly depicted by Brahma in his own song:

**"I worship Govinda, the primeval Lord, residing in His own realm, Goloka, with Radha, resembling His own spiritual figure, the embodiment of the ecstatic potency possessed of sixty-four arts —**

**"In the company of Her confidantes, sakhis, embodiments of the extensions of Her bodily form, permeated and vitalised by His ever-blissful spiritual rasa".**

In this way Brahma embellishes the gayatri.

Finally, what is the purpose of this meditation? The Agni Purana says,

**"To attain liberation, I meditate (dhimahi) on the eternal, effulgent, pure, all-knowing, Supreme Lord, who is the controller of all."**

**\*\*\*\***

In the Brahma-gayatri, dhiyo yo nah pracodayat ("who enthuses our meditation") conveys the ultimate goal (prayojana). The word "who" refers to our most worshipable Radha, upon whom we meditate.

Again, Caitanya Mahaprabhu says, "...we meditate \[dhimahi\] on Him' indicates the execution of devotional service and the ultimate goal of life." In other words, meditation is both the means and the end.

That the goal of meditating (dhimahi) on Srimati Radharani is further meditation (dhiyah) upon Her might seem to some a kind of circular reasoning that avoids defining a true goal (prayojana).

To clear this doubt we may recall that meditation means devotional service, and that the Brahma-gayatri directs us to devotional service in practice, which then leads to devotional service in love of God.

Until love of God is achieved, the very nature of devotional service in practice is such that practitioners are never satiated but instead remain increasingly enthused (pracodayat) to serve.

And when devotees have achieved ecstatic devotion or loving devotion, they become so enchanted by the taste of their perfection that they become wildly enlivened (pracodayat) to continue serving.

The very nature of Krsna's devotional service and ecstatic love for Him are such that no devotee ever feels he has had enough of it. This was Krsna's personal admission to Narada Muni in Dvaraka."

Brahma-gayatri concludes its message by reaffirming that mature meditation in prema-bhakti grants one eligibility for service to Radha and Krsna and the flowering of an eternal relationship with Them.

We may understandably wonder why we should meditate on or aspire to serve Srimati Radharani if our eternal relationship with Krsna is, for example, that of a cowherd boy or a parental figure.

The answer is that although Radha is Krsna's eternal consort, She is also the origin and sustainer of all moods of devotion — servant, friend, parent, or lover — displayed by Krsna's companions.

The Vraja-vasis may think they are equal or superior to Radha and Krsna, but in truth they are unwitting players in the great drama of the divine couple's loving pastimes directed by Yogamaya.

Krsna's perfect companions serve Radha as cows, friends, rivals, or contrary in-laws. And practising devotees serve Her by serving the order of the disciplic succession through their spiritual masters.

And when we reach maturity in our devotional service in practice to Sri Radha, She blesses us with a ray of divine effulgence — indicated by bhargah — and reinstates us in our original position and mellow.

The Brahma-gayatri, then, directs us towards prema-bhakti through service to Sri Radha. As Prabodhananda Sarasvati says,

**"Persons who avoid service to Sri Radha, but yearn for Krsna's company—**

**"Are like persons seeking moonlight without the moon. Little do they know that Radha is the source of the waves of love for Krsna. Such persons can taste only a drop of the great ocean of nectar."**

Whether at the stage of practice or perfection, the most relishable (varenyam) form of devotional service is found in nama-sankirtana, chanting the names, glories, and pastimes of the divine couple.

Those who take savitur as Lord Caitanya worship His effulgence, which is the halo of love for Krsna, and attain perfection in the land of pure devotees, Svetadvipa, to relish prema-sankirtana.

Devotees who take savitur as Krsna worship His internal potency, Radha, to attain perfection in the land of gopas and gopis, Vraja, to serve Krsna's pastimes, which culminate in the rasa dance.

These are the best perfections offered by Brahma-gayatri, and they have been made known to us through the pages of Srimad-Bhagavatam by the guiding grace of Lord Caitanya and His followers.

This study of Brahma-gayatri concludes with my own meditations on what we have just heard about some of savitur's meanings, a synthesis of those meanings, and the story of how Gayatri-devi became a gopi.

But first consider the simplicity and transparency of Srila Prabhupada's own translation, "Let us meditate on that worshipable effulgence of the divine sun, who enthuses our meditation."

Rather than a literal translation, Srila Prabhupada gives us its essence, which does not mention the maha-vyahrtis of secondary importance to the true message of the Brahma-gayatri.

Having directly disclosed the divine sun to be Krsna and taught us that Radha belongs to Him, His Divine Grace openly invites us to meditate on Her and aspire for Her service. Jaya Srila Prabhupada!

When I take savitur to mean Krsna, to me the mantra says: "Om. Let us chant and hear the divine glories of the self-effulgent creator and worshipable Deity of the three worlds, Sri Krsna.

"He kindly reveals to pure devotees His original form as a playful cowherd boy in the supreme abode of Gokula, where He is always accompanied by His spiritual counterpart, beautiful Radharani.

"Let us meditate on He who spoke the Bhagavad-gita, who inspires His devotees to regain their eternal spiritual forms and moods of love in His eternally effulgent realm through selfless service to Radha."

When I take savitur to mean the jiva, I take the mantra to say: "Om! The subject we must understand within the material world is the self, the luminous soul that sees and knows its own environment.

"After realising the transcendental nature of the soul we should turn our attention to the source of all effulgence, the dual form of the Absolute Truth, Radha and Krsna, in Their original home, Goloka.

"Let us always enthusiastically absorb ourselves in service to the divine couple by meditating on Them with a controlled mind, and by selflessly engaging in active service to Them with our senses.

"As a result of cultivating service to Krsna through Radha, we will, by Her grace, attain our eternal loving devotional mellow in the spiritual body of a cowherd within the effulgent realm of Vraja."

When I take savitur to mean Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu — and by natural extension, to also refer to the chanting of the Lord's holy names (harinama-sankirtana) — to me the mantra says:

"Om! Let us meditate upon the holy names of Sri Krsna, and upon effulgent Lord Gauranga, the merciful deliverer of Krsna's names. And as we do, let us inspire others to do the same.

"The holy name and Lord Gaura always inspire us to withdraw our attention from the pleasures of the three worlds and instead to fix it on spontaneous loving service to the divine couple in Vraja.

"By Radha's grace, our practice of pure devotional service will at last reveal a spiritual body best suited to assist Her pastimes with Krsna and, if we so desire, a second spiritual body fit to serve Gauranga.

"Thus, by the power of the holy name, devotees may simultaneously and eternally relish the festival of Lord Krsna's rasa dance in Vrndavana and Gaura's prema-sankirtana in Svetadvipa."

When I combine all three interpretations, to me the Brahma-gayatri mantra says: "Om! Let us meditate upon Krsna who has appeared recently as Gauranga to usher in the process of nama-sankirtana.

"Let us meditate upon the deliverer of the three worlds who frees us from material miseries, teaches us knowledge of the self, and awards us the privilege of loving devotion in Vraja under Radha's guidance.

"Inspired by Him to pursue our assured eternal identity and spiritual form, let us please Him by our pure sadhana surcharged with the taste of giving His mercy to those ignorant of that divine treasure.

**\*\*\*\***

The Brahma-gayatri reveals the Absolute Truth, and so its glorification, word meanings, and inspired meditations are like an ocean, of which I have offered but a thimbleful to you, dear readers.

I am not a very good scholar or Vaisnava, but what I have written about Brahma-gayatri the reader can accept as true because it is based on the teachings of Srila Prabhupada and Srimad-Bhagavatam.

Our good fortune is exceptional. Srila Prabhupada empowered us to chant the Brahma-gayatri, disclosed to us its most esoteric secrets, and directed us to the highest perfection proclaimed therein.

And yet, even after hearing of this privileged good fortune, weighed down by the burden of our past misdeeds, we sometimes doubt whether we are capable of meditating in this way.

Such doubts are unfounded. The slightest trace of the Lord's mercy can empower sincere devotees not only to meditate on Him through the mantra but also to understand and serve Him in love.

Brahma was unable to remember the Lord in the beginning of creation. But the melodious notes of Krsna's flute guided him, and the primordial sound om and the Gayatri were revealed to him.

We who recite this mantra will eventually come to understand it to be non-different from the divine couple. We are therefore never further away from Radha and Krsna than the chanting of Gayatri.

As Narada Muni says, "The Supreme Lord Sri Krsna, whose glories and activities are pleasing to hear, at once appears on the seat of my heart, as if called for, as... I begin to chant His holy activities."

Practising devotees sometimes complain that the indirect or esoteric language of Gayatri makes meditating on it more difficult, a difficulty which in turn leads to mechanical, inattentive chanting.

That may be true, but the confidential nature and esoteric language of Brahma-gayatri is in part what prompted Vyasadeva and his followers to give their elaborate commentaries to humankind.

The full elucidation of Brahma-gayatri is the Srimad-Bhagavatam itself; thus, the more one regularly hears the Bhagavatam, the more one's intelligence is attuned to the message of the gayatri mantra.

It behoves us, then, to have faith in the wisdom of the Vedic system, which keeps the final goal of all knowledge hidden from direct perception but makes it readily available to one with a purified heart.

Lord Vamana is non-different from the Brahma-gayatri, but at His father's behest He underwent the upanayana ceremony, receiving the mantra from the sun god and a sacred thread from Brhaspati.

Just as a rough diamond reveals its sparkling brilliance when polished, the holy name, various divine mantras, and the spotless Purana all reveal Radha and Krsna when repeatedly heard and chanted.

By meditating on the gayatri mantra, Lord Brahma systematically assimilated the truths of the Vedas and realised himself to be an eternal maidservant of Krsna, and so he glorified the Lord.

Moreover, one arrives at the confidential message of the gayatri mantra not by esoteric interpretations and insight from Srimad-Bhagavatam alone, but also by the testimony of gayatri-devi herself.

The Padma Purana recounts the events leading up to Gayatri-devi, mother of the Vedas, taking birth as a gopi in Vrndavana so that she could take part in Sri Krsna's confidential pastimes.

Once Lord Brahma was preparing for a sacrifice. His wife, Savitri, was late, and so he installed an unmarried girl named gayatri from the cowherd community as his wife for the purpose of the function.

Gayatri's relatives shortly came looking for her, and they were understandably disturbed to find that she had been taken away from their society and married without their permission.

Lord Visnu, seeing Gayatri's father so upset, promised that when the sacrifice was complete, He would personally take birth amongst the cowherds in Vrndavana, along with gayatri.

Furthermore, Visnu told the other gopas that He would accept their daughters as well. At this everyone was satisfied. Thus, at the time of Krsna's earthly appearance, gayatri took birth as a gopi.

Thakura Bhaktivinoda narrates further details of this pastime. After her marriage to Lord Brahma, Gayatri-devi gave birth to the four Vedas, the Upanisads, and other transcendental literatures.

Later, gayatri came to know that the Upanisads had succeeded in becoming gopis. She then recalled Lord Visnu's vow and began to hanker for the perfection of being a gopi in Sri Krsna's pastimes.

To that end, gayatri then took up rigorous devotional practices, and with the help of the Gopala-tapani Upanisad, she received Krsna's blessing and took birth in Vraja where her desire was at last fulfilled.

Then, on a full-moon night Krsna called for her with His flute song. Gayatri at once left her home and went to the shore of the Yamuna, where Krsna was standing beneath a tree, bathed in moonlight.

Gayatri appeared before Krsna with a complexion darkened by His own, her face sweetened by a bashful smile. With her gaze transfixed on His lotus feet, she aimlessly scratched the ground with her toe.

And so the mother of the Vedas accepted the body of a gopi to become Krsna's paramour and enter into the mysteries of Radha's and Krsna's confidential pastimes, to which only the gopis have access.

Indeed, without becoming a gopi herself, Gayatri would have been unable to reveal the goal of service to Radha, for it is said that "only from the gopis can these pastimes be expanded or broadcast."

The Gayatri mantra is eternal. How it seems to appear from Gayatri- devi, the sadhaka goddess, should be understood as a pastime, just as it appears that Agni gives Krsna His own eternal disc-weapon as a gift.

Since the time Gayatri became a gopi, she has been glorifying the divine couple from the five faces of her personified mantra-form, tasting divine bliss and giving it to those who meditate upon her.

By carefully meditating on the Brahma-gayatri, we realise our relationship with Radha and Krsna, perfect our practice, and finally attain the goal — the last of the three phases of devotion.

The fruit offered by a mantra is called mantra-siddhi. And although it is the cintamani nature of Gayatri to fulfil whatever desire we may cherish, pure devotees desire only one thing.

**"O Lord of the universe! I have no desire to accumulate wealth, nor to enjoy beautiful women, nor do I want any number of followers. I only want Your devotional service in my life, birth after birth."**

Generally speaking, the fruit of chanting a mantra comes in the form of seeing the mantra's presiding Deity. But worshipping Radha and Krsna in order to see Them is not approved of in this verse.

Oh, how we have been blessed by Srila Prabhupada! By his divine grace, Radha-Krsna Deities have been installed in temples and homes all over the world, where devotees can easily see Them face-to-face.

Since the presiding Deities of Brahma-gayatri are now so readily visible everywhere, all that is left for devotees to pray for is loving devotional service at Their Lordships' lotus feet, birth after birth.

That is my own aspiration, and I advise the devotees in Hungary — where, by Srila Prabhupada's mercy, the divine couple have appeared in New Vraja-dhama as Radha-Syamasundara— to seek the same.

Therefore, let us meditate upon Krsna's flute-song, manifested in the form of twenty-four syllables that stimulate us to accept the service of Sri Radha as the means for giving pleasure to Syama.

**\*\*\*\***

Dear devotees! The mind must always think of something. Left to its own devices it drags one down to hell, but fixed on the beautifully decorated forms of Radha and Syama it leads to pure love of God.

Meditate day and night on the sublime forms of the presiding Deities of New Vraja-dhama. In time They will gladly reveal the sweetness and wonder of Their personal qualities, pastimes, and abode.

For remembering Radha and Syama, even more important than to silently murmur the gayatri mantra thrice daily in fixed meditation, is to move one's lips and loudly chant the Hare Krsna maha-mantra.

Of all knowledge that grants liberation, Brahma-gayatri is the seed. From that seed grow the Vedas, whose sweet fruit — chanting Krsna's names — admits one into the realm of spontaneous love, Goloka."

Radha and Syamasundara hear us when we chant Their names with pure devotion; indeed, They are forever grateful for service once rendered, never forgetting the times we have chanted with love.

Krsna once said, "When I was away from Draupadi, she cried with the words, 'He Govinda!' This call for Me has put Me in her debt, and that indebtedness is gradually increasing in My heart!"

Let us therefore constantly meditate upon the all-effulgent forms of Radha and Syamasundara, who, by Their beauty and kindness, appear on earth with Their divine abode and enthuse us to remember Them.

Omkara's three components — represented by a, u, and m — are Syamasundara, Radha, and all living beings. Therefore om, as the resting place of everything, is New Vraja-dhama's seed and support.

Just as Brahma-gayatri depends on its seed (om) to be effective, New Vraja-dhama's wealth, beauty, fame, and success are dependent on Radha-Syama's being pleased by the service of Their devotees.

Just as Brahma's first words were the names of Lord Krsna, I pray that every day the first and last words from the mouths of all the residents of New Vraja-dhama are the names Radha and Syama.

It is my conviction that the golden form of Lord Narayana, whom pure-hearted brahmanas worship within the core of the sun, and who controls and illuminates the universe, Himself worships Syama.

Every day of the year — winter, spring, summer, and fall — the sun beams through the glass dome of Radha-Syama's temple to bathe and warm the divine couple with the caress of its soft golden rays.

Is that sunshine actually the goddess of fortune in a form she assumes to offer service to Srimati Radharani, hoping to fulfil her long-cherished but unfulfilled desire to become a gopi?

I have faith that Narayana and His consort serve Radha-Syama by illumination to show meditative, pure-hearted souls that New Vraja-dhama is an embassy of Goloka, the best of all effulgent places.

In New Vraja-dhama, cows peacefully graze on rolling hills rich with a kind of devotion unavailable in the causal ocean, the Brahman effulgence, the Vaikuntha planets, or the opulent palaces of Dvaraka.

The cause of New Vraja-dhama's radiance is the golden form of Sri Radha, who personifies the potencies of knowledge and pleasure that have their source in the transcendental Cupid, Syamasundara.

Indeed, Radha's presence makes not only New Vraja-dhama but also Syama Himself glow. He is refreshed by Her touch, captivated by Her fragrance, enchanted by Her words, and bewildered by Her lustre.

Since Syama— the leader of all demigods, foremost of all persons, the most qualified being — submits to Radha, who will not surrender to Her to be infused with love for Him, the very essence of Her being?

When I chant Gayatri, either in the physical presence of Radha and Syama or away from Them, my mind is drawn to meditate upon Them as They enact Their pastimes in New Vraja-dhama. I pray:

"Let us meditate upon He whom the wise worship — Syama. Residing in the image of His original abode with Radha, He is the divine Cupid who arouses in us attachment to Her and the desire to serve Her."

If we find it too difficult to fix the mind on such ideas while reciting Gayatri, we can sit before Radha-Syamasundara— or an image of Them within — and pray, "I am yours," or "Please accept me."

The Vedic tradition of spiritual cultivation represented by the Brahma-gayatri mantra was designed for people born in brahmana families, and who actually had brahminical qualities.

Such qualified brahmanas no longer exist in this day and age, and so the Supreme Lord Narayana spoke the essence of the Vedic system in a simplified form known as the pancaratrika system.

These pancaratra scriptures describe five forms of the Lord as objects of worship — the most notable of which is the Deity — and teach pure devotional service to Krsna and the rituals of Deity worship.

Devotees who accept vows to follow the four regulative principles, decorate their bodies with tilaka, and chant Hare Krsna may be initiated with pancaratrika-mantras and worship the Deity.

In this way the pancaratrika system makes available an alternate system of purification, spiritual practice, initiation, and worship, activities that before were done only through the Vedic tradition.

In addition to the pancaratrika system, another approach is current in this age: the bhagavata method, which supplants the philosophical teachings of the Vedas and the chanting of Vedic hymns.

The pancaratrika and bhagavata systems are not mutually exclusive; rather, they are complementary. Together they offer a comprehensive system for elevation to the goal aimed at by the Vedas.

The reader may recall that loving devotional service in Goloka Vrndavana takes two principal forms: chanting and hearing about Krsna (sankirtana), and rendering personal service to Him (seva).

In Vrndavana, at night's end the gopis rise, worship their household deities, and churn yogurt as they loudly sing the glories of lotus-eyed Krsna, dissipating inauspiciousness in all directions.

At that time Mother Yasoda wakes her sleepy son, ties His pitch-black hair into a topknot, washes His lotus face with water from a golden jug, and happily dries Him off with her apron.

These two aspects of spiritual perfection (nama-sankirtana and seva), accompanied by a selfless loving mood, also form the basis of all spiritual practices on earth, both in the Vedic and post-Vedic eras.

The Vedic system taught these two activities indirectly, whereas the pancaratrika and bhagavata systems teach them directly, through regulated devotion and spontaneous devotion respectively.

**\*\*\*\***

The bhagavata system of Caitanya Mahaprabhu's sankirtana movement gives special emphasis to chanting the Hare Krsna maha-mantra congregationally with love and devotion.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu said, "One should associate with devotees, chant the holy name of the Lord, hear Srimad-Bhagavatam, reside at Mathura, and worship the Deity with faith and veneration."

The Lord considered these five activities the essence of all spiritual practices. The first four comprise the bhagavata system (sankirtana) and the fifth, Deity worship, the pancaratrika system (seva).

Nama-sankirtana is sufficient in itself to take devotees back to Godhead, but usually beginners are riddled with bad habits and thus find it difficult to chant and hear with fixed attention.

For this reason Caitanya Mahaprabhu and His followers recommend that we engage in temple worship and learn to serve the Deity in order to facilitate the process of purification that removes bad habits.

But first, to qualify for worshipping the Deity, devotees must regularly chant Hare Krsna. Then they are initiated with gayatri- and pancaratrika-mantras to assist the process of nama-sankirtana.

In this way the two spiritual processes work hand in hand to elevate conditioned souls from the lowest conditions of material contamination to the highest states of transcendental realisation.

Because nama-sankirtana can be done without consideration of time, place, or previous qualification, anyone can easily take it up, just as we can begin reading transcendental literature like Bhagavad-gita.

Chanting and hearing lead one to keep company with devotees. In this way beginners develop sufficient faith and spiritual strength to follow regulative principles and become devotees themselves.

In time such devotees accept a spiritual master, become initiated, and take up Deity worship, although their spiritual practices continue to concentrate primarily on chanting Lord Krsna's holy names.

The pancaratrika system helps us avoid material contamination, keeping us spiritually fit until nama-sankirtana awakens our spontaneous attachment to Krsna. Then loving service begins.

Lord Caitanya says,

**"By chanting the Gayatri mantra one can obtain freedom from material existence, and by chanting the Hare Krsna mantra one will be able to see the lotus feet of the Lord."**

The deeper we understand the connection between the chanting of pancaratrika-mantras and nama-sankirtana, the more equipped we arc to receive the respective benefits of both transcendental processes.

Pancaratrika-mantras initially help us overcome offences to Krsna's holy names, and when our nama-sankirtana becomes pure we can chant the same pancaratrika-mantras with spontaneous attachment.

Without being enriched by the mood of spontaneous devotion, such mantras can at best free us from material contamination. With spontaneous devotion, however, they can bestow love of God.

Gayatri mantras have the potential to act like the maha-mantra because in essence they are also names of Krsna, only preceded by a bija and followed by an offering of respect and a call for meditation.

Herein lies the reason Brahma-gayatri can purify the heart but cannot by itself reveal one's eternal identity or one's love for the Lord unless accompanied by deliberate meditation on Radha and Krsna.

Om, savitur, bhargah, and deva, for example, are secondary names of the Lord that have the power to liberate but not to bestow prema like primary names such as Krsna, Govinda, or Gopljanavallabha.

The Lord's secondary names (gauna-nama) describe the Lord's connection with the creation and bring benefits up to liberation, but more importantly give one a chance to chant the primary names.

In contrast, the Lord's primary names (mukhya-nama), such as Rama, describe the Lord as He is in the spiritual world. Empowered with His full potencies, they grant all perfections, including prema.

Since the Brahma-gayatri is the monarch of all mantras and can in no way be less than its subjects, Vaisnava acaryas have revealed its esoteric meanings in terms of Lord Krsna's primary names.

In this way our authorities have disclosed the ultimate intention of Brahma-gayatri as it relates to the pancaratrika-mantras, giving us a spiritually consistent understanding and meditation.

The Brahma-gayatri is like the closed bud of the white lotus of nama-sankirtana that unfolds through the various pancaratrika-mantras into its fully blossomed and aromatic form, the Kama-gayatri.

The sequence of mantras from Brahma-gayatri to Kama-gayatri is significant. That the Brahma-gayatri comes first confirms that chanting the maha-mantra qualifies one as a true brahmana.

In this regard Devahuti says to her son, Kapila, "Even a person born in a family of dog-eaters immediately becomes eligible to perform Vedic sacrifices if he once utters the holy name of Hari."

Next, the sequence shows that the pancaratrika system supplants the Vedic. And finally, it means that the mantras spell out and elaborate upon the esoteric meanings within the Brahma-gayatri.

As I write this, the coherent integrity of the Gaudiya Vaisnavas' mantra theology enchants me. But then these mantras are forms of Radha and Krsna, so how could they be anything less than sublime!

That said, it behoves us to always remember that perfecting our chanting of these mantras depends wholly and solely upon our ability to become fully absorbed in the bhagavata system of worship.

Thus Srimad-Bhagavatam declares,

**"By regular attendance in classes on the Bhagavatam and by rendering of service to the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is almost completely destroyed —**

**"And loving service unto Uttamasloka, the Personality of Godhead, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact."**

This is the way Srimad-Bhagavatam begins.

And that most profound of all scriptures ends on the same note: "I offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Hari, the congregational chanting of whose holy names destroys all sinful reactions."

The bhagavata system, or as Srila Prabhupada says, "the way of Srimad-Bhagavatam," thereby defines itself. One should hear the Bhagavatam and chant Krsna's names in the company of great souls.

When scripture refers to nama-sankirtana, it is generally describing the loud, congregational chanting of the Hare Krsna maha-mantra in the style of Caitanya Mahaprabhu and His eternal associates.

Such nama-sankirtana reveals its full potency by awakening all good fortune, penetrating the directions, over-flooding the world with prema, and by bringing all pious souls running, eager to take part.

And silent chanting of the pancaratrika-mantras, when done in the same mood as nama-sankirtana, awakens the soul's spontaneous devotion and eventually gives rise to spontaneous love.

The history of how the potency of nama-sankirtana transformed a brahmana from Pragjyotisa-pura from a regulative to a spontaneous practitioner illustrates this truth perfectly.

**\*\*\*\***

In the city of Pragjyotisa-pura lived a humble brahmana who received in a dream the ten-syllable Gopala mantra from the goddess Kamakhya, along with instructions on how to meditate on it.

Although uneducated and materially inclined, the brahmana became somewhat purified at heart by the power of the mantra. In a detached mood he left his home and family to tour places of pilgrimage.

Once, while passing through Prayaga, the brahmana came upon a group of Lord Visnu's devotees engaged in a sankirtana festival. Still ignorant, however, he could not understand what they were doing.

Moreover the brahmana could not recognise Lord Visnu to be the same Supreme Lord as the Gopala he was worshipping. Nonetheless, departing for Mathura, he continued to chant his mantra faithfully.

Amazingly enough, by the power of the mantra and contact with Lord Visnu's devotees, the brahmana relished some semblance of the symptoms of ecstasy as he chanted Gopala's name in solitary places.

Then, as he was walking along the bank of the Yamuna near Kesi-tirtha, he heard someone chanting Krsna's names in a way that sounded like a mixture of singing and crying in pure love.

After some effort, the brahmana found the saint Gopa-kumara in a riverside grove. Gopa-kumara's cowherd features convinced the brahmana he had found the personified form of his mantra, Gopala.

The brahmana threw himself at Gopa-kumara's feet and enquired about the real goal of life and how to achieve it. At this, Gopa-kumara, the perfect guru, began to consider the situation.

The brahmana had advanced spiritually by chanting the Gopala mantra. He was now attached to his newfound source of happiness, the regulated recitation of his mantra in solitary meditation.

These pancaratrika practices, however, would not give the brahmana spiritual perfection. To see the Lord directly, in love, he would need to become attached to sankirtana of the Lord's holy names.

Once he had learned the glories and techniques of nama-sankirtana, the brahmana would then be able to chant his mantra in the way of the bhagavata system and awaken spontaneous attachment.

Because of the brahmana's ignorance of the revealed scriptures, Gopa-kumara thought that simply presenting sound arguments might nor induce his new student to alter the present course of his sadhana.

Thus Gopa-kumara decided to describe the path he had taken to perfection. That would awaken the brahmana's attachment to nama-sankirtana and his taste for hearing the nectar of the Bhagavatam.

His new student would then understand that the goal of life is to attain spontaneous love for Gopala in the mood of the Vraja-vasis in Goloka, and that nama-sankirtana is the means to attain it.

After completing his story and blessing his disciple, Gopa-kumara saw prema arise in the brahmana, who began to chant the names of Krsna like a madman. The Lord then appeared before guru and disciple.

The Pragjyotisa brahmana learned many lessons on his path, but most important was the absolute necessity of adopting the bhagavata system to realise the full potential of pancaratrika-mantras.

After hearing this story, we should not foolishly abandon Deity worship and take to exclusive chanting and hearing because the bhagavata system is the essence of Krsna consciousness.

As long as material desires linger in the heart and pure attachment to Lord Krsna has not yet awakened, we must follow the pancaratrika process as outlined by acaryas such as the great Narada Muni.

Devotees sometimes argue that it is not so important to become informed chanters by knowing the meaning of the mantras we chant or by studying the philosophy that underlies such mantras.

But Sanatana Gosvami corrects this misunderstanding by including the details of the Pragjyotisa brahmana's path to perfection, in which we are taught that weak faith is not enough to achieve the goal of life.

It is said, "To whatever extent one understands the scriptures and knows the truth of spiritual life, to that extent one will have faith that Lord Narayana is the Supreme Personality of Godhead."

The more we know about a mantra and how to meditate on its meanings, the deeper our faith in the mantra becomes. With that deepening of faith comes an increase in the quality of our chanting.

Moreover, until we are firmly established in perfect knowledge of our relationship with Lord Krsna and the role that chanting our mantra plays in that relationship, our chanting is not yet pure.

Before the names comprising a mantra can bless us with love for Krsna, the fog of bad habits must be dissipated by the sunlight of transcendental knowledge, as received from great devotees.

Just as the brahmana from Pragjyotisa-pura purified his chanting by transcendental knowledge, we may unearth the divine jewels of krsna-nama by careful study of the mantras received from Sri Guru.

By the transformative effect of the pure name, we can also perceive Vrndavana's spontaneous spirit of selfless devotional service underpinning the strict regulations of Deity worship (arcana).

What's more, to the degree that we chant our pancaratrika-mantras in the spirit of pure sankirtana, to that degree we realise that the Deity of Krsna is not a statue but directly the son of the king of Vraja.

The beautiful Deity of Sri Krsna attracts the hearts of everyone by His sweetness. He is the Cupid of Cupids and — with Srimati Radharani and other gopis like Lalita — the enjoyer of the rasa dance.

Krsna's smiling face and exquisitely dressed form excite His devotees' transcendental elation. Such fortunate souls, with full faith in the Lord's benevolent nature, reveal their minds to Him in confidence.

Advanced devotees can also hear the Deity speaking to them, at which time they lose all other consciousness and become absorbed in glorifying the Lord as if they had hundreds or thousands of tongues.

Submerged in the sankirtana of Krsna's names and pastimes, they taste bliss unimaginable to impersonalists. Such devotees achieve freedom from material entanglement without separate endeavour.

Once a young brahmana came to Vrndavana to ask the Deity Gopala to bear witness to a promise he had made earlier in the Lord's presence. The brahmana pressed Gopala, saying:

"My dear Sir! You are very merciful, and You know everything. Kindly be a witness in this case. A person who knows things as they are but does not bear witness becomes involved in sinful activities."

But Gopala was not inclined to leave His temple in Vrndavana. The Deity then told the brahmana to return to his village and assemble the elders together. "When they call," Gopala said, "I will appear."

The young brahmana rejected this solution and insisted that Gopala leave Vrndavana and come personally in His Deity form to testify by speaking the truth directly from His beautiful lotus lips.

Sri Gopala said, "I've never heard of a Deity walking from one place to another." "That may be true," the brahmana cleverly replied, "but then how is it that You, a Deity, are speaking to me?"

The Lord could not defeat the logic of His pure devotee, who was convinced that Gopala was not a statue but the cowherd prince of Vraja Himself. For the sake of His devotee, He could do as He liked.

Defeated by the strength of His servant's love for Him, Gopala left His altar and followed behind the brahmana, step after step, walking the long distance from Vrndavana to the town of Vidyanagara.

After testifying, and at the behest of His servants, Lord Krsna, now known as "the witness Gopala," remained in Vidyanagara for many years to proclaim to the world how merciful He is to His servants.

Just as the Deity is directly Krsna and can walk and talk, mantras that contain Krsna's names, as well as the names themselves, are also imbued in full with His personal potencies, being identical with Him.

**"The holy name of Krsna is like a touchstone. As Krsna personified in sound, His name is identical with Him in every way, transcendental and eternally free from material contamination."**

Provided that such pancaratrika-mantras are chanted with the proper devotional mood and informed transcendental awareness, they can reveal Sri Krsna and bestow on attentive devotees pure love for Him.

**\*\*\*\***

Having heard a description of the Brahma-gayatri mantra and an introduction to the pancaratrika-mantras, let us now hear about the mula- and gayatri mantra with which we glorify the spiritual master.

In the sequence of mantras that Vaisnavas chant three times daily, the Brahma-gayatri is the esoteric utterance of the Absolute Truth and the root from which grows the tree of the pancaratrika-mantras.

Transcendental knowledge first appears to conditioned souls in the form of the spiritual master, from whose lips that knowledge is first heard in the form of the guru's inspiring divine instructions.

Contemplation of the Supreme, then, begins by offering respects to the spiritual master and meditating upon the eight sublime truths connected to the spiritual master's service to Krsna.

Lord Caitanya is the great master of all gurus and the spiritual master of the three worlds (jagad-guru); as such, He is to be offered all respects and meditated upon as the combined form of Radha and Krsna.

The path leading to service to Radha and Krsna in Vraja is a slippery and winding one. It is very difficult to navigate even for great devotees, not to mention meditative yogis and introspective sages.

Still, "Things that are very difficult to do become easy if one somehow or other remembers Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. But if one docs not remember Him, even easy things become very difficult."

By Lord Caitanya's grace, we come to know and surrender to the Supreme Lord, Krsna, who is the all-attractive cowherd boy roaming along the Yamuna's shores with His blissful gopi girlfriends.

Through the pure sankirtana of Krsna's names, Lord Gauranga's grace reveals the rare treasure of divine amorous love, through which pure-hearted devotees join the pastimes of the lover of the gopis.

He is the ever-fresh Cupid who shoots arrows of amorous attraction at Vraja's passionate gopis from the bows of His transcendental limbs, which defy poetic description but demand to be embraced.

The Brahma-gayatri unfurls to reveal that guru and Gauranga arc guiding us to Krsna through the process of regulated devotional service, and at last to spontaneous amorous love like that of the gopis.

Knowledge of relationship, practice, and the ultimate goal are revealed by the diksa-mantras and spelled out in detail by the cream of the Vedas — Srila Prabhupada's books and their purports.

A devotee's spiritual life matures as the whorls of the lotus of remembrance — formed by each successive mantra — unfold one after another to reveal the central receptacle of amorous love for Krsna.

Whatever our permanent mood of ecstasy — be it friendship, parental, or amorous love — it exists within and serves Radha's and Krsna's amorous pastimes and is also nourished by thoughts of those pastimes.

May that Lord who at the beginning of creation inspired Brahma with full knowledge of His Supreme Self, and who appeared to me in the form of my spiritual master, be pleased with me.

May Lord Gopinatha, who appears in the form of His own devotee to taste Sri Radha's love for Him and who begs Her for that love, inspire me to describe the esoteric forms He takes in transcendental sound.

Early one morning in Goloka, Sri Radha gave an order to the cowherd boy Sarupa: "A brahmana from Mathura who is My devotee is coming to My grove. Go there alone, the first thing today.

"Enlighten him with good instructions, console him, and help him to quickly attain Krsna's grace." May soft-hearted Radha, who is always concerned for those who suffer from neglecting Hari, bless us.

May my spiritual master, Srila Prabhupada, the most learned of transcendentalists, the personification of Lord Caitanya's mercy, Goloka's emissary of Srimati Radharani, be pleased with us.

According to their karma, living entities are made to wander the universe, but only a very few fortunate souls receive the seed of the creeper of devotion by the mercy of both guru and Krsna.

My life began in the darkest of ignorance. Swept away by the strong waves of illusion with everyone else, I continually engaged in forbidden acts that, time after time, invariably ended in misery.

When that misery at last plumbed the depths of despair, somehow, perhaps as a result of some past pious deeds, I called out to the Lord for help, even though I had no idea who He was or how to find Him.

I recall sitting on a ridge off Mount Royal, overlooking the city of Montreal. As I watched the aimless bustle beneath me, I wondered whether anyone had actually heard my petition.

Little did I know that just a stone's throw away from where I sat, Lord Jagannatha, the merciful, all-cognisant master of the universe, was in the Hare Krsna temple on Pie IX Street. And He was listening.

The Lord had not only heard, but He also took pity upon me and, in a characteristically wonderful display of kindness, He would soon direct me to the lotus feet of my eternal spiritual master, Srila Prabhupada.

By Srila Prabhupada's grace I came into the company of devotees, heard Krsna's holy names and the teachings of sacred scriptures, and was engaged in service to Caitanya Mahaprabhu's mission.

And yet, in addition to these incomparably wonderful gifts, Srila Prabhupada, transcendental philanthropist that he is, further blessed me with service to Sri Krsna and His divine consort Sri Radha.

From my very first encounters with Krsna consciousness I used to gaze at a colourful painting of a youthful couple standing before an idyllic background and think, "It all looks so familiar."

Now, by Srila Prabhupada's grace, I know that young couple to be the dual forms of the Absolute Truth — Radha and Krsna — and that idyllic background to be Their eternal home, Goloka Vrndavana.

Now, by the mercy of my divine master, knowledge and practice of devotional service has led me to directly see and serve Radha and Syamasundara in the celestial setting of New Vraja-dhama.

Since time immemorial I had been inimical to Krsna, but by some unimaginable good fortune, Srila Prabhupada awakened me to the fact that I am, in truth, the eternal servant of Radha and Syama.

I acknowledge that many great gurus may walk the earth. But my spiritual master saved me. Therefore my primary duties are to offer him my respectful obeisance and to serve him without reservation.

Dear devotees! Just as Krsna ended my aimless wanderings in this material world by bringing me to the lotus feet of my Prabhupada, the Lord has no doubt brought the same good fortune to you.

Submissive faith in the spiritual master's instructions is the seed from which all aspects of one's relationship with Sri Guru grow. In the absence of that seed a connection has not yet been made.

Inspired by Lord Krsna from within his heart, the spiritual master guides the disciple to liberation with the beacon of transcendental knowledge through the phases of relationship, practice, and goal.

The guidance of the spiritual master docs not depend on his physical presence. In his absence, directly or indirectly, the guru continues to guide the faithful disciple. Somehow I perceived this truth.

In the spring following Srila Prabhupada's disappearance, I travelled to Vrndavana and, with a heavy heart, offered obeisances where a makeshift samadhi stood above his divine body. It was a difficult time.

One night while in Vrndavana I dreamt of Srila Prabhupada. We were walking around the temple accompanied only by the sound of singing birds in an atmosphere sweetened by our mutual affection.

Srila Prabhupada's bearing was regal, his stride powerful, and the tap of his cane on the ground punctuated his words. He spoke about Krsna and how I should not delay in going back to Godhead.

After walking for some time we came to a place where Srila Prabhupada sat down. I sat at his feet as he looked at me with ineffable kindness. As my already turbulent heart erupted, I wept.

Unable to express the unbearable sorrow I felt in Srila Prabhupada's physical absence, I could only cry. He knew the contents of my heart completely, and so he put my head in his lap and stroked it lovingly.

I wanted to say so many things, but tears of sorrow, like waves in the ocean, washed my words far away. Tenderly caressing my head, Srila Prabhupada told me, "Don't worry. Everything will be all right."

I awoke with those words resonating in my heart. As the years and decades pass, they have proved true, as Srila Prabhupada's words always have. I didn't have to worry. Everything was all right.

Through the media of his instructions, books, and followers, Srila Prabhupada continued to instruct me and guide me. And when needed, he would appear in my dreams and instruct me directly.

It is said that the truths regarding the guru are innumerable and inconceivable. No doubt it is so. In summary, I will simply say that Sri Guru saves us from the follies of ignorance and gives us Krsna.

**\*\*\*\***

Krsna's unrelenting desire and efforts to deliver conditioned souls — either personally, or through His expansions, potencies, and servants — is known as the principle of the spiritual master (guru-tattva).

The guru-tattva principle is embodied in devotees who, inspired by Krsna's compassion for the suffering conditioned souls, take up His mission to deliver them. Thus they arc known as spiritual masters.

Krsna and the spiritual master are one and equally worshipful in the sense that they share the same mission of delivering the fallen. As such, they represent the source and sum total of all the demigods.

The spiritual master appears in two different but identical manifestations of Krsna: the initiating spiritual master and the instructing spiritual master. These two differ only in their dealings.

As an expansion of Madana-mohana's form (krsna-rupa), the initiating spiritual master re-establishes the disciple's lost relationship with Krsna by initiating him into transcendental knowledge.

And as the expansion of Govindaji's personality (krsna-svarupa), the instructing spiritual master reveals the path by which the devotee can awaken love for the master of the gopis, Sri Gopinatha.

Regarding the siksa-guru, it is said, "One should know the instructing spiritual master to be the Personality of Krsna. Lord Krsna manifests Himself as the Supersoul and as the greatest devotee of the Lord."

Srila Prabhupada points out that the instructing spiritual master who constantly guides a disciple in his spiritual development generally becomes that disciple's initiating spiritual master later on.

Amongst the Lord's servants, the guru is one of three kinds: the devotee who is eternally liberated, the devotee who has achieved perfection through practice, and devotee who is still practising.

A disciple who accepts a practising devotee as guru should know that unless the spiritual master is very advanced, an uttama-adhikari, the guru's guidance will be somewhat lacking in direction.

The scriptures prescribe many spiritual disciplines to overcome the influence of the three modes of nature, but by simply serving a Vaisnava guru with devotion one achieves that same goal.

Faithful service to one's spiritual master pleases Krsna more than following other purificatory Vedic injunctions, such as penance, self-discipline, ritual worship, or brahminical initiation.

Therefore a sincere disciple is convinced that service to the spiritual master includes service to the Lord, that it is the path leading to transcendence, and that it is the best means to see Krsna face-to-face.

The Padma Purana says,

**"Devotion to my spiritual master is more important than devotion to Hari, for if I am devoted to my spiritual master, then Lord Hari will personally reveal Himself to me."**

Just as the Lord is present in mantras that glorify Him, the spiritual master is present in the words of the Guru mula-mantra and Guru- gayatri, and in the meditation upon the guru prescribed by them.

The same divine grace that inspires the Vaisnava guru to instruct a devotee and to accept him as a disciple is also present in the mantras given at initiation. Thus the guru and the mantras he gives are one.

The spiritual master distributes Krsna's grace in the form of transcendental mantras that reveal the guru's identity along with the truth about the Lord and the means to attain eternal service to Him.

The Brahma-gayatri is an esoteric call to the service of Radha and Krsna in Goloka. That the next mantras glorify Sri Guru indicates that Their Lordships call us through the spiritual master.

Thus continues the eternal, transcendental dynamic: guru initiates one with the Brahma-gayatri (Krsna), who directs one to worship the Guru mula-mantra, which leads to further meditation on Krsna.

Aim, the seed of the Guru mula-mantra, expands from omkara. It is also called the vagbhava-bija, in that it represents the nature (bhava) of speech (vac), which is to transmit transcendental knowledge.

Aim is also known as the seed of knowledge (cid-bija), the syllable of the goddess of learning, Sarasvati. And since the goal of knowledge is love for Krsna, ultimately aim directs one to Srimati Radharani.

The guru-bija (aim) not only invokes knowledge but also reveals the medium through which transcendental knowledge appears — the guru — who directs us to the loving devotional service of Sri Krsna.

However, in addition to being the seed, revered origin, and ultimate purpose of transcendental knowledge, aim is also — in an even more fundamental sense — the seed of devotional service.

Just as om represents the Absolute Truth, aim symbolises the soul's natural propensity to serve, conveyed in its root form (mula) as the offering of obeisance to the spiritual master, *aim gurave namah*.

Thus devotional service and transcendental knowledge are intimately related as cause and effect. How, then, can the cid-bija do anything but direct us to serve Krsna through service to the spiritual master?

As Suta Gosvami said,

**"By rendering devotional service unto the Personality of Godhead, Sri Krsna, one immediately acquires causeless knowledge and detachment from the world."**

In the quest for the Absolute Truth, we begin by offering obeisance to the spiritual master with the words aim gurave namah, following which we should devote our lives to serving the order of the guru.

Later in this chapter we will see how Guru-gayatri encourages us to know our spiritual master, as we should, by serving his instructions. In this way, all truths regarding the spiritual master are revealed.

The best service to the spiritual master is to become a spiritual master oneself and inspire our disciples to do likewise in turn. That is Caitanya Mahaprabhu's legacy to His followers, *amara ajnaya guru*.

Initially, the spiritual master should be seen as the representative of Nityananda, and when a disciple becomes advanced, he will recognise his gurus identity, which may be that of a maidservant of Radha.

Srila Prabhupada says that the spiritual master may represent either Sri Radha or Lord Nityananda. To the sincere disciple, the inner identity of his guru is revealed through service and surrender.

And so as we worship the spiritual master with the Guru mula-mantra, we should bear in mind that he is both transcendental knowledge personified and an eternal associate of Radha and Krsna.

We have received such invaluable gifts from the spiritual master that we must — out of love or duty — remember him, worship him, and offer him respectful obeisances, lest we risk being condemned.

Furthermore, the word namah ("the offering of respects") means more than merely speaking pleasant words, showering flower petals, or even worshipping the form of the guru with sixteen items.

To worship Sri Guru means to worship the entire disciplic succession, current representatives as well, by practical actions — small or great — furthering their mission to bestow mercy on the fallen (jiva-doya).

At an advanced age Srila Prabhupada sailed to America, alone and penniless, determined to convince hedonists to give up their ways and worship Krsna. That effort alone was namah to his spiritual master.

Namah also implies strictly following one's vows of initiation, renouncing improper behaviour, carrying out worldly duties in relation to Krsna, and chanting Hare Krsna without offence.

In other words, the songs of praise, flower offerings, and the elaborate worship given to the spiritual master are only perfect when we conduct our lives according to the guru's will and teachings.

The guru is properly worshipped by treating him as one would treat Krsna, as the representative of all the demigods. He should never be envied, disrespected, or considered to be merely an ordinary man.

Our acaryas prescribe that the guru be remembered at least thrice daily, but to advance nicely we should unite our hearts with the words of the guru and make every endeavour an offering to him.

By asking us to offer Him whatever we do, eat, offer, or give away in charity, Lord Krsna implies that we should offer Him these things through the spiritual master — *aim gurave namah*.

I offer my respectful obeisances to my spiritual master, who is seated at the lotus feet of the Panca-tattva. He is an instrument of their mercy and is absorbed in thoughts of service to Radha and Krsna.

**\*\*\*\***

Simply by remembering the Lord's associates — the spiritual masters — we are freed from material entanglement, assured of the fulfilment of all our desires, and awarded shelter at the Lord's lotus feet.

The Guru-gayatri is a song that glorifies the spiritual master for freeing us from suffering. It has four distinct parts: *aim, gurudevaya vidmahe, krsnanandaya dhimahi, and tan no guruh pracodayat*.

As already mentioned, aim is the seed (bija) that identifies the spiritual master as the source of transcendental knowledge that leads to the fullest expression of divine love, as personified in Sri Radha.

The phrase *gurudevaya vidmahe* is the portion of the mantra that deals with relationship. It indicates that the guru, who delivers his disciples with transcendental knowledge, is to be known.

"He who is an ocean of mercy, who is fulfilled in all respects, who has all good qualities, who works for the benefit of all souls, who is free from lust, who is perfect in all respects —

"Who is well-versed in the scriptures, who knows the science of Krsna, who can remove all the doubts of his disciples, and who is always alert in the service of Krsna is known as a genuine guru."

Sastra says, *gopayed gurum atmanah*: "One should hide one's guru," meaning that disciples should refrain from flaunting their spiritual masters out of false prestige. Still, I am proud of Srila Prabhupada.

But my pride is not false. His Divine Grace was truly extraordinary. By establishing ISKCON and writing his Bhaktivedanta purports, he secured the path of salvation for generations the world over.

Srila Prabhupada's words are so empowered that even common men unfamiliar with Vedic culture can easily understand the topmost conclusions of the Vedas. Others can but walk in his shadow.

Srila Rupa Gosvami established in this world the mission to fulfil the desire of Lord Caitanya, and Srila Prabhupada set up an international society, ISKCON, as an instrument to serve that mission.

Thus Srila Prabhupada's followers pray,

**"O ocean of mercy! O friend of the poor! O lord and master of the devotees! Give me the shade of your lotus feet. Your fame is spread all over the three worlds !"**

From the moment I came in touch with Krsna consciousness, I understood that Srila Prabhupada was both a spiritual teacher and the transcendental general of Lord Caitanya's sankirtana army.

I was not alone. My peers also understood that Krsna consciousness meant executing devotional practices alongside vigorous preaching, and we could all see that Srila Prabhupada was the exemplar of both.

The word vidmahe is a call to understand the relationship between disciple and guru as that of servant and master, though eternally fixed. In Sri Prahlada's words, "We have no other relationship."

In the Guru-gayatri, the most significant word is krsnanandaya. Its interpretation, like that of savitur in the Brahma-gayatri, determines how one meditates on the mantra as a whole, and thus on the guru.

The word krsnanandaya has three basic meanings. One portrays the guru's external identity, which we see in his activities. The other two reveal his confidential identity, which he conceals in his heart.

In the first meaning, the spiritual master is called krsnanandaya because he gives Krsna pleasure by being responsible for guiding disciples along the path chalked out by Krsna in the Bhagavad-gita.

In the Bhagavad-gita, referring to one who explains His teachings to the devotees, Krsna proclaims,

**"There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear."**

Moreover, the spiritual master delivers Krsna's mercy, spreads Mahaprabhu's sankirtana movement, trains disciples to worship the Deity of the Lord, and persuades them to eat only krsna-prasada.

By such acts of devotion the spiritual master becomes Krsna's dear-most servant; indeed, their relationship is so intimate that scripture enjoins that we honour the guru just as we honour Krsna.

And, of course, by pleasing Krsna, the spiritual master automatically enjoys bliss himself (krsnanandaya). From within the heart of such an intimate devotee, Krsna gives all enlightenment and true pleasure.

The second meaning of krsnanandaya is attributed to that spiritual master whose heart is overflowing with the bliss of love for Krsna as a result of his having achieved maturity in Krsna consciousness.

This meaning of the word krsnanandaya might lend itself to esoteric interpretations of the guru's eternal identity, but guru and disciple always remain master and servant, albeit in different forms.

Attempts to understand the spiritual master, however, should always be guided by scripture, which declares that the guru's eternal identity is revealed in proportion to the disciple's surrender and service.

To discourage neophyte devotees from trying to ascertain the eternal identities of their gurus, Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura modified the traditional Guru mula-mantra and Guru-gayatri.

The change directly emphasised the spiritual master's identity as the embodiment of realised knowledge and indirectly revealed service to Krsna as the means for realising further knowledge of the guru.

Since the spiritual master can only be known by his mercy, artificial attempts to unveil his eternal identity will always be futile. Therefore, vidmahe also invites us to know what the guru reveals of himself.

When asked about his own spiritual identity, Srila Prabhupada would usually remain silent. Once he replied, "That you don't need," hinting that the guru can be known according to the disciple's qualification.

In trying to understand the spiritual master, our first duty is to try to understand what he wants. As Srila Prabhupada says,

**"You must know the mind of the person... you want to serve."**

In addition to one's initiating and instructing spiritual masters, devotees should also know He who resides in the heart, measuring only eight inches, with four hands, each holding different objects.

And since gurudeva also refers to the instructing spiritual master, for members of ISKCON vidmahe says, "You should know the teachings, persona, and mission of the founder-acarya, Srila Prabhupada."

The spiritual master's great happiness (krsnanandaya) stems from his mind and senses being empowered by transcendental loving devotion to constantly perceive Krsna's pastimes within his heart.

And while outwardly conducting themselves as intermediate devotees, such elevated spiritual masters are sometimes overwhelmed by bliss and laugh or cry loudly, or sing and dance like madmen.

Spiritual masters revelling in krsnanandaya realise Krsna within everything and everything within Krsna, but they also distinguish between the Lord, His devotees, the innocent, and the envious.

The third meaning of krsnanandaya identifies the spiritual master as the representative of Krsna and His potency of pleasure (ananda), Srimati Radharani, or of Their combined form, Lord Caitanya.

Of perfected spiritual masters, most are situated in their eternal identities as Radha's and Krsna's maidservants. And they guide their disciples to the divine couple from that realised platform.

Perfected devotees who have realised a mood other than amorous love, like that of friendship, are also considered associates of Radha and Krsna, for all mellows in Vraja serve the amorous mellow.

These spiritual masters are also krsnanandaya because they are empowered by Sri Radha to initiate disciples with mantras and knowledge imbued with the moods and aim of amorous love.

One example of a guru in the mood of a cowherd boy being krsnanandaya is Gopa-kumara, who was personally requested by Sri Radha to bring the brahmana from Pragjyotisa-pura back to Godhead.

Practising devotees who act in the role of spiritual masters also direct their disciples towards the divine couple, but they do so as learned guides who themselves aspire for the same goal they impart to others.

Krsnanandaya, when applied to spiritual masters in the mellow of amorous love, also indicates constant absorption in hearing, chanting, and remembering Radha's and Krsna's names, forms, and pastimes.

Within himself, in the identity of a maidservant, he makes arrangements that contribute to the perfection of Radha's and Krsna's amorous pastimes within the groves of eternal Vrndavana.

In this world, service to the divine couple also takes the form of spreading Krsna consciousness, which is a fountainhead of bliss to Radha and Krsna, and to the spiritual master himself.

**"O Gurudeva! Intensely devoted to Radha, you reside with Her girlfriends and are thus always in Her presence. I continually offer my obeisances unto you, the abode of loving devotion to Krsna."**

And of course a guru situated in amorous love is able — by the all-inclusive nature of his continuous ecstasy —to bestow upon his disciples the taste and perfection of all other transcendental mellows.

Having accepted the spiritual master as the representative of Radha and Krsna, krsnanandaya can also refer to the representative of Lord Caitanya, who is, after all, the combined form of Radha and Krsna.

Thus the sweetness of Vraja's amorous mellow — difficult to taste directly — is readily savoured by Sri Guru and his followers through the munificence of Lord Caitanya's sankirtana movement.

Krsnanandaya, then, describes a guru who, by his dedicated service to Lord Caitanya, brings such unending pleasure to Radha and Krsna that They readily bestow upon him the bliss of his coveted mellow.

**\*\*\*\***

The word *dhimahi*, common to all gayatri mantras, calls upon the worshipper to engage in meditation (dhyana) — in this case upon the word *krsnanandaya* — as a means of devotional cultivation.

As already mentioned, curiosity regarding the spiritual master's inner identity may be natural, but a disciple's spiritual advancement comes from meditating instead on how to fulfil Srila Gurudeva's order.

Srila Prabhupada was profoundly inspired by Cakravarti Thakura's commentary on a particular Bhagavad-gita verse: "The disciple should consider the order of the spiritual master to be his life and soul."

This kind of total, unceasing absorption in the teachings of the spiritual master moulds one's life into a pilgrim's journey. It is a life of reflection (manana) and the fullest meaning of the word dhimahi.

To aid us in this introspective life, Srila Prabhupada wrote books to serve as road maps for our journey, and he expected us to read them. Indeed, he said, "If you want to know me, read my books."

Thus the word dhimahi tells the devotee to be absorbed in remembrance (smaranam) while chanting gayatri, indeed, at all times. In other words, one should never forget Sri Guru.

How can we neglect Krsna's order to serve the guru after having received so many transcendental gifts from him? Is it honest to accept something of such great value without giving something in return?

As Prahlada Maharaja says about Narada Muni,

**"Your servant kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him."**

A disciple's most important service is to become fully Krsna conscious — to perfectly apply the teachings of the guru and the scriptures, showing by example that those teachings yield fruit.

By worshipping the Deities of Radha and Krsna in regulated devotion with the mantras received at initiation, we gradually develop a spontaneous attraction for Krsna and living in Vrndavana.

By hearing of Krsna's pastimes with the gopis, our spontaneous attraction becomes further developed. From attraction, amorous love fully matures, which enables us to serve Krsna in the form of a gopi.

As Srila Prabhupada's disciples and followers, we should give spiritual knowledge by publishing and selling books, chanting Hare Krsna in public, opening temples, and establishing rural communities.

By fixed determination to become Krsna conscious and to give Krsna consciousness to others, we finally enter that realm of bliss in which Gurudeva eternally serves Radha and Krsna. That is dhimahi.

Thus, attentive selfless service to the spiritual master is the perfection of meditation and the source of all good fortune, as the Lord Himself showed by example while living with His guru, Sandipani Muni.

In His youth Krsna lived at the home of His spiritual master. Once the guru's wife asked Krsna and one of His fellow students, the brahmana Sudama, to collect firewood from the forest nearby.

While they were out, a storm suddenly engulfed the forest. Unable to find their way back, the two boys passed the night in distress until their guru, along with the other students, came to their rescue.

Elated, Sandipani Muni said,

**"My dear boys! I am glad to see that you are willing to undergo any kind of trouble for the satisfaction of your spiritual master. That is the way to liquidate your debt to him.**

**"The disciple's duty is to dedicate his life to the service of the spiritual master. Now I bless you. May all your desires be fulfilled and may whatever you have learned from me always remain with you."**

The final portion of the Guru-gayatri tells of the goal of meditation and pleads for the blessings of pure love and confidential service, gifts exclusively within the realm of the spiritual master's good will.

The phrase *tan no guruh pracodayat* ("May he enthuse us as he is enthused.") is a petition to the spiritual master that the source of his inspiration be the source of our inspiration, and our service as well.

In other words, "May Gurudeva inspire us to follow his example and make Radha and Krsna the objects of our loving devotion. May he accept us as his eternal servants and share with us the bliss he feels."

Loving devotional service is Sri Guru's very life. Service, then, is the basis of our relationship and the goal to which he guides us. Moreover, we know him through our service to him.

Although this world is "no place for a gentleman," while living here we whole-heartedly serve Lord Caitanya's mission. And when the time is right, we may continue to serve Sri Guru in Goloka.

The goal of the Guru-gayatri — the goal to which Sri Guru directs the disciple — has been perfectly summarised by Srila Prabhupada in his "motto for human life," to go back home, back to Godhead.

The word tat ("that") refers equally to the words krsnanandaya and dhimahi as divine attainments to which the spiritual master, by his own example, incites the sincere, determined disciple.

By the grace of Krsna, such disciples aspire to achieve the goal of meditating on the combined meanings of krsnanandaya given earlier, a goal that has two facets — tasting and distributing bliss.

Tasting the mellow of ecstatic bliss (rasa-asvadana) and rendering loving service to Krsna are the spontaneous outcomes of engaging in our eternal transcendental relationship with the Supreme Lord.

In this regard Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami says, "O queen of Vrndavana! Ever since Rupa-manjari filled my eyes with light in the land of Vraja, I have yearned to see the red lac on Your lotus feet."

Distributing the bliss of love of God (prema-dana) is the mission of the Panca-tattva's followers, who deliver that bliss while tasting it themselves through the agency of the sankirtana movement.

A devotee does not mind where he takes birth — in Goloka Vrndavana or in hell — his only desire is to be amongst Vaisnavas, whose company provides constant krsnanandaya.

The spiritual master's heart is melted together with the hearts of Radha and Krsna in a pool of bliss. And when a drop of that krsnanandaya lands on the disciple he desires much more of the same.

When the word tat refers to dhimahi as the cause of the disciple's spiritual enthusiasm, it implies a special meditation (dhyana) on the responsibilities and identity of the spiritual master.

If we are receptive to the spiritual master's efforts to awaken within our hearts transcendental knowledge and loving devotion, we will surely feel the shelter of these divine potencies.

Narottama Dasa Thakura reveals his meditation on his spiritual master in the following way:

**"He opens my darkened eyes and fills my heart with transcendental knowledge (divya-jnana).**

**"My spiritual master is my Lord birth after birth. From him loving devotion (prema-bhakti) emanates, by him ignorance is destroyed. The Vedic scriptures sing of his character."**

Contemplating spiritual truths in tandem with offenceless devotional practice awakens spiritual emotion, which in turn dissipates ignorance about one's own identity and the identity of Sri Guru.

Contemplation on the eternal identity of the spiritual master, then, is a natural consequence of deep reflection on and strict adherence to Gurudeva's mission, teachings, and instructions (vani-seva).

Such realised meditation invokes genuine enthusiasm, because it satisfies the self (yenatma suprasidati), whereas artificial speculation on the spiritual master's eternal svarupa leads to nothing substantial.

Another reading of tat refers to the devotional mood of the spiritual master, a mood he imparts to the disciple through the mantras he gives at initiation, and which flowers with attentive chanting.

Because of the constitutional nature of one's permanent mellow of devotion (sthayi-bhava), its effect is to inspire one in the loving service of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu and the divine couple.

Waves of devotional mellows flow from the spiritual master — who is like a great aquatic playing within the waters of Radha's and Krsna's loving pastimes — and lap upon the shore of the disciple's heart.

Narottama Dasa Thakura prays,

**"O my master! Please place me under the shade of your lotus feet. If I have pleased you at all with my devotional service, then kindly cast your merciful glance upon me.**

**"If you are satisfied with me, the desires of my mind will be fulfilled. I will attain the company of Lord Caitanya, and with my present eyes I will be able to see the transcendental forms of the divine couple.**

**"Without your mercy I may spend my days and nights chanting the names of Radha and Krsna and hearing Srimad-Bhagavatam, but I will never realise my desire for Their personal company.**

**"O Gurudeva! Bless me that Radha's and Krsna's lotus feet will always be impressed within my mind and that I may have your company when you manifest your form as a maidservant of the divine couple.**

**"O my master! When, by your mercy, will I be able to serve the foremost gopi, Srimati Radharani, and submerge in the bliss emanating from the divine couple's lotus feet?**

**"At that time I will string garlands of forest flowers. Approaching Radha and Krsna, I will place the garlands around Their necks, and anoint Their limbs with fragrant substances like aguru.**

**"I will follow the gopis' orders and offer the divine couple betel nuts, draw artistic tilaka patterns on Their foreheads in red sindura, and fan Them with a camara whisk as They sit on a golden throne.**

**"O my divine master! If at some time you are merciful to me, then these desires of my heart will be fulfilled, and I will be able to directly see the transcendental sweetness of the divine couple."**

Finally, with the words tan no guruh pracodayat, we meditate upon the spiritual master as the source of enthusiasm, empowering us no engage everything in Krsna's service (yukta-vairagya).

**\*\*\*\***

To share Srila Prabhupada's enthusiasm to serve Sri Krsna, in the same way he does and with the same ecstasy — both in this world and in Goloka — is my ambition and the source of my enthusiasm.

ISKCON embodies Srila Prabhupada's enthusiasm to serve Krsna in this world, and my constant meditation is how to serve his movement, which is a display of His Divine Grace's love for his Lord.

Srila Prabhupada said we would have an ISKCON in the spiritual world. I take that to mean we will continue to be part of his circle of associates, helping him serve Radha and Krsna in Goloka.

When I chant the Guru-gayatri I meditate upon that divinely empowered person whom Krsna sent to this world to write books. His Divine Grace told my friend Tamala Krsna Goswami:

"In Goloka, Krsna told me, 'You go down there and preach.' I replied to the Lord, 'It's such a nasty place. I don't want to go. Please let me stay here.' But Krsna persisted and said, 'Don't worry about anything.
