‘“I will make every arrangement for your comfort. In the meantime, write your books, and when you have finished, then you can return.’” Srila Prabhupada concluded, ‘“Then I said, ‘All right.’”

Although I was born in a part of the world that Vaisnavas did not frequent, in time, by the arrangement of Divine Providence, I ended up living just down the street from Srila Prabhupada in Montreal.'

And as the bliss and compassion radiating from Srila Prabhupada coalesced into a society of devotees who took it upon themselves to distribute his divine mercy, some of that mercy fell upon me.

One way or another, Srila Prabhupada dragged me out of the well of materialistic household life and inspired me to offer myself in his service. It was the best thing I ever did. His Divine Grace saved me!

As decades roll by I try to understand him hut remain like a dwarf who tries again and again to capture the moon. But I must try because I owe everything to him, and I want to please him because I love him.

I know that it was he who fulfilled Lord’s Caitanya’s prophesy that Sri Krsna’s name would be spread around the world, and it was he who disclosed Vyasadeva’s eternal truths to present-day human society.

Defying old age he continually travelled the globe to glorify the one personal God, and to enthuse his disciples to distribute his books and live their lives like Vraja-vasis — dependent on land, cows, and Krsna.

He said that if we knew his eternal identity we would faint. True to Vaisnava etiquette he kept his ecstasy hidden, but when on occasion he could no longer control it, he inundated us with krsnananda.

At times, whether seeing him from a distance or sitting beside him, I could feel the unmistakable power of the divine bliss inside of him that some devotees also saw as an effulgence radiating from his body.

We all thought he was indispensable to our lives and to spreading Krsna consciousness, yet by teaching us how to die — his final lesson— he showed us that we would have to learn to live by his vani.

Srila Prabhupada disappeared from material vision but continued to guide us from within our hearts through his instructions, his books, his followers, his deity form, and through our memories of him.

When Lord Caitanya was planning to leave Nadiya and take sannyasa, He assured the devotees that he would appear in two different ways so they could always be with Him — in kirtana and His deity form.

I am convinced that Srila Prabhupada has also invested a special potency of his presence in nama-sankirtana as well as in his deity form, and in recollections of him, by his disciples especially.

A few years after his disappearance I concluded that the very special bonding that occurs amongst devotees when speaking and hearing about Srila Prabhupada is actually the presence of His Divine Grace.

I joined ISKCON with a desire to take sannyasa. When that desire neared fruition, Prabhupada came to me in a dream. I asked him, “Srila Prabhupada! How can one become free from sex desire?”

He held his bead bag in front of my face and said, “Harer nama, barer nama, barer namaiva kevalam.” Then looking me in the eyes, he added, “There is no other way! No other way! No other way!”

In ways like this Srila Prabhupada continued to guide me, from Kisora-Kisori’s service to Harmony and then across the ocean, whose storms were mild by comparison to ISKCON on UK’s turbulence.

In another dream we were walking together in what appeared to be a cow pasture of Vraja when Srila Prabhupada stopped and, with his left hand, opened the sky like a curtain to reveal the world of men.

Below, in a dingy valley, the atmosphere thick with the smoke of loneliness and misery, I saw people toiling in dirty factories, scurrying nowhere in noisy cars, and fighting over trifles to no avail.

Turning towards me, tears falling down his cheeks, Srila Prabhupada said, “See how much they are suffering. Help them!” Shortly after that dream, perhaps in fulfilment of its order, I came to Hungary.

At the same time Srila Prabhupada was engaging me in these services, he also nurtured my devotional creeper with showers of the same bliss that sustained him and with which he had spread his movement.

As I went about distributing his books, preaching, worshipping Sri Sri Gaura-Nitai’s Deity forms, and organising festival tours, he showered me with a bliss that served only to increase my desire for it.

Then two moons rose over Hungary — one black, one golden. What was once a shower became a downpour of krsnananda, increasing my taste for the holy names and personal service to Radha and Syama.

By hearing Krsna’s Vrndavana pastimes, a flower of attachment to Radha and Syama began to blossom in my heart along with an intoxicating desire to serve Them as the gopis do.

Speaking about His attachment to the Vraja-vasis, Sri Krsna says: “All the inhabitants of Vrndavana-dhama—My mother, father, cowherd boyfriends, and everything else — are like My life and soul.

“And amongst all the inhabitants of Vrndavana, the gopis are My very life and soul. And amongst the gopis, You, Srimati Radharani, are the chief. Therefore You are the very life of My life.”

Commenting on these statements, Srila Prabhupada explains that because Radha enchants Krsna by Her qualities, She is actually Vraja’s queen and Krsna is Her “instrument” and “decoration.”

This particular mood of krsnananda expressed by Srila Prabhupada inspired me to conclude that the best way to serve Krsna is to serve Srimati Radharani. As Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami prays:

**“O goddess! I shall never ask You for anything other than service to Your lotus feet. I repeatedly offer my respectful obeisances to Your friendship, but my only aspiration is to become Your maidservant.”**

Although I am stuck in the mire of wicked desires, and although I pursue a goal rarely won even by great souls, I know that my spiritual master can make anything possible, even service to Sri Radha.

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto the saviour of the fallen, my spiritual master, \[Srila Prabhupada\], whose mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

I therefore meditate upon my spiritual master, praying that he reveals to me the full nature of my own eternal bliss, which like dry tinder has been ignited by a spark of krsnananda from his glance.

About his mercy I could write reams! I had possessed only oceans of suffering and could but hope for more of the same. In that dark hour he came to me with joined palms and implored me to give up my pain.

In place of suffering he gave me sublime truth in several forms: Vedic knowledge, the holy names, Gauranga, Radha-Syamasundara, and krsnananda — truth’s very own essential blissful nature.

I had nothing. He gave me everything. My spiritual master saved me. And all that he asked in return was for me to be happy. I therefore offer my obeisances unto him — aim gurave namah!

Therefore, thrice a day I pray with all my heart, “May I understand my spiritual master, who is always in blissful Krsna consciousness, and may I mediate on him being enthused as he enthuses me.”’

## Chapter 33 - Gaura Mula-mantra Gaura-gayatri

Sri Gaurasundara! O vast ocean of immortal love for Sri Krsna’s lotus feet, I offer my respectful obeisances unto You. Do You sometimes remember me, the most wretched of all wretched souls?

To worship Lord Caitanya, allow me to explain the Gaura mula-mantra (*klim gauraya namah*) and the Gaura-gayatri (*klim caitanyaya vidmahe visvambharaya dhimahi tan no gaurah pracodayat*).

I pray that both Lord Nityananda and Srila Prabhupada will empower me to worship Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu by praising the mantras Gaudiya Vaisnava brahmanas chant to remember and adore Him.

Having meditated upon my spiritual master, I now repose my mind and words on the original guru, Sri Nityananda Prabhu. Remembering Him destroys all obstacles and diffuses supreme bliss.

Has there ever been a transcendentalist more sublime than Sri Nityananda Rama? In the dress of an avadhuta, He freely distributes the names of Gaura and Krsna as He laughs, dances, and weeps.

Overcome with ecstatic love for Gauranga, Nityananda staggers as He walks about, raises His arms to the sky, soaks the ground with His tears, and rattles heaven and earth with loud shouts of “Hari! Hari!”

A gunja-berry crown sits atop His curly hair, which sways to and fro about His broad forehead marked with tilaka. Blue garments hang in folds from His lion-thin waist, gold anklets jingle on His reddish feet.

Staff in hand, graceful arms adorned with golden ornaments, cheeks abloom with a childlike smile, He blesses all with a sweet voice made sweeter still by the broken words of intoxicating divine love.

Inebriated with premia, His reddened eyes roll as He cries out, “O friend, Abhirama!” Sometimes He frantically searches in all directions, bellowing, “O My brother, Krsna! Where are You?”

Seeing the conditioned souls crushed in the serpentine coils of Kali-yuga, Nitai, filled with compassion, induces them to chant, “Hari! Hari!” thereby bestowing upon them sublime loving devotion.

All glories to the deliverer of the worlds, the cause of all causes, the source of all bliss, the eternal Supreme Being, who in ancient times was called Balarama, but who now also bears the name Nityananda.

Let me now pay homage to my Deities, Dayala-Nitai-Gaura-Hari, who in my hour of need preserved my enthusiasm, nurtured my devotion, and eventually turned me over to Radha-Syamasundara.

While felling trees with only three other devotees on the vast acreage of Harmony, I felt lonely, even in spiritual life. At that time I humbly requested Gaura and Nitai to allow me to serve Their Deity forms.

Two years later I relocated to the UK. It was then that Their Lordships’ beautiful forms appeared through the agency of my friend in Calcutta, thus opening a sublime new chapter in my life’s journey.

For twenty-five years I made offerings to Them, bathed and dressed Them, decorated Them with gems, gold, and silks. They became my devotional cynosure in a life filled with service, sadhana, and prayers.

I lived with Them in motor homes and carried Them in a box as we travelled on planes together. Fellow passengers often watched in disbelief as I offered Them full worship in airport departure lounges.

As They travelled around the UK or Hungary, riding on palanquins during hari-nama or Ratha-yatra, They were my masters, friends, guardians, wards, and of course, the alluring objects of my worship.

Uncountable times I opened Their travel case at a customs desk or while going through airport security and witnessed Their disarmingly sweet faces transform grumpy officials into humble enthusiasts.

The governmental restrictions on preaching in Hungary were lifted in 1989. That winter we held three days of lectures and kirtanas on a university campus, with a festival finale in the main auditorium.

As Dayala-Nitai-Gaura-Hari waited for me on Their altar in the motor home, I gave a talk in the lecture hall and then led a long kirtana in which the students danced wildly in the aisles.

The entire university may have heard that kirtana. No doubt, the students and faculty who didn’t attend heard about it from those who did. Due to that one festival, six students became full-time devotees.

When I returned to the motor home I was amazed! Gaura was standing on the extreme front end of the altar and Nitai was at the rear. When I left They had been side by side at the centre.

How was this possible? I decided that there could be no other explanation than that They too had been dancing in the kirtana. That incident was one of many that attested to Their playful nature.

Once I dreamt that I was at a public festival at which devotees were engaged in various services. Some were distributing prasada from a long table to a queue of guests that stretched off into the distance.

As I passed by, a devotee with long hair looked up from His service, raised His arms and, obviously intending to attract my attention, playfully called out to me with a remarkably sweet “Hare Krsna!”

Curious to know who this long-haired devotee was, I took a closer look only to realise that He was my Lord Nityananda — serving prasada. He winked at me and disappeared, and then I woke up.

By Their Lordships’ mercy my taste for preaching increased, as did my patience to take care of newcomers. Emboldened to ply uncharted seas, I could better understand the meaning of unlimited kindness.

Then Dayala-Nitai-Gaura-Hari mercifully arranged for Radha-Damodara to accept my worship, which eventually, and sadly, led to my entrusting Them to a worthy householder couple.

Now that I use every spare moment to write, I am unable to continue serving two sets of Deities. I am certain that Their Lordships’ desire was for me to direct my devotion to Radha-Damodara.

Following that order, I worship Gaura-Nitai in my mind, ever grateful to those two sankirtana generals who launched preaching in Hungary and who awakened me to the service of Radha and Krsna in Vraja.

**\*\*\*\***

In the appendix entitled “The Glories of Caitanya Mahaprabhu” I pay tribute to the Lord, His associates, and mission. Scriptural statements in support of that glorification now follow.

Despite scriptural evidence that supports Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s ontological identity as the Supreme Lord Sri Krsna, certain Vedic scholars and so-called learned Vaisnavas reject Caitanya’s divinity.

That scriptural evidence includes statements from sruti texts like the Svetasvatara Upanisad, smrti texts like the Vayu Purana, tantric texts like Krsna-yamala, and the emperor of all texts, Srimad-Bhagavatam.

The Ananta-samhita of Narada-pancaratra details the appearance of eternal Navadvipa in this world. It also beautifully summarises — in a single verse — all the evidence mentioned in the preceding stanza:

**“The Supreme Person, Sri Krsna Himself, who is the life of Srimati Radharani and is the Lord of the universe in creation, maintenance, and annihilation, appears as Gaura, o Mahesvari.”**

Of all types of scriptural evidence, sruti is universally accepted as most authoritative. What follows is a summary of the Atharva Vedas Caitanya Upanisad, which was discovered by Bhaktivinoda Thakura.

Once the sage Pippalada enquired from his father, Lord Brahma, “Please tell me what real auspiciousness is?” Brahma replied, “Return after a full year of purification by celibacy, yoga, and austerity.”

Pippalada came back a year later and asked two questions, “O Lord! The people of Kali-yuga are always sinful. Which Deity and which mantra should they worship to be liberated from this condition?”

At this, Brahma replied, “Listen, my son, as I disclose a confidential truth. That transcendental person who at times appears with a red, black, or white complexion will appear in Kali-yuga in a golden form.

“That one Supreme Personality of Godhead, the all-pervading Supersoul, Govinda, appears as Gaura along with His eternal abode Goloka, which appears as Navadvipa on the river Jahnavi.

“As a great saint and powerful mystic beyond the three modes of nature, the very emblem of divine acts, and the perfect devotee, He will teach the path of pure devotion throughout the world.

“My dear son,” Brahma continued. “Because He showers His unlimited mercy on my sampradaya, I offer my respectful obeisances unto Him, who can be known by studying the Vedanta aphorisms.

“Let us offer our obeisances unto that oldest of persons, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, who is also the all-cognisant, ever-youthful Krsna, knowing whom one overcomes illusion and death.”

Having answered Pippalada’s first question, Brahma paused for a moment to reflect before addressing his son’s second question: Which mantra can liberate the unfortunate souls of the Age of Kali?

“The original mantra for deliverance is comprised of Krsna’s holy names and imbued with the Lord’s energies of bliss (hladini), which pleases everyone, and knowledge (samvit), which destroys irreligion.

“Those names are Hari, Krsna, and Rama. Hari removes the knots of material desire from the heart, Krsna attracts everyone, and Rama gives pleasure to all living beings through transcendental bliss.

“These three names appear in the following arrangement of thirty- two supremely attractive syllables: Hare Krsna, Hare Krsna, Krsna Krsna, Hare Hare / Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare.

“This mantra is transcendental and confidential. It is understood only through pure devotion, and in Kali-yuga one can conquer the illusory energy by constantly chanting it in japa. There is no other way.”

In conclusion, Brahma encouraged Pippalada to overcome all lamentation by serving Lord Caitanya in love, by worshipping Him, meditating on Him, and by philosophically understanding Him.

Two basic truths underlie Lord Gauranga’s appearance. Externally He came to spread love of God by propagating the chanting of the maha-mantra, and internally He came to relish Radha’s love for Krsna.

It behoves us to keep these two truths in mind as we meditate on and offer prayers to Caitanya Mahaprabhu; indeed, these truths help us to appreciate the mantras that represent the enigmatic Sri Caitanya.

Knowledge of mantras and of the process of chanting is a great science, the essence of which we must know in order to reap the full benefit of devotional practices, in as short a time as possible.

As Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami points out in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, even if one chants the maha-mantra for many births, the result — love for Krsna — will not come if one chants with offence.

Since time immemorial, certain combinations of letters in the Sanskrit alphabet have represented certain deities. These combinations form primordial sounds called seed mantras (bijas).

These bija-mantras not only represent specific Deities but also contain the Deities’ potencies, truths, and mellows. Such transcendentally empowered sound vibrations are known as sabda.

The names of the Lord, however, are not actually composed of letters, although they appear to be. Rather, the individual letters are but parts of the eternal sound of the Lord’s fully independent names.

The names of Krsna combined with a bija and a word of praise (jaya), obeisance (namah), or meditation (dhimahi) are what constitute mantras, which are as eternal and divine as the names themselves.

Depending on how these mantras are structured, they direct the unlimited potencies of their presiding Deities to specific goals, just as the name Krsna attracts and the name Rama gives bliss.

Which bija precedes a given mantra also influences its result. For example, aim gauraya namah petitions Gaura for transcendental knowledge, whereas klim gaurdya namah petitions Him for prema.

Any mantra that contains the Supreme Lord’s names is called namatmaka. Such mantras are as powerful as the principal or secondary names of the Lord of which the mantras are comprised.

Indeed, the purpose of all Vedic mantras is to somehow or another bring conditioned souls in contact with — and keep them in contact with — Krsna’s holy names, the source of supreme good fortune.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu mentions a general principle in regard to the names of the Lord, namnam akari bahudha nija-sarva-saktih: “O Lord! You have invested all Your potencies in those names.”

The potency in the name of a particular form of Krsna is the same as the potency in the form itself. For example, the potency of the mantra *om namo bhagavate vasudevaya* is equal to that of Vasudeva.

The more names of Krsna a mantra contains, the better that mantra is, and because the Hare Krsna mantra consists only of the three best names of Krsna in the best arrangement, it is called the maha-mantra.

Jiva Gosvami also asserts in his Bhakti-sandarbha that when transcendental mantras are chanted by great saints, the mantras themselves become surcharged with still more potency.

Initiating gurus also imparts their transcendental touch to mantras. Vyasadeva says,

**“O expert and thoughtful men! Relish Srimad- Bhagavatam, the mature fruit of the desire tree of Vedic literatures.**

**“It emanated from the lips of Sri Sukadeva Gosvami. Therefore this fruit has become even more tasteful, although its nectarean juice was already relishable for all, including liberated souls."**

Saints also sweeten the mantra with the special flavour of the ecstatic love within their hearts and transmit that mellow to those fortunate souls who have the opportunity to hear it from them.

Despite the all-powerful potencies inherent in authorised mantras, the benefit received from chanting them will also depend upon the chanter’s purity of heart and his or her purpose for chanting.

The extent to which bad habits cover the heart proportionately affects our ability to taste the mantra; indeed, the mode of nature affecting us makes our chanting purifying, entangling, or harmful.

Some devotees contend that to receive the seed of loving devotion one must hear a mantra from a spiritual master who is firmly situated in ecstatic love, otherwise one’s chanting will be fruitless.

This argument is false. Krsna is the original source of all mantras, and therefore the seed of love is already within them. Furthermore, many great devotees have watered that seed by their chanting.

An initiating guru not yet transcendentally realised but free from offence functions as an instrument of his own spiritual master. The guru then transfers a mantra to his disciple, its potency intact.

Still, as described earlier, such a guru must act as the personality of servitor Godhead and as a representative of Gauranga, who is the Supreme Lord disguised as the personality of servitor Godhead.

The spiritual master, thinking himself the humble, transparent representative of the disciplic succession from Caitanya Mahaprabhu, acts as a servant of the divine mantra klim gauraya namah.

**\*\*\*\***

Scripture emphatically declares Caitanya Mahaprabhu to be the Supreme Lord, but certain sections of the brahmana community to this day reject Mahaprabhu and the mantras that extol Him.

Such small-minded people do not accept the Gaura mula-mantra and Gaura gayatri as eternally vibrated by Vaisnavas from immemorial time; instead, they insist that these mantras were recently composed.

Srila Prabhupada cites the Atharva-veda-samhita, in which there is a Vedic mantra that was long ago used in the worship of Lord Caitanya as Visvambhara, *visvambhara visvena ma bharasa pahi svaha*.

Moreover, pancaratrika scriptures and the writings of previous acaryas testify that the Gaura mantras, as already described, are worshipped before, during, and after the appearance of Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

In the Urdhvamnaya-samhita, Vyasa says to Narada,

**“O brahmana, you have asked which mantra is used to worship Gauranga. That very secret king of six-syllable mantras is klim gauraya namah.**

**“The words gauraya namah may also come after the bijas of Maya, Rama, Ananga, and Sarasvati, but when they are combined with the kama-bija— klim—they form a desire tree that gives divine love.”**

Sri Caitanya-caritamrta tells how the great devotee Sivananda Sena secretly chanted the four-syllable Gaura-gopala mantra —gauranga— during the time of Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s advent.

The four- and six-syllable mantras may differ in construction, but their essence — worship of Lord Caitanya — is the same. The fact that they have been used throughout history supports their authenticity.

Indeed, Gaura mantras have been chanted since Satya-yuga, when the brahmana Divadasa chanted the name Gauranga as his sadhana, under the authority of the presiding deity of the holy place Puskara.

Lord Nityananda told Jiva Gosvami about Divadasa, who gave up family life and went on pilgrimage. On the way to his beloved Puskara-tirtha he dreamt that he should stay in Navadvipa.

Divadasa followed that divine order and constructed a cottage in Navadvipa, where he lived until he was too old to travel. Still, he deeply lamented that he never completed his pilgrimage to Puskara.

By the power of the dhama, however, the personality of Puskara appeared to the brahmana and revealed the eternal presence of Puskara-tirtha in Navadvipa, where Divadasa then bathed happily.

The personality of Puskara instructed Divadasa about the truths of Navadvipa and Gauranga, “Even after chanting Krsna’s name for millions of years an offensive person cannot awaken loving devotion.

“If one worships Lord Gauranga, however, by chanting His name, one’s crooked mentality will quickly depart, and in a few days one will be able to attain Radha and Krsna in Vrndavana.

“As a result of chanting Gauranga’s name, one will attain a spiritual form and one’s own grove in Vraja. Under the shelter of the gopis, one will he blessed with loving service to the divine couple.”

The point is that the mantras glorifying Lord Caitanya, like other eternal Krsna mantras, have been chanted by practising devotees (sadhakas) and perfected devotees (siddhas) since time immemorial.

And because Lord Caitanya appears as a covered incarnation in the guise of a devotee, His mantras are also shrouded with that same potency, which veils them in mystery to all but those with faith.

Nonetheless, a person who does not accept Lord Caitanya’s divinity and the eternality of His mantras — even after hearing the evidence that substantiates these truths — is condemned with force.

Jarasandha was a strict follower of Vedic rituals, but because he was unable to accept Krsna as the Supreme Lord, he is classified as a demon. The same can be said of one who does not accept Gauranga.

If someone requires logic to accept the scriptural evidence in support of Lord Caitanya’s divinity, then Kaviraja Gosvami declares, “...kindly apply your logic to the mercy of Lord Caitanya.”

One who docs this without prejudice will come to the natural conclusion that Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s munificent dispensation of mercy is possible for the Supreme Lord only and for no one else.

Who else would have abandoned Visnupriya-devi, the goddess of fortune herself, to accept the renounced order and distribute priceless love of Krsna to those who didn’t even want it? Only God!

“O Mahaprabhu! To honour a brahmanas curse, You gave up the goddess of fortune, whose glance is desired by the demigods and whose opulence is the most difficult attachment to renounce.

“To show great mercy to the helpless living entities controlled by maya, You chased after them and gave them devotional service to You. I offer my humble obeisances unto Your lotus feet.”

Whenever I hear this verse, the phrase maya-mrgam dayitayepsitam anvadhavad reminds me of how Lord Caitanya delivered the leper Vasudeva, and as I recall that profound event, my heart melts.

On His tour of South India, Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s habit was to spend each night at the home of a different brahmana, and in the morning continue on His way, happily chanting Hare Krsna.

One morning, after staying at the house of a brahmana named Kurma, the Lord continued His journey as usual. An enlightened brahmana, Vasudeva, who also happened to be a leper, lived nearby.

Vasudeva heard that Caitanya Mahaprabhu was in his village and so he hurried to Kurma’s home only to discover that the Lord had just departed. In utter disappointment, Vasudeva fell unconscious.

When he returned to consciousness, the leper brahmana bitterly lamented having missed the Lord, but Mahaprabhu, the Supersoul within, heard Vasudeva’s cries and at once returned to embrace him.

As soon as Caitanya Mahaprabhu touched Vasudeva, the brahmanas leprosy vanished and his body became young and beautiful. This display of the Lord’s causeless mercy astonished everyone present.

After quoting Sudama Brahmana’s words to Lord Krsna, Vasudeva said,

**“O my merciful Lord, such mercy is not possible for ordinary living entities. Such mercy can be found only in You.**

**“Upon seeing me, even a sinful person goes away due to my bad bodily odour. Yet You have touched me. Such is the independent behaviour of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”**

I consider us all to be like Vasudeva, fallen souls suffering from the leprosy of material enjoyment. And yet because in our own ways we called out to the Lord, Caitanya Mahaprabhu has come to save us.

We must keep Lord Caitanya’s mercy in the front of our minds, and we must live as Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu instructed Vasudeva to live — humbly, always immersed in chanting Krsna’s names.

The Gaura mantra is eternally set in priceless jewels on hexagonal tiles (yantras) surrounding the centre of the Yoga-pitha in Navadvipa, which is identical with the Yoga-pitha in Vrndavana.

The loving exchanges between Srimati Radharani and Sri Krsna and the gopis, such as the rasa dance, expand the lotus of divine sweetness throughout Vraja from the Yoga-pitha in Govinda-sthali.

Similarly, the nama-sankirtana of the Panca-tattva expands the lotus of magnanimity by distributing love for Sri Krsna throughout Navadvipa from the Yoga-pitha at Mayapur.

As they worship Caitanya Mahaprabhu, His bee-like devotees meditate upon this transcendental abode, which is shaped like an eight- petalled lotus spread out on the back of a giant turtle.

The petals of this lotus comprise beautiful groves and gardens that surround the palatial homes of the Lord’s companions, who always perform sankirtana in the mellow of ecstatic love (prema-sankirtana).

Within the house of Jagannatha Misra, at the centre of the Yoga-pitha , is a beautiful temple adorned with the jewels of devotion and other divine gems, along with strings of pearls and flower garlands.

Inside this temple is nama-sankirtana s original scat, a throne with golden sides inlaid with rubies, a backrest made of lapis lazuli, and a silken canopy supported by four spiral-shaped sapphire columns.

The throne is upholstered with a cotton-stuffed seat and tubular back bolster and is surrounded by the letters of the mantra klim gaurdya namah set in sparkling gems on golden hexagonal tiles.

The combined form of Radha and Krsna — Gaura, king of devotee brahmanas — is seated on that throne, surrounded by His expansion, His incarnation, His devotional energy, and His pure devotee.

His golden bodily hue is the colour of the greatest love for Krsna. His shining blackish hair is the colour of the amorous mellow tinted with divine lust and bound together with a string of priceless pearls.

His spotless face is like the full moon rising just above the horizon; His large lotus eyes are restless from the host of ecstasies dancing in His heart, but a steady, gentle smile lights up His face with sweetness.

Dressed in golden-coloured silk robes that flow with the grace of waves on the ocean of mellows, His body sparkles with fresh aguru, sandalwood paste, and mund-enchanting lines of tilaka.

The Lord is covered with various ornaments, from the ankle bells on His pinkish lotus feet, to the diamond earrings flashing upon His cars, to the glowing ornaments jingling alongside His Vaijayanti garland.

O Supreme Lord! O protector of the surrendered souls! You are now playing the role of Your own devotee, and Your lotus feet arc the only object of perpetual meditation for those living entities who are pure.

Your lotus feet destroy all illusion and fulfil all desires. They are the abode of all holy places. Worshipable even by demigods, such as Lord Brahma and Lord Siva, they are the shelter of all that exists.

O Lord! Your lotus feet mitigate the troubles of Your devotees, and they are the only sturdy boat for crossing over the perilous ocean of material existence. Therefore I offer my obeisances unto them.

**\*\*\*\***

The worship of Caitanya Mahaprabhu is eternal and has always been carried out in this world. But in previous ages only a few highly elevated Vaisnavas worshipped Him secretly within their minds.

Prior to Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s descent to this planet and the subsequent unveiling of His eternal names and Deity form, His devotee followers worshipped Him with Krsna mantras.

But after Lord Caitanya’s advent, His devotees began to worship Him openly and directly with His names and the eternal mantras formed from those names, mantras that we chant up to the present day.

The seed vibration klim that introduces the Gaura mula-mantra is called the kama-bija. This seed establishes the essential theme of the Gaudiya Vaisnavas’ meditations, and so it must be well understood.

Later I will discuss klim in detail while speaking of the Gopala mantra. Here I will highlight only the main features of the kama-bija as they relate to offering obeisance to Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

The letters that comprise klim are Radha, Krsna, and Their mutual love. Connected to the Gaura mula-mantra, they show that Gaura is Radha and Krsna, and that He teaches devotees to love Them.

Also implied in klim’s connection to the Gaura mula-mantra is that devotees who have a desire (kama) to attain Radha and Krsna cannot approach the divine couple directly but must go through Gauranga.

The placement of the Gaura mantras between the Guru and Krsna mantras in the sequence of Gaudiya diksa-mantras also shows that worship of Gauranga must precede direct service to Radha and Krsna.

In short, even after sufficiently worshipping our spiritual master, if we approach Radha and Krsna without first worshipping Lord Caitanya, insuperable obstacles and, ultimately, failure will result.

At the end of Dvapara-yuga, Krsna kindly appeared, and in so doing taught that He is God and must therefore be worshipped. But even after such explicit instructions almost everyone misunderstood.

Krsna predicted this in the Gita when He said, “Out of many thousands among men, one may endeavor for perfection, and of those who have achieved perfection, hardly one knows Me in truth."

As Kali-yuga progressed and Krsna’s followers became increasingly confused — especially by Krsna’s mysteriously human-like conduct— they were even more prone to misconstrue Krsna’s message.

This unfortunate state of affairs prompted Krsna to appear as a devotee of Himself, Caitanya Mahaprabhu, to personally explain His teachings and show by His own example how to follow them.

Krsna made surrender a prerequisite for knowing Him, and thus knowledge of Him was very difficult to obtain. But Lord Caitanya is more liberal. By setting no conditions, He made knowing Krsna easy.

Lord Caitanya only requested that we chant Krsna’s holy names and thereby realise the result of the austerities, sacrifices, pilgrimages, and scriptural study normally required to know the Lord.

In this regard, the sage Karabhajana said,

**“In the Age of Kali, intelligent persons perform congregational chanting to worship the incarnation of Godhead who constantly sings the names of Krsna."**

Unfortunately, many people born in India, and even Vaisnavas who should know better, neglect Lord Caitanya and His teachings. They insist on trying to understand Radha and Krsna directly, to no avail.

Professional reciters of Srimad-Bhagavatam are of that ilk. What they teach about Krsna, especially regarding Krsna’s pastimes with the gopis, is poisonous, like milk touched by the lips of a serpent.

Even other Vaisnava sampradayas circumvent Lord Caitanya and are thus confused about the truths regarding Krsna. Some even go so far as to criticise the Supreme Lord for engaging in paramour love.

As a final piece of evidence in favour of worshipping Krsna through Lord Caitanya, one may consider the practical example of how the early members of ISKCON became so knowledgeable about Krsna.

They were not born in Vaisnava families. They were steeped in sinful habits from the beginning of their lives. But they easily mastered the science of Krsna because they surrendered first to Lord Caitanya.

Srila Prabhupada distils all this evidence by saying,

**“\[The\] Krsna consciousness movement means to understand Krsna through Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Otherwise it is very difficult.”**

To honour this sequence of worship—guru, Gaura, then Krsna — temple Deities, prayers, and mantras for meditation are always presented in the same sequence as our Gaudiya initiation mantras.

And so the fact that klim precedes gaurdya namah, and that the Gaura-gayatri precedes the Gopala mantra, indicates that we must worship Gauranga before approaching Radha and Krsna.

As already mentioned, gaurdya namah (“I offer my respectful obeisances unto Gaura”) is an eternal mantra sages have always intoned in their worship of Lord Sri Krsna in His golden form.

We have yet to discuss the reason behind Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s golden colour. It is a complex topic that deserves due consideration. As Srila Prabhupada stated, “That is a big explanation.”

In a prayer to Lord Caitanya, Svarupa Damodara Gosvami says, “I bow down to Him, who has manifested Himself with the sentiment and complexion of Sri Radha although He is Krsna Himself.”

This prayer points out two unique characteristics Krsna adopts from Srimati Radharani that transform Him into Caitanya Mahaprabhu: Her sentiment (bhava) and Her complexion (dyuti).

Just as it is said that “the face is the index of the mind,” upon consideration one concludes that the changes in a person’s complexion often reflect changes in His or Her mood or sentiment.

Krsna’s sentiment as the supreme enjoyer, especially of the amorous mellow known as syama-rasa, permeates His entire being, dyeing Him in its own distinctive, blackish hue; thus He is called Syamasundara.

Srimati Radharani is the embodiment of pure loving devotional sen vice known as mahabhava, which is the colour of molten gold. For that reason She is also known as Tapta-kancana-gaurangi.

When Krsna gives up His own natural sentiments for those of Srimati Radharani, accepting Her desires and emotions as His own, He becomes His own servant and assumes Her complexion.

In short, Caitanya Mahaprabhu has His beautiful golden complexion because He becomes so enriched with Srimati Radharani’s emotions that, forgetting He is Krsna, He fully identifies with being Her.

Rupa Gosvami writes,

**“Krsna desired to taste the limitless nectarean mellows of the love of one of His multitude of loving damsels \[Sri Radha\], and so He has assumed the form of Lord Caitanya.**

**“He has tasted that love while hiding His own dark complexion with Her effulgent yellow colour. May that Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu confer upon us His abundant grace.”**

Gauranga’s golden complexion proclaims His unrestricted access to Srimati Radharani’s confidential sentiments of love. It also declares His boundless desire to glorify Krsna and chant Krsna’s holy names.

As the combined form of Radha and Krsna, who freely gives divine love through the chanting of Their names, Gaura is such an extraordinary incarnation that He must be worshipped (namah).

Srimad-Bhagavatam measures the intelligence of the people in this age by their ability to recognise Caitanya Mahaprabhu and to worship Him through nama-sankirtana as do His eternal associates.

Klim gauraya namah is a call to the twice-born to engage in the sankirtana of Lord Krsna’s names and pastimes as recounted in Srimad-Bhagavatam, and also to worship the Deity of Gauranga.

The result of chanting the Gaura mantra along with the maha-mantra is wonderful because it gives the worshipper access to the heart of Lord Caitanya, and, in turn, to the hearts of Radha and Krsna.

By such access, we develop love for Gaura and Radha-Krsna simultaneously. And if we so desire, we can also earn two spiritual bodies— one as Gaura’s associate, the other as Radha’s and Krsna’s.

The word gaura is defined as something yellow, shining, and pure. Let us offer our obeisances to Gaura with a golden complexion, a radiance that destroys ignorance, and a pure desire that gives love for Vraja.

**“The creeper of pure devotional service, whose fruit yields the most splendid mellow of love for Maharaja Nanda’s son, remained hidden in the Vedas, unknown even to great sages of bygone eras.**

**“But by His great mercy, compassionate Sri Krsna Caitanya made that creeper sprout in the land of Bengal and cultivated it around Will Saci’s son again enter the pathway of my eyes?”**

**\*\*\*\***

*Klim caitanydya vidmahe visvambharaya dhimahi tan no gaurah pracodayat*: “Let us mediate on Lord Caitanya, who is understood as the maintainer of the universe, and who has enthused us to do so.”

In this mantra, the principles of relationship, practice, and ultimate goal — as they pertain to Lord Caitanya, the most munificent form of the Absolute Truth — expand in sequence from the kama-bija, klim.

The Gaura-gayatri states explicitly that which is hidden in the Vedic mantras: Gaura is the centre of all relationships, devotional service to Him is our only duty, and love for Him is the ultimate goal of life.

These three subjects are explained in every aphorism of Vedanta-sutra. Thus Gaura-gayatri, which perfectly summarises the entire Vedic literature, deserves to be studied with intense scrutiny.

Our relationship with Lord Caitanya is revealed to us through the devotional effort of hearing from authority. This is expressed by the words caitanyaya vidmahe: “Let us try to know Lord Caitanya.”

And to try to know Him is all we can do. Suta Gosvami affirms,

**“As the birds fly in the sky as far as their capacity allows, so do the learned devotees describe the Lord as far as their realisation allows.”**

But try we must! After describing Krsna’s rasa dance, the pinnacle of all pastimes meant to entice us back to Goloka Vrndavana, Sukadeva Gosvami tells us that we are now bound to serve the Lord.

That venerable sage says, “Krsna manifests His eternal human-like form and performs His pastimes to show mercy to the devotees. Having heard such pastimes, one must engage in service to Him.”

What’s more, Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura comments that the word bhavet (“must”) makes it compulsory for us to hear about Krsna’s rasa-lila pastimes, and in a wider context, all of His pastimes.

Let us try to know Lord Caitanya, whose very name means “living force” or “consciousness,” who instructed Brahma from within the heart with Vedic knowledge, and who later appeared in Nadiya.

Lord Caitanya’s presence is easy to perceive because He is the source of our existence as well as our ability to perceive that existence. Krsna confirms this when He says, “I am the life of all that lives.”

The Upanisads declare that, as the supreme eternal conscious being, the Lord is the cause of the eternality and consciousness of the infinite number of eternal conscious beings whom He maintains.

Yet Caitanya Mahaprabhu is not simply a distant supervisor of His creation. He participates in perpetual loving exchanges with His loyal companions in the transcendental realm of Svetadvipa.

He also descends to the material world to teach His rebellious children, by His personal example, how they can free themselves from the bonds of fruitive work, which cause endless suffering.

He abandons His godhood to assume the role of a devotee, a feature unique to Lord Caitanya, and one that places Him above all of the Supreme Lord Krsna’s other forms, even Krsna’s original form.

Krsna instructs the world from His position of inimitable divinity, but Caitanya Mahaprabhu becomes one of us and demonstrates by His personal example how to follow the Supreme Lord Krsna’s teachings.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu thinks, “I shall personally inaugurate the religion of the age—nama-sankirtana. I shall accept the role of a devotee and teach devotional service by practising it Myself.”

Lord Caitanya shows extreme humility, patience, and kindness in teaching His wayward children. But his magnanimity extends far beyond displaying and teaching these compassionate qualities. How?

He not only teaches pure devotion as the means of liberation, but He also distributes the most sublime love as the means to attain Krsna. And so Sri Rupa calls Him “the most munificent incarnation.”

Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami invokes auspiciousness before describe ing the nectar of Lord Govinda’s daily pastimes,

**“I surrender to all-merciful Sri Krsna Caitanya, whose activities are all wonderful.**

**“He has converted all three worlds, which were maddened by ignoranṣe, and saved them from their diseased condition by making them mad with the nectar from the treasure-house of love of God.”**

As already mentioned, another of Lord Caitanya’s unique features is His identity as Radha and Krsna combined. But to understand Caitanya’s role as Radha, one must first understand His role as Krsna.

As Srila Prabhupada says,

**“The pleasure potency of Krsna’s internal energy \[Sri Radha\] is a most difficult subject matter, and unless one understands what Krsna is, one cannot understand it.”**

In teaching us how to practice loving devotion to Krsna with our senses, Caitanya Mahaprabhu adopted the role of Srimati Radharani, who always amazes Krsna by the strength and fidelity of Her love.

To safely avoid offence in coming to know Srimati Radharani we must follow the guidance of Srila Prabhupada and know Sri Krsna as Lord Caitanya and as the object of Lord Caitanya’s love.

Otherwise, if we try to know Radha directly, circumventing Lord Caitanya’s teachings and example, we may mistake Her for an ordinary girl and thus lose sight of reality, falling back into illusion.

While instructing Sanatana Gosvami in Varanasi, Lord Caitanya addressed the topic of Krsna’s incarnations, and the verse beginning krsna-varnam tvisdkrsnam, which describes the Lord in Kali-yuga.

Spoken by Karabhajana Muni, this verse says,

**“In the Age of Kali, intelligent persons perform congregational chanting to worship the incarnation of Godhead who constantly sings the names of Krsna.**

**“Although His complexion is not blackish — indeed it is the colour of molten gold — He is Krsna Himself. He is accompanied by His associates, servants, weapons, and confidential companions.”**

Sanatana Gosvami enquired, “How can I understand who is the incarnation for this Age of Kali?” The Lord replied, “By two symptoms— personal characteristics and marginal characteristics.”

The Lord continued, “Bodily features, nature, and form are the personal characteristics (svaripa-laksana). Knowledge of His activities provides the marginal characteristics (tatastha-laksana)."

In trying to know Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, we must thoroughly study these two characteristics in relation to Him, and in a way that remains faithful to His teachings and His personal example.

Lord Caitanya’s bodily hue and nature are His personal characteristics; His distributing love of God and chanting the Lord’s names are His marginal characteristics (activities), as confirmed thus:

**“By performing the sacrifice of congregational chanting of the holy name, scholars in the Age of Kali worship Lord Krsna who is now non-blackish due to the great upsurge of the feelings of Sri Radha.**

**“He is the only worshipable Deity for the paramahamsas, who have attained the highest stage of the fourth order (sannyasa). May that Supreme Person, Lord Caitanya, show us His great causeless mercy.”**

As already mentioned, we can gain further insight by studying why Lord Caitanya appeared. His appearance was twofold: the external reason, and the internal, confidential reason.

The external reason — to inaugurate the nama-sankirtana movement and thereby distribute love of God—has already been discussed. Let us now consider the internal, confidential reason He appeared.

The internal reason — which Srila Prabhupada calls the “main reason” —is also twofold: the Lord wanted to taste the mellow of love for Himself and to distribute spontaneous devotion to the world.

To remove the burden of the world and bestow regulated devotion is the work of Lord Visnu. Only Krsna, however, can distribute the Vraja-vasis’ spontaneous devotion and pure love.

Lord Caitanya’s personal secretary, Svarupa Damodara Gosvami, revealed three aspects of what is called *prema-rasa-niryasa karite asvadana*: “Tasting the sweet essence of the mellows of love of God.”

The three forms of spiritual enjoyment Lord Caitanya tasted as He chanted Krsna’s names, during the festival of Ratha-yatra and especially at night in the solitude of the Gambhira, were as follows:

To fathom the glories of Sri Radha’s love for Krsna, the qualities in Krsna that She alone tastes through Her love, and the ecstasies that She feels when She realises the sweetness of His love.

Our scriptures and acaryas have recounted various pastimes in which Radha and Krsna unite, Their forms combining to become Lord Caitanya, giving Krsna access to Radha’s matchless loving emotions.

The tantras narrate the following pastime: Once Sri Radha approached Krsna and out of transcendental curiosity requested Him to satisfy Her heart’s desire. She said, “Dearly beloved!

“My love for You is so strong that I cannot bear to be separated from You. For this reason I would like to be one in form with You. But this special form should not nullify My own personality.

“Our combined form should retain My moods, and We should both identify with it. It should be a joy to behold, its movements should bestow auspiciousness, and it must be infused with My mahabhava.

Krsna at once agreed to Srimati Radharani’s proposal, assuring His beloved that She would soon behold the object of Her desire. Then the divine couple returned to Their respective homes and duties.

That night Krsna appeared to Radharani in a dream. He had a beautiful golden form endowed with the lustre of Her ecstatic moods, a form that embodied Their mutual embrace and combined energies.

In an inconceivable way, possible only for the universal teacher, Krsna had transformed His one form into a figure that embodied two separate identities — dark Krsna’s inside, fair Radha’s outside.

That form was of a brahmana very fond of devotional service, fully mature in the moods of ecstatic love, and transcendental to all material designations. In this way Radha beheld Gaura in Her dream.

As Jagannatha Dasa Babaji says, “The truths of Lord Caitanya are inconceivable!” No doubt they are. And yet for our own purification we must try to know Lord Caitanya — *caitanyaya vidmahe*.

Knowledge of the Supreme Lord should be put into practice — both for our own benefit and for the benefit of others—especially by the brahmanas. Otherwise we are brahmanas in name only.

Knowledge of Lord Caitanya’s personal and marginal characteristics, as well as the confidential and external reasons He appeared, prepares Vaisnava brahmanas to follow the Lord’s precepts and example.

In this way we enter the realm of cultivation (abhidheya) and execute the nine limbs of devotional service. Our relationship with Visvambhara then matures, becoming more tangible day by day.

The essence of caitanyaya vidmahe is this: One who has not heard of Lord Caitanya is truly unfortunate, and one who has heard of Him and tries to know Him unearths an invaluable treasure.

However, if after coming to know something of that golden dancer we do not serve Him, then our knowledge, brahminical training, chanting of Brahma-gayatri, and worship of Sri Guru is for naught.

**\*\*\*\***

With the sacred words visvambharaya dhimahi let us now meditate on the beautiful golden form of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, who is also known as Visvambhara, the maintainer of the universe.

The phrase visvambharaya dhimahi of the Gaura-gayatri is the devotional cultivation (abhidheya) section, which follows the attainment of knowledge (sambandha) of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

The word abhidheya (“that which should be done”) is formed from the root abhi-dha. As mentioned earlier, knowledge of Lord Caitanya carries with it the obligation to serve. As it is said, *tat-paro bhavet*.

Sincere practitioners, then, will stay constantly absorbed in thoughts of serving (dhimahi) —with body, mind, and words — the mission of Lord Visvambhara, as well as Lord Visvambhara Himself.

Earlier in this work, we studied the word dhimahi by analysing its syllables. Whether it appears explicitly in the Gayatri mantras or implicitly, as in the Gopala mantra, it indicates devotional service.

To be fixed in that devotion, Vaisnavas carefully study the meaning of visvambhara. As Krsna says, “Those who know what they arc doing achieve success in their work, whereas ignorant people do not.”

The verbal root of the word visvambhara is dubhrn, which is related to both “nourishing” and “maintaining.” As the Upanisads declare, Caitanya Mahaprabhu is the one person who maintains all others.

The word visvambhara can also be divided into visva (“everything” or “the universe”) and bhara (“maintainer”), then the name Visvambhara means “He who maintains the universe.”

However, spreading the religion for the age, killing demons, and nourishing and maintaining all living entities are all administrative responsibilities that rest with Lord Visnu, not with Sri Krsna.

Lord Caitanya also nourishes and maintains the universe, but in a way that even Lord Visnu docs not: Sri Caitanya distributes the Vraja-vasis’ love for Krsna and the bliss they taste in that love.

Vrnda-devi once said, “O Paurnamasi! If Lord Hari had not descended in Mathura with Sri Radha, this entire creation — and especially Cupid, the demigod of love — would have been useless.”

We may wonder how beginners and practising devotees, who are still far from attaining love for the son of Maharaja Nanda, benefit from Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s universal showers of love of Godhead.

Actually, some degree of krsna-prema is present at every stage of devotional service. Mahaprabhu’s grace enables all of us to relish at least a drop of divine love’s taste when we chant Hare Krsna.

And that taste of loving devotion, however slight, enables devotees to resist Maya’s allurements and to stay fixed in their determination to come ever closer to Sri Krsna. All glories to Lord Gauranga!

I meditate upon all-enchanting Lord Visvambhara! His resonant voice, effulgent body, singular beauty, tear-filled glance, boundless fame, and well-wishing predisposition nourish all godly souls.

As He dances amongst His companions, like the wind gusting through a forest, He calls out, “Hari! Krsna! Rama!” spreading a soothing sweetness, like ripples across a pond, throughout all three worlds.

A brahmajyoti-like effulgence radiates from His limbs, but this eternal divine light is imbued with bliss and knowledge, which lights up the universe and dispels the dense darkness of Kali-yuga’s ignorance.

His tears of separation from Krsna cascade like the Ganges River as it falls from Brahmapuri to Patalaloka. Those tears bathe celestials, humans, and demons alike in countless waves of ecstatic bliss.

Lord Visvambhara’s intense anguish at the suffering of the conditioned souls, and His great eagerness for them to taste vraja-bhakti's bliss, frees them from the steely chains of self-inflicted sorrow.

His limitless knowledge, piercing logic, and superhuman skill prevail upon even the most bullheaded Mayavadi and Buddhist leaders until their stone hearts finally melt with pure love for Krsna.

Renowned wordsmiths chronicle His beauty, fame, pastimes, and character in their songs and books, giving hope, life, and peace to those souls fortunate enough to hear those poetic masterworks.

Visvambhara is a golden mountain bedecked with the jewels of krsna- prema. He is the sankirtana general who inspires His pure devotees to wander the earth and freely distribute those jewels as gifts to all.

In this way we meditate upon the Supreme Lord Visvambhara as He who maintains us with love for Sri Krsna, and in whom Lord Visnu resides, maintaining all living entities in all other respects.

And while, out of affection, Lord Visnu looks after the welfare of all creatures, Lord Caitanya personally cares for His devotees in a unique way, as Krsna says in the Bhagavad-gita, *yoga-ksemam vahdmy aham*.

To Sanatana Gosvami and Haridasa Thakura, Lord Caitanya says, “I think of you as My little boys, to be maintained by Me— Because of affection I always consider you to be like My little children.”

Srila Prabhupada tells the story of Arjunacarya, a great saint and devotee of the Lord, who doubted that Krsna would personally supply His devotees’ needs, despite the Lord’s assurance in the Gita.

To confirm that He literally meant what He said — that He would provide the necessities of His worshipper — Krsna then personally brought food and other cooking ingredients to Arjunacarya’s home.

The name Visvambhara, then, also denotes the special relationship between the Lord and His devotee, or more specifically, the ties of unconditional affection that tightly bind master and servant.

Needless to say, such an affectionate guardian as Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu will surely maintain those who sacrifice their comforts, and even risk their lives, to preach Krsna’s glories on His behalf.

And so visvambharaya also means that Lord Caitanya is the maintainer of the sankirtana movement, of which He, along with Lord Nityananda, is the founding father (sankirtanaika-pitarau).

**\*\*\*\***

Devotional cultivation begins when we embrace the truth that Visvambhara is the one supreme master and all the rest of us living entities are His servants (*ekale isvara krsna, ara saba bhrtya*).

We devotees identify ourselves as servants of Lord Caitanya, regardless of our level of spiritual advancement, some of us by intellectual conviction, some of us by mature spiritual realisation.

The source of this sense of servitude is Nityananda-Balarama. He and His expansions — along with Their devotees — share this sentiment, as do the residents of Vrndavana, like the gopis and Nanda Maharaja.

One of the characteristics of love for Krsna is that even in Vraja, where Krsna’s divinity is generally covered, that love still inspires superiors, equals, and inferiors to see themselves as Krsna’s servants.

For example, when Uddhava was preparing to leave Vrndavana, the Vraja-vasis brought gifts for him to carry back to Krsna. At that time they all displayed an attitude of service towards their village hero.

Speaking on behalf of his people, Nandaraja said, “May our minds be attached to the lotus feet of your Lord Krsna, may our tongues chant His holy names, and may our bodies lie prostrate before Him.”

Radha and Krsna left the rasa dance to enjoy each other’s company, and Radha, when suddenly left alone by Krsna, made Her sorrow known to Her Lord and master as She cried out in desperation:

“O My Lord, O My husband, O most dearly beloved! O mighty-armed Lord! Where are You? Where are You? O My friend, reveal Yourself to Your maidservant, who is very much aggrieved by Your absence.”

And Advaita Acarya, while dancing wildly, at times loudly sings, “I am a servant of Lord Caitanya, a servant of Lord Caitanya. I am a servant of Lord Caitanya, and a servant of His servants.”

Only through this attitude of service can the sweetness of the Lord be tasted. For this reason, Lord Sri Krsna, eager to taste that sweetness for Himself, assumes the form of a devotee — Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

To cultivate a relationship with Caitanya Mahaprabhu means to accept and gradually perfect one’s service to Him. At perfection, the Lord bestows upon His devotee the eternal mellow of love for Krsna.

Just as Caitanya Mahaprabhu revealed His eternal identity as Radha and Krsna to Ramananda Raya, He will likewise reveal Himself to any sincere devotee who has passed the tests of service and surrender.

All of us know the Supreme Lord Krsna’s call to surrender unto Him. He says, “Abandon all varieties of religion and just surrender unto Me. I shall deliver you from all sinful reactions. Do not fear.”

This surrender demanded of us by the Lord is in no way sentimental. The very substance of dhimahi, our meditation upon Visvambhara, is synonymous with the six-step process known as saranagati.

We who are eager to surrender to the lotus feet of the Lord must accept those things favourable for devotional service, reject all unfavourable things, and believe firmly in the Lord’s protection.

We must also feel exclusively dependent on the mercy of the Lord, have no interest separate from the Lord’s own interest, and feel humble at all times. These six items comprise surrender: saranagati.

The inclination and ability to surrender are both cultivated through nine essential practices of pure devotion, which begin with hearing and chanting about Krsna’s names, forms, qualities, and pastimes.

These are followed by remembering Krsna, serving His lotus feet, worshipping His Deity form, offering prayers to Him, becoming His servant, making Him a friend, and surrendering everything to Him.

Of all devotional practices, the chanting of Krsna’s names is most important; indeed, our acaryas enjoin that the chanting of the Hare Krsna maha-mantra must accompany all other devotional activities.

Jiva Gosvami says, *yad anydpi bhaktih kalau kartavyd, tada tat-samyogenaivety uktam*: “In Kali-yuga, whatever type of bhakti one performs \[like arcana\] must be accompanied by kirtana.”

The maha-mantra will bestow love for Krsna, however, only if it is chanted purely, and so sastra warns us not to commit the ten offences, which block the flow of prṛḍṁ from Krsna’s holy names.

As well as urging us to chant Krsnas names in order to worship Lord Caitanya, the phrase visvambharaya dhimahi also suggests we chant Lord Caitanya’s names to purify our chanting of the maha-mantra.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu appears also as five eternal truths. Chanting the names of these five truths blesses devotees with “the competency to chant the Hare Krsna maha-mantra without offence.”

In this way, the phrasevisvambharaya dhimahi also invites devotees to chant the *Panca-tattva maha-mantra, sri-krsna-caitanya prabhu-nityananda sri-advaita gadadhara srivasadi-gaura-bhakta-vrnda*.

Moreover, the phrase visvambharaya dhimahi asks the followers of Caitanya Mahaprabhu to go door to door and induce people to chant Krsna’s names, to worship Krsna, and to teach others about Krsna.

One day Lord Caitanya unexpectedly ordered Nityananda Prabhu and Haridasa Thakura, “Listen! Listen, O Nityananda! Listen, O Haridasa! I request you to follow My order in every respect.

“Go to every home in Navadvipa with this request, ‘O brother! Please chant Krsna’s name! Worship Krsna, and teach others about Krsna!’ (bolo krsna, bhaja krsna, êîrî krsna-siksa).

“Don’t tell them anything else. Make them chant. At the end of the day come back and tell Me what has happened. Beg them to chant. If anyone will not, then I will cut him apart with My cakra.”

Those who sincerely study Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s teachings will readily acknowledge that the Lord wants His servants to help Him in His mission to distribute the holy names throughout the world.

Only immature devotees who haven’t fully understood the will and teachings of the Lord sit idly by and contemplate the mercy of Sri Caitanya while the Lord Himself works for the ultimate welfare of all.

Lord Krsna makes His expectations clear when He says, “It is the duty of every living being to perform welfare activities for the benefit of others with his life, wealth, intelligence, and words.”

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu further defines the kind of welfare activities that everyone —especially initiated devotees — should adopt. To the brahmana Kurma, Caitanya Mahaprabhu said:

“Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Sri Krsna as they are given in the Bhagavad-gita and Srimad-Bhagavatam. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.”

But even after hearing this instruction, some Gaudiya Vaisnavas object that they would rather not compromise their own spiritual progress by keeping company with non-devotees and materialists.

Such arguments of the faithless and weak of heart minimise Lord Caitanya’s assurance that the waves of material life (visaya-taranga) cannot touch a preacher, whom the Lord personally protects.

The worship of Caitanya Mahaprabhu is successfully performed not merely by meditating on the Lord, nor even by decorating the Lord’s Deity form. It is accomplished by serving the mission of the Lord.

Srila Prabhupada often said that the first-class devotee is always absorbed in thinking of how to spread Krsna consciousness all over the world. That is one meaning of dhimahi. His Divine Grace writes:

**“When a person realises himself to be an eternal servitor of Krsna, he loses interest in everything but Krsna’s service. Always thinking of Krsna, devising means by which to spread the holy name of Krsna —**

**“He understands that his only business is in spreading the Krsna consciousness movement all over the world. Such a person is to be recognised as an uttama-adhikari \[a topmost devotee\].”**

This preaching spirit, characteristic of the bhagavatas-marga, is an indispensable aspect of service to the Lord; still, conditioned souls must follow the pancaratrika system and worship the Deity as well.

The sankirtana movement’s leading Deities are Gaura-Nitai. Unmindful of offences, They accept worship through regulative devotional practice and, in return, bestow spontaneous vraja-bhakti.

And so They are called parama-karuna, “the most merciful incarnations.” Their process of worship is also praised as the easiest and most joyful, for it is composed of singing, dancing, and feasting.

By serving His name, Deity form, devotees, and mission, let us worship Visvambhara, the maintainer of His devotees, of the sankirtana movement, and of pure devotion—visvambharaya dhimahi.

**\*\*\*\***

The Gaura-gayatri mantra aims at infusing all inoffensive chanters with the potency (tat) that inspires (pracodayat) Lord Caitanya to dance and chant in ecstasy — *tan no gaurah pracodayat*.

The full extent of Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s enthusiasm for Krsna is unfathomable. But we get a hint of how great His loving enthusiasm is by how it transforms His blackish complexion to a golden one.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu is not only the embodiment of enthusiasm for Krsna consciousness, but also the source of consciousness itself. And so by His blessings we may also become inspired with ananda.

Vaisnava brahmanas pray to Caitanya Mahaprabhu for the ultimate goal of life, the gift of loving devotion in the same form that relentlessly stirs His own heart — the pure devotion of the Vraja gopis.

Karabhajana Muni, one of the famed nine Yogendras, after speaking of the incarnation for this age, offers prayers ideal for meditating on Caitanya Mahaprabhu and Radha-Krsna while chanting Gayatri.

In this way Karabhajana implies that the goal (prayojana) of the aforementioned sacrifice of chanting Krsna’s holy names may be the eternal shelter of Lord Caitanya, or of Radha and Krsna, or of both.

That all-knowing nava-yogendra prays,

**“My dear Lord! You are the Maha-purusa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and I worship Your lotus feet, which are the only eternal objects of meditation.**

**“Those feet destroy the embarrassing conditions of material life and freely award the greatest desire of the soul, the attainment of pure love of Godhead. My dear Lord!**

**“Your lotus feet are the shelter of all holy places and of all saintly authorities in the line of devotional service and are honoured hy powerful demigods like Lord Siva and Lord Brahma.**

**“My Lord, You are so kind that You willingly protect all those who simply bow down to You with respect, and thus You mercifully relieve all the distress of Your \[sincere \] servants.**

**“In conclusion, my Lord, Your lotus feet are actually the suitable boat for crossing over the ocean of birth and death, and therefore even Lord Brahma and Lord Siva seek shelter at Your lotus feet.”**

The first word of this prayer, dhyeyam (“fit to be meditated upon”), establishes the lotus feet of the golden-complexioned Maha-purusa as the object indicated by the word dhimahi in the Gayatri mantra.

The next word, sada (“always”), implies that there are no fixed rules governing the chanting of the Lord’s name, as mentioned in the verse preceding Karabhajana’s prayer, for the goal is to chant constantly.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu Himself has written that all of Krsna’s personal potencies are invested in His names, and there is no time or place inappropriate for chanting them (tatrarpita niyamitah).

What will be the result of constant chanting? One will attain Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet, which are the abode of all holy places (tirthaspadam) and the boat by which to cross the ocean of suffering.

It is true that while serving Krsna, practising devotees may sometimes he distracted by the vicious qualities of Kali-yuga, like lust or greed. But Visvanatha’s commentary on Karabhajana’s prayer reassures us.

The essence of his commentary is that those who faithfully serve Lord Caitanya’s mission are protected and delivered from the inevitable defects they contact by living in the contaminated Age of Kali.

The words bhrtyarti-ham (“which relieve the distress of Your servants”) imply that Lord Caitanya removes the miseries of devotees and awards them love for Krsna, as He did for the leper Vasudeva.

In his next prayer, Karabhajana reveals that Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s unbounded enthusiasm makes the Lord run after the conditioned souls to share His own pure bliss with them. The sage says:

**“O Maha-purusa! I worship Your lotus feet. You gave up the association of the goddess of fortune and all her opulence, which is most difficult to renounce and which even the demigods hanker for.**

**“Being the most faithful follower of the path of religion, You thus left for the forest in obedience to a brahmana s curse. Out of sheer mercifulness You chased after the fallen conditioned souls.**

**“Like a master running to catch His pet, You pursued they who are always in pursuit of false enjoyment and are at the same time engaged in searching out Your own desired object, Lord Syamasundara.”**

This prayer is undoubtedly an offering to Caitanya Mahaprabhu, the Maha-purusa, who as a young man married Visnupriya-devi, the incarnation of the goddess of fortune and the mistress of all opulence.

It also refers to a brahmana in Navadvipa who, unable to enter Srivasa Thakura’s house to participate in the Lord’s nocturnal kirtanas, became disturbed in mind and angry with the Lord.

The next day the brahmana saw Caitanya Mahaprabhu by the Ganges and pronounced this curse: “Because Your behaviour has displeased me, I now curse You to be bereft of all material happiness!”

In the mood of Krsna’s devotee, Lord Caitanya heard the brahmanas words and jubilantly thought, “If My hopes for material enjoyment are finished, then service to Krsna will be My only shelter.”

On the pretext of the brahmana’s curse, the Lord gave up His beautiful wife and opulent household, embraced the renounced order of life, and walked from forest to forest throughout South India.

Everywhere, Lord Caitanya induced materialistic people, for whom the illusions of mayd were everything, to chant Krsna’s names, confirming that of all incarnations He is the most merciful (dayita).

Karabhajana’s devotional meditation foretold the Lord’s eternal pastimes, which were recorded almost fifty centuries later by Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s biographers like Vrndavana Dasa Thakura.

In offering homage (vande) to the Lord, Karabhajana Muni discloses that the shelter of Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet are the goal of those who unreservedly give themselves to the process of nama-sankirtana.

The enthusiastic worship of Mahaprabhu by chanting Krsna’s names admits us into Svetadvipa, where the Lord and His companions perform sankirtana with ever-expanding enthusiasm (pracodayat).

Jiva Gosvami s comment on this verse gives an alternate reading, which says the verse can also refer to Krsna, who left the opulence of Mathura (*surepsita-rajya-laksmim*) to live in Vrndavana forest.

Just after His birth, Krsna’s parents, Vasudeva and Devaki, out of concern for their son’s safety, requested Him (arya-vacasa) to somehow avoid Kamsa’s wrath, which Krsna did by moving to Vraja.

Krsna showed the highest regard for religious principles (dharmistha) by killing many demons and revelling in boyish acts (anvadhavat), like playing games, tending cows, and romancing with the gopis.

But most significantly, because of Her great love for Him, Krsna always remained just like a toy animal (mrgam), a plaything in the hands of His own internal potency (maya) — Srimati Radharani.

And so, although it appeared that Krsna left Vrndavana for Dvaraka, the ropes of Radha’s love kept Him bound to Vraja; in fact, it was His Vasudeva expansion who left, for Krsna never leaves Vrndavana.

The aspiration that arises in the heart from performing pure nama-sankirtana determines whether one gains entrance into Lord Caitanya’s pastimes, Lord Krsna’s pastimes, or both simultaneously.

In other words, for a devotee who aspires only for Lord Caitanya’s internal mood — that of a gopi worshipping Krsna — Gaura-gayatri offers promotion to celestial Vraja as a gopi companion of Krsna.

Or, for a devotee who aspires for the Lord’s external mood — that of a devotee distributing the holy names — it offers promotion to celestial Navadvipa as a pure devotee companion of Gauranga Mahaprabhu.

And finally, a devotee who is equally inspired by Mahaprabhu’s internal and external moods may simultaneously attain two spiritual bodies —that of a Vraja gopi and also of a perfected sadhaka.

Such wonderful perfections are available only to the followers of Lord Caitanya, not to devotees of any other incarnation of the Lord, not even to devotees of Lord Krsna. As Prabodhananda Sarasvati writes:

**“Unknown even to the devotees full of love for Govinda, the secrets of pure devotional service have been revealed by the holy name during Gaura's advent. Let me glorify that Lord Gaura.”**

Genuine spiritual enthusiasm arises from contact with the Lord’s internal potency, a contact initiated at the stage of bhava and reaching maturity when prema fully blossoms in the heart.

It is this intense spiritual enthusiasm, which begins at the stage of bhava, that practising devotees aspire for when they chant tan no gaurah pracodayat. Rupa Gosvami defines sadhana-bhakti in this way:

**“When transcendental devotional service, by which love for Krsna is attained, is executed by the senses, it is called sadhana-bhakti, or the regulative discharge of devotional service.**

**“Such devotion eternally exists within the heart of every living entity. The awakening of this eternal devotion is the potentiality of devotional service in practice.”**

Such is the way of bhakti.

And this aspiration, when directed towards following in the footsteps of the gopis, as detailed in the Gopala mantra and Kama-gayatri, fully matures only through the grace of Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

In the course of that maturation, the relationship one enjoys with Lord Caitanya is always in the mood of servitude, and the cultivation of pure devotion almost always occurs through regulated practice.

When a ray of Mahaprabhu’s divine enthusiasm touches one of His servants, the lotus of aspiration unfolds the petals of spontaneous love. We should all aspire for this — tan no gaurah pracodayat.

**\*\*\*\***

As previously mentioned, the practising devotee’s relationship with Caitanya Mahaprabhu always stays in the realms of neutrality and servitude. It does not rise to the three higher rasas.

As a sincere and humble devotee serves and worships Caitanya Mahaprabhu, his unique, individual, and eternal relationship with Krsna in Vrndavana will spontaneously awaken within his heart.

Gaura and Radha-Krsna are one, but because Their moods and pastimes differ, the way in which They accept worship also differs. It is wrong to worship Gaura as one would worship the divine couple.

If a practising devotee so aspires, as soon as the worship of Caitanya Mahaprabhu in the mood of servitorship fully matures, feelings of amorous love for Krsna naturally awaken within the devotee’s heart.

At that time, the blessings of Caitanya Mahaprabhu enable such a fortunate devotee to enter the transcendental realm of Sri Vrndavana to serve Radha and Krsna as a follower of the gopis.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura narrates how Srila Jiva Gosvami, after being led by Lord Nityananda on a parikrama of Navadvipa-dhama, received Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s blessings to enter Vrndavana.

The Thakura writes,

**“One night along the way to Vrndavana, Gauranga appeared to Jiva in a dream and said, ‘Go to Mathura. You and your uncles, Rupa and Sanatana, are all very dear to Me.**

**‘“Together you should compose books that will illuminate the path of devotional service. Dedicate your life to the service of My form as Radha and Krsna, and always contemplate Their pastimes in Vraja.’”**

Wherever they may be, and whatever time of day it is, a practical way for devotees to remember Krsna’s pastimes is to think about what the Lord is doing at that time of day in eternal Vrndavana.

Even after becoming adept at recalling the pastimes of Radha and Krsna in Vrndavana, advanced devotees will also spend time remembering the pastimes of Caitanya Mahaprabhu in Navadvipa.

Sri Rupa Gosvami and Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura have both written works called Sriman Mahaprabhor Asta-kaliya-lila-smarana-mangala-stotra, which relate Gauranga’s daily pastimes.

This chapter concludes with a summary of those two works. I pray this summary will help us enter deeper into the chanting of Gaura-gayatri and qualify us to properly approach the Gopala mantra.

Fixing our minds upon the handsome form of Gaura, whose hair is braided with strands of jasmine flowers, whose limbs are graced with many ornaments, upon whose neck hangs a Vaijayanti garland —

Whose feet are adorned with tinkling anklets, and whose every limb is decorated with signs of ecstasy even demigods and sages can only hear about, we remember Gaura’s eightfold daily pastimes.

At the end of night, as dawn approaches, the pleasant sound of chirping birds rouses Caitanya Mahaprabhu, who at once thinks of Radha and Krsna awakening together in a secret kunja of Vraja.

Thoughts of the divine couple stir the Lord's ecstatic love, which causes a shower of tears to soak His entire body. After composing Himself, He pleases his wife, Visnupriya, with sweet words.

The Lord then goes to another room and sits upon a dais while His devotees help wash His lotus face. Refreshed, He goes to greet His mother and other relatives and friends who have come to see Him.

Seated in the courtyard of His home, absorbed in the mood of Srimati Radharani, the golden Lord describes Krsna’s morning pastimes at Nandagrama, as symptoms of ecstasy dance upon His limbs.

The sun slowly rises while Caitanya Mahaprabhu and His friends go to worship and bathe in the Canges, after which the Lord is dressed in silk cloth, fine ornaments, flower garlands, and sandalwood paste.

Sri Caitanya then returns home to perform religious rituals, worship Lord Visnu, and honour the Lord’s remnants with His devotees. Then Gauranga happily sleeps for some time and dreams of Krsna.

At the arrival of forenoon, Gaura at once awakens and plunges into remembrance of Krsna’s forenoon pastimes, in which the darling boy of Vraja leaves Nandagrama for the forest to tend His father’s cows.

Gaura so closely identifies with the anxiety Krsna’s departure causes the Vraja-vasis that He imitates their words and actions, exciting His companions — who are none other than those same residents of Vraja.
