“These girls simply thought of Him as their male lover and associated with Him in that intimate mood. Thus their karmic bondage was nullified and they abandoned their gross material bodies.”

At this point readers may be wondering how this study of the rsi-cari gopis’ evolution can assist us in our own spiritual development or help us to fulfil the order (dhimahi) of the Kama-gayatri mantra.

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura answers this question by revealing Sukadeva Gosvami’s confidential and ultimate intention, which was to inspire devotees to surrender themselves to Krsna.

When the gopis were confined to their rooms, they felt such intolerable separation from Krsna that the very force of their agony, expressed through crying and so on, destroyed all inauspicious things.

The acarya explains that by hearing about the pain of separation the rsi-cari gopis felt, our false egos are dismantled. Our selfishness shaken to the core, we become ashamed of our petty attachments.

Furthermore, when the rsi-cari gopis meditated upon Krsna, He eventually appeared to them in person, full of love, and drove away their sorrow by embracing them with His transcendental limbs.

Within their own homes these gopis tasted loving exchanges with the transcendental Cupid. All other aspirations, material and spiritual, were now wholly inconsequential, for they had attained perfection.

When we hear of the accomplishments of the gopis, we become convinced that our paltry quests for sense gratification, and even liberation in the name of devotion, are insignificant by comparison.

As a result we achieve resolve in our devotional practices (bhajana). Freed from reactions to past pious and sinful acts, we gradually begin to eliminate bad habits through the process of anartha-nivrtti.

The answer, then, to the question of whether practising devotees benefit from hearing this pastime is an emphatic yes. And the ways in which we benefit have been mentioned in the previous stanzas.

Along with describing the liberation of the rsi-cari gopis, Sukadeva Gosvami points out the informal nature of their relationships with Krsna and how those liaisons served to spiritualise their bodies.

Like all of their peers, the rsi-cari gopis who could not leave their homes were unaware that Krsna was the Supreme Lord. They knew Him only as their lover, and they mingled with Him in that way.

Scriptures deplore and forbid unwedded love, but Lord Krsna, as His pastime, showed that this ordinarily contemptible practice, when in relation to Him, is the highest expression of transcendental love.

Devotional scholars praise the gopis’ love above even that of Krsna’s queens in Dvaraka, whose reverence for their husband obstructs them from realising Krsna’s personality to the extent Vraja’s milkmaids do.

By His Vrndavana pastimes Krsna sets new standards by which to evaluate transcendental opulence; simple gunja necklaces and peacock feathers are more valuable than ornaments or crowns of gold.

And paramour love, ordinarily condemned, is made superior to love sanctified by vows of marriage, and even to the exalted meditation of perfected yogis. Such is the transcendental influence of the Lord.

Yet even those who accept such unconventional standards may question on another level the propriety of Lord Krsna’s sporting intimately with women who have already been enjoyed by others.

The eternal principles of worship dictate that only uncontaminated things can be offered to the Lord. Anything already enjoyed by another is automatically deemed unfit for offering to Krsna.

This objection is addressed by the phrase jahur guna-mayam deham, “they abandoned their gross material bodies.” Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura elucidates the meaning of this phrase.

He writes that prior to hearing the sound of Krsna’s flute, the rsi-cari gopis’ bodies were simultaneously material and spiritual. It was the material aspect of those bodies that their husbands had enjoyed.

After hearing Krsna’s flute and being purified by the ordeal of separation from Him, the gopis’ bodies changed. The material aspects fell away and only pristine spiritual bodies remained.

How can the body be partly material and partly spiritual? By engaging in the spiritual activities of chanting, hearing, and remembering Krsna, our senses take on spiritual qualities — they are spiritualised.

Krsna tells Uddhava, *nirguno mad-apasrayah*: “One who has taken shelter of Me is understood to be transcendental to the modes of nature.” And yet the material constituents of the body remain.

The material aspects of our bodies are more evident when in ordinary affairs we speak, hear, and remember mundane things. Even as devotees, then, our bodies have material and spiritual aspects.

The relative proportions of these two aspects depend upon the degree to which we are absorbed in spiritual activities; the more Krsna conscious we are, the more prominent is the spiritual aspect.

This transformation in which the material aspect of our bodies disappears completely, and we become spiritualised at the stage of prema, is described by Sri Kavi to Maharaja Nimi:

**“Devotion, direct experience of the Supreme Lord, and detachment from other things — these three occur simultaneously for one who has taken shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead —**

**“In the same way that pleasure, nourishment, and relief from hunger come simultaneously and increasingly, with each bite, for a person engaged in eating.”**

In the context of this topic we may wonder:

“Why, if their bodies are completely spiritual, do we see pure devotees with unalloyed love for Krsna, such as Srila Prabhupada, leave a material body behind at the time of death?”

Premi bhaktas leave behind their material bodies for two reasons: to strengthen the false arguments of atheists and to protect the confidential nature of pure devotional service.

Nevertheless, to confirm the spiritual nature of His servants’ bodies, Krsna occasionally allows devotees — such as Dhruva Maharaja or the Pandavas—to return to the spiritual abode without a change of body.

Returning to our discussion of the rsi-cari gopis, it is clear from the preceding arguments that as their absorption in Lord Krsna intensified, the material aspects of their bodies gradually dissolved.

Only after the gopis’ bodies manifested their purely spiritual nature did Krsna appear before them and directly accept their service, for they were now unsullied and free from past karmic contamination.

Now that these rsi-cari gopis had also attained loving devotion, Yogamaya fully supported their efforts to be with Krsna. Their families and husbands, therefore, could no longer obstruct them.

Years later, while Krsna was living in Mathura, He sent the gopis a message in which He reminded the rsi-cari gopis of this incident: “Although some gopis had to remain in the cowherd village —

“And so could not join the rasa dance to sport with Me at night in the forest, they were nonetheless fortunate. Indeed, they attained Me by thinking of My potent pastimes.”

When the rsi-cari gopis heard this message they were very pleased, for it revived their memory of that pastime. They, along with the other gopis, then sent messages of love to their most dearly beloved.

Since these rsi-cari gopis were still present in Vraja long after the rasa dance, the meaning of their abandoning “their gross material bodies” is that they gave up their material bodies without dying, like Dhruva.

Since abhidheya is the process of becoming fit to associate with “the one with a spiritual body” (ananga), the rsi-cari gopis offer an instructive example in the development of one’s spiritual body.

Under the direction of Govindaji, Yogamaya arranges for sincere devotees — at varying degrees of spiritual advancement — to qualify for the amorous mood that so enchants Gopijana-vallabha.

Of the Dandaka sages who took birth as rsi-cari gopis in Gokula, those who were qualified to attain prema early in life were blessed with spiritual bodies that were never touched by men.

These were the gopis whom Yogamaya permitted to follow the call of Krsna’s flute. They, like the other qualified and eternally liberated gopis, faced no obstruction in leaving their homes and meeting Krsna.

The second group of rsi-cari gopis were less qualified and slower to advance. Because they were still conditioned and bound by family obligations, they married and bore children by their husbands.

By the fire of separation from Krsna, however, they gradually gave up the material aspect of their bodies, and in their purified spiritual bodies pure kama was awakened and burned brightly.

Thus, on the night of the rasa dance, after inciting a slight quarrel with their husbands, Yogamaya sent this second class of gopis out to Krsna, after the first group of rsi-cari gopis had already left.

And the third group of rsi-cari gopis — of whom we have just heard about at length — who harboured some lingering impurity, Yogamaya kept locked up in their husbands’ homes, where Krsna came to them.

Since they were finally freed of all contamination and fully qualified for Krsna’s association, Yogamaya arranged for these gopis to go out on another night, and thereafter to be with Krsna at will.

What happened to the rsi-cari gopis when they returned home? Since they no longer had any attachment to their husbands or children, Yogamaya sheltered them from further contact with their husbands.

Immersed in the ocean of love for Krsna, wherein the aquatic predators of His qualities and pastimes continually devoured their minds, the gopis appeared to their families to be possessed by ghosts.

In this deranged condition they were unable to perform any household chores and their breasts no longer gave milk. Unable to care for even their own children, they were scorned by their families.

Although their husbands and families thought the gopis had gone mad, learned sages like Uddhava were transcendentally envious, for they could recognise the gopis’ symptoms as devotional ecstasies.

Yogamaya arranged for these girls to be completely shunned by their families so they would be free to move about the forests of Vrndavana and meet unrestrictedly with Krsna, the only master of their heart.

Since all the rsi-cari gopis now possessed fully spiritual bodies, there can be no objection to Krsna accepting them. The gopis were golden chalices, newly cast, from which Krsna drank the nectar of pure love.

**\*\*\*\***

The path traversed by the Dandakaranya sages — meditating on their diksa-mantra, taking birth as gopis during Krsna’s manifest pastimes, and at last attaining perfection — is open to everyone.

In fact Srila Prabhupada declares that the ultimate purpose of his mission is to enable his followers to attain the same purity by which the rsi-cari gopis were able to freely sport with Krsna.

He says,

**“This Krsna consciousness movement is for approaching Radha-Krsna, to be associated with the Supreme Lord in His sublime pleasure dance. That is the aim of Krsna consciousness.”**

But although we daily chant Kama-gayatri, which explicitly extols Krsna’s amorous sports, we should remain cautious about hearing or meditating upon Krsna’s pastimes with the gopis in other ways.

Srila Prabhupada repeatedly cautioned us to avoid devotional practices for which we are not qualified. At the same time, he urged us to systematically become qualified for loving service to Krsna.

In his introduction to the “Krsna Book,” Srila Prabhupada prescribes hearing Krsna’s pastimes with the gopis not only as an antidote to sex life but also as a way to attract materialists to Krsna.

Paraphrasing the famous “vikriditam” sloka, he writes:

**“In the Bhagavatam it is stated that if one hears the pastimes of Lord Krsna with the gopis, from authorities with submission, then —**

**“He will be promoted to... transcendental loving service to the Lord, and the material disease of lust within His heart will be completely vanquished...such hearing will counteract material sex life.”**

Madhvacarya quotes another verse from the Skanda Purana that makes the same case:

**“By the mercy of Lord Visnu, one who follows the path of devotional service gains liberation as a by-product —**

**“And such a person’s lusty desire, which would normally invoke misfortune, instead invokes the mercy of the Supreme Lord Sri Visnu when exhibited in pure devotion.”**

How merciful is Lord Krsna!

To explain who is, or isn’t, qualified for what level of absorption in Krsna’s pastimes would take more time than we have to spend here, but those who have received diksa-mantras should consider this:

By thrice daily repeating the Kama-gayatri ten times, we enjoin ourselves to become acquainted with the truths, mellows, and pastimes of the transcendental Cupid of Vrndavana, Kamadeva.

In so doing we regularly urge ourselves — and others — to engage in devotional acts based upon hearing about Krsna, by which our attachment for that lover of the gopis may continually expand.

And finally, we pray to become immersed in the same loving mood that attracts Krsna to hunt the gopis with His flower-arrows, and which inspires the gopis to be willing game for their pursuer.

Raghupati Upadhyaya once described Krsna as a hunter of the gopis to Caitanya Mahaprabhu,

*êam prati kathayitum ise samprati êî va pratitim ayatu*  
*go-pati-tanaya-kunje gopa-vadhuti-vitam brahma*

**“To whom can I speak who will believe me when I say that Krsna, the Supreme Lord, is hunting the gopis in the bushes by the banks of the river Yamuna? In this way the Lord demonstrates His pastimes.”**

Krsna’s and the gopis’ amorous love is the theme of the Kama-gayatri. By receiving this mantra we acknowledge that Srila Prabhupada considered initiates qualified to cultivate its mood.

Slowly, carefully, and systematically, then, we should learn about (vidmahe), cultivate (dhimahi), and pray for the pure devotional mood of the gopis (pracodayat) that is so attractive to the Lord.

At the very heart of the Kama-gayatri mantra is the process that uncovers our latent, spontaneous Krsna consciousness, a process inspired by a longing for the Krsna consciousness of the gopis.

Intense longing for the gopis’ natural attachment to their beloved is gained and nurtured by hearing not only Krsna’s amorous pastimes themselves but also how that act of hearing draws us closer to Him.

To reiterate, the rsi-cari gopis exemplify how attraction to Krsna develops and leads to liberation, a topic Pariksit Maharaja inquired about to benefit the audience of Srimad-Bhagavatam.

Pariksit was amazed at how the gopis, ignorant of Krsna’s divinity and attracted to the Supreme Lord as a paramour, could have been freed from the powerful influence of the material modes.

In reply, Sukadeva Gosvami reminded his disciple of an incident he had earlier described — Sisupala’s death. If Sisupala, who hated Krsna, had been liberated, then what lay in store for the adoring gopis?

To comprehend the rsi-cari gopis’ liberation, which is especially wonderful since they appeared to be simple lusty girls, one must bear in mind Krsna’s omnipotence, and His desire to liberate the world.

As practising devotees, we may sometimes wonder how we will ever surmount the modes of nature, let alone achieve love of God. But it behoves us to remember that Krsna is the shelter of His servants.

Even if the rsi-cari gopis — or for that matter any practising devotees — are covered by the modes of nature, Krsna never is. He is never hampered in reciprocating our affection for Him.

Sripada Madhvacarya gives scriptural evidence to prove that the rsi-cari gopis were not lusting after Krsna; rather, their kama grew from previously existing knowledge and their pure devotion to the Lord.

They were, after all, learned sages who had already concluded that Krsna was the Absolute Truth, and who had rendered pure devotional service, which endeared them to Lord Rama.

In the same way, a practising devotee with a good foundation of transcendental knowledge and regulated devotion may gradually awaken an attraction for Krsna like that of the Vraja-vasis.

Although Sisupala intensely hated Krsna, his emotion was based on previous knowledge and devotion as the Lord’s gatekeeper Jaya. That devotion purchased the Lord’s mercy and liberated Sisupala.

One who is favourable and affectionate towards Krsna — if even conditionally so-is surely a candidate for Krsna’s mercy, which the Lord is always more eager to bestow than we are to accept.

Krsna appears in this world, displays His pastimes, and speaks eternal wisdom only to benefit the conditioned souls, whom He loves dearly but who have yet to recall the process of learning how to love Him.

If after hearing about Him a lost soul develops a desire to be His servant, friend, parent, or lover, Krsna reciprocates with and nurtures such attachment. Wherever there is devotion, Krsna’s mercy follows.

Even though tainted with lust, anger, or fear, a practising devotee who directs his imperfect devotion to Krsna becomes entangled in the unlimited attributes of the powerful, attractive, and merciful Lord.

Although Sri Suka had spoken on this subject earlier, he repeated this principle,

*kamam krodham bhayam sneham aikyam sauhrdam eva ca*  
*nityam harau vidadhato yanti tan-mayatam hi te*

**“Persons who constantly direct their lust, anger, fear, protective affection, feelings of impersonal oneness, or friendship towards Lord Hari are sure to become absorbed in thought of Him.”**

Krsna and His names and pastimes are attractive in every way. The result of being attracted to Him is to become absorbed in Him, and if one becomes absorbed in Him — the all-pure — one’s heart is purified.

As one’s heart is progressively purified by absorption, he becomes even more attached to Krsna, which again accelerates this unending cycle of increasing absorption, purification, and attachment.

In due course of time such a fortunate soul becomes so absorbed in the wonders of Krsna consciousness that he attains indifference to the material world — liberation comes without separate endeavour.

Therefore, as Srila Prabhupada was fond of quoting, *tasmat kenapy upayena manah krsne nivesayet*: “Somehow or other, by any means, one should fix the mind on Sri Krsna.” This is actual dhimahi.

We may rightly question how, without knowing the techniques of smaranam, we can make efforts (upayena) to remember Krsna. Srila Prabhupada would address this doubt with another favourite quote.

*Sarve vidhi-nisedhah syur etayor eva kinkarah*: “All the rules and prohibitions mentioned in sastra should be the servants of these two principles \[always remember Krsna and never forget Him\].”

At the beginning of our practice (abhidheya), whatever way we remember Krsna while chanting Kama-gayatri — or any other mantra, especially the Hare Krsna maha-mantra— is the right way.

To cultivate our personal remembrance of Krsna, in time we may receive and apply other rules and prohibitions from our spiritual masters, until at last such remembrance comes spontaneously.

Somehow we must become attached to Krsna. And if we serve Him with such attachment, He personally provides the intelligence to overcome obstacles and fulfil the requirements for meeting Him.

Yet we must always bear in mind that perfected attraction, such as the rsi-cari gopis attained, appears only at the stage of liberation. The emotion we feel before we are liberated is but a shadow of pure love.

In the ecstasy of love, the gopis forget that Krsna is the Supreme Lord, but we, who are not at that standard of pure devotion, must not, for if we do forget we will compromise our hard-earned spiritual credits.

We should not be foolish and think — like the sahajiyas — that we can lust after Krsna, neglect the regulated practice of devotional service founded upon knowledge and detachment, and still be liberated.

For example, of the Apsaras in heaven who have lusty feelings for Krsna, only those who have pure devotion for Him are eligible for liberation, not others, no matter how beautiful they may be.

When Pariksit Maharaja voiced surprise that the rsi-cari gopis could attain liberation by worshipping Krsna as their paramour, he was only echoing the doubts of certain sages present.

The truth is that no one can be liberated without the practice of pure devotional service. It was the gopis’ devotion that burned up their karmic bonds and admitted them into Krsna’s company.

To emphasise this truth, Sukadeva Gosvami responded to his disciple’s initial surprise by reminding him that it is the Lord who liberates this world, thus he should not have been so astonished.

Why not? Because the very reason Krsna appears in this world with His entourage, such as the eternally liberated gopis, is to liberate sincere practitioners, sadhakas like the rsi-cari gopis.

In short, to achieve His purpose, Krsna looks beyond the external means by which a devotee worships Him — friendship or lust — and accepts the essence, for which He is called *bhava-grahi janardana*.

This is how the romantic attitude of the rsi-cari gopis, full of pure devotion, gave them perfection. It is also how we get free from lust by remembering the gopis and their devotion for the Lord.

Narada gives a brilliant analogy to illustrate how dedicating one’s activities to Krsna’s service is the best way to be freed from the miseries that accrue from activities performed for selfish reasons.

*Amayo yas ca bhutanam jayate yena su-vrata*  
*tad eva hy amayam dravyam na punati cikitsitam*

**“O good soul! Does not a thing applied therapeutically cure a disease that was caused by that same thing?”**

Dear readers! Just as Krsna appears in person to liberate His devotees, He also appears in the form of our mantras: the diksa-mantras— particularly Kama-gayatri — and the Hare Krsna maha-mantra.

By chanting these mantras with the attitude, determination, and sincerity with which the Dandaka sages undertook their devotions, we too may attain perfection in the manner of the rsi-cari gopis.

Sridhara Svami comments,

**“Although this universe is distinct from the transcendental paraphernalia of Krsna’s pastimes, still, by remembering the moods of kama and sneha in Krsna’s devotees —**

**“Even today anyone in the universe can become free from the flux of the modes of nature.”**

Hearing these encouraging words, we can eagerly remember Krsna and chant puspabanaya dhimahi.

**\*\*\*\***

The part of Kama-gayatri that addresses the ultimate goal (prayojana) is presided over by Srila Gopinatha and contained in the words tan no ‘nangah pracodayat. In English, Srila Prabhupada renders this as follows:

**“Let us meditate on Him, being thus enthused by the transcendental Cupid.”**

With this translation Srila Prabhupada asks all his initiated followers to deeply ponder the original mellow of amorous love.

“The transcendental Cupid” in this sense refers not to Krsna, the Cupid of Vraja, but to the metaphorical Cupid who inspires Krsna to pursue the amorous impulses of eternal youth — in short, srngara-rasa.

Srila Prabhupada’s translation, then, is a request for us to learn, cultivate, and meditate upon the truths of the amorous mellow, how that mellow affects Krsna, and what role it plays in His pastimes.

As explained earlier, meditation is not a neutral act in which the meditator and the object of meditation remain forever apart, but one in which they naturally meet as master and servant.

If we are to meet Krsna and share His transcendental exploits in the amorous mellow, we must be eager to follow in the footsteps of the gopis, and in that way become worthy to receive such a privilege.

Gopinatha is ananga. He identifies Himself only as the gopis’ lord and lover. As such He is eager to increase the sphere of His influence, which is to say He is eager to welcome new gopis into His kingdom.

The word ananga also offers an admonition we should take seriously. Gopinatha’s realm is transcendental. It is accessible only by perfected souls, not by those who identify with their material bodies (anga).

Sastra informs us that we must pass from the stage of cultivation to ecstasy, and finally, like the rsi-cari gopis, we must be freed from all material taint before we are allowed to enter Krsna’s entourage.

From the example of the rsi-cari gopis one may rightly conclude that the diksa-mantras are effective for those who are practising devotees, or perfected practitioners, but not for those with spiritual forms.

By the practice of pure devotional service, including the chanting of our diksa-mantras like Kama-gayatri, we can at most attain awareness of our eternal spiritual forms, or identities, called svarupa-siddhi.

But the rsi-cari gopis show by example that svarupa-siddhi is not the final word in perfection; even after taking birth as a gopi in Vrndavana, further purification is needed to join Krsna’s entourage.

The final stage of perfection, in which one is freed from all material designations — gross or subtle-is called vastu-siddhi, attainment of which is beyond the range of mantras like Kama-gayatri.

We find no mention of the rsi-cari gopis chanting the Gopala mantra in Vraja, as they did in their previous births, but it is clear that they heard and chanted about Krsna with the nitya-siddha gopis.

From this we can conclude that although meditation upon the Kama-gayatri directs us to our eternal identity and destination, the need to chant it disappears before final spiritual attainment. Why is that?

Because chanting the Kama-gayatri mantra is a practice of Vaisnava brahmanas, a material bodily designation completely apart from one’s eternal spiritual form — for example, of a gopa or a gopi.

Lord Caitanya has taught that our spiritual identities are different from material designations like “brahmana" or “practising devotee,” designations to which the chanting of a mantra may condition us.

Our spiritual identities are solely dependent upon our being the property of Gopinatha as His servants or maidservants, absolute roles far distant from souls still bound to chanting mantras for perfection.

But, as already mentioned, the gopis — whether aspiring or perfected— always speak to one another about Krsna. They also sing His names, including the Hare Krsna maha-mantra.

Commenting on Narottama Dasa Thakura’s verse, *golokera prema-dhana hari-nama-sankirtana*, Srila Prabhupada says, ... the vibration of the Hare Krsna mantra is coming from Krsna’s planet, Goloka.”

Both Narottama’s verse and Prabhupada’s purport confirm that the Hare Krsna mantra is chanted not only in this world by practising devotees but also in Goloka by Krsna’s eternal companions.

In the same purport Srila Prabhupada also says, “And this chanting produces love of Krsna,” which indicates that chanting Hare Krsna is the means to go from svarupa-siddhi to vastu-siddhi.

In conclusion, we may accept that the meaning of ananga and Srila Prabhupada’s translation—“Let us meditate on Him being thus enthused by the transcendental Cupid” — are both a call to chant Hare Krsna.

If we chant Hare Krsna purely, without offence, and in tandem with worship of Kama-gayatri, we will be transported beyond the realm of liberation to the shelter of Lord Krsna’s lotus feet.

And in chanting Kama-gayatri, we must cultivate a mood that is likewise compatible with the Hare Krsna maha-mantra, the chanting of which should elicit the symptoms Narada once disclosed to Vyasa.

While speaking of his ascent to perfection and his eternal activities, Narada tells his illustrious disciple,

*pragayatah sva-viryani tirtha-padah priya-sravah*  
*ahuta iva me sighram darsanam yati cetasi*

**“The Supreme Lord Sri Krsna, whose glories and activities are pleasing to hear, at once appears on the seat of my heart, as if called for, as soon as I begin to chant His holy activities.”**

From this we should know that we achieve our eternal service to Krsna and fulfil the ultimate goal of life — of going back home, back to Godhead — by the grace of the Hare Krsna maha-mantra.

While chanting Kama-gayatri and the maha-mantra, I try to meditate upon Radha and Syama, and aspire to serve Them in this world — and in the world beyond — in a mood like that of a gopi.

Whether my aspirations are ever fulfilled depends upon Their grace coming to me through Srila Prabhupada. But simply thinking of Them and chanting Their glories reveals a joy greater than all others.

I conclude this glorification of the Kama-gayatri mantra by singing of the divine couple’s qualities and pastimes, which are known to a very few, rare souls in this world. I offer my obeisances to those rare souls.

**\*\*\*\***

Srimati Radharani is the ever-youthful embodiment of concentrated love for Krsna, and Her lithe golden form is the enchanting crest jewel in the tiara of Vrndavana’s youthful beauties.

Sriman Syamasundara’s monsoon-hued form of bliss showers nectar upon the cowherd girls’ cataka eyes, and His powerful, shapely limbs are the perfection of His own artistic skills.

Radha and Syama are both blackish inside and out and golden inside and out. We can understand this sublime riddle by analysing Their divine attributes and the meanings Their names possess.

Outwardly, Syama is blackish like a blazing sapphire, but turns golden from Gauri’s touch. And Gauri is a syama heroine because Her shining golden form is always comforting to the sense of touch.

Within, Syama is the blackish fountainhead of the amorous mellow and yet golden by constant thoughts of Gauri. Gauri is blackish by obsessive thoughts of Syama but golden by Her pure love.

Portrayals of Radha’s and Syama’s forms are endless. But our perception does not extend beyond this world, so Vaisnava poets must compare Their divine forms to worldly objects.

In such metaphors, poets and objects alike fail to touch upon the true splendour of the divine couple. I must confess I feel that same limitation in my present efforts at praising Radha and Syama.

As much eloquence as I can muster, my effort to praise Radha’s and Syama’s transcendental forms is like trying to illuminate a mountain with a lamp — the part seen is but a tiny fraction of the whole.

For example, is Syamasundara like a dark autumn moon? If He were, then why would the moon descend to earth? Is He like a black tamala tree? If He were, who has ever seen a tree walk or play the flute?

What I can safely say, to be sure, is that He is the son of Maharaja Nanda, and that He is constantly and deliriously searching for a certain gopi in the groves of Vrndavana forest.

Similarly, about Radha I can say that She is the seed of transcendental beauty, the goddess of all that is spontaneous and natural, and the gopi whose love has stolen Syamasundara’s mind.

Radha and Syama are both ananga — without material forms — and the complementary facets of pure love; He, the transcendental Cupid of Vraja, and She, the personification of selfless amorous passion.

The word ananga also implies that Radha-Syamasundara sometimes lose awareness of Their bodies as a result of the influence Their qualities have on each other. In other words, They faint.

The way Syama leans backwards, seeming to hesitate, as Radha leans forwards, dominating Their shared space with the wealth of Her attributes, gives the impression that Syama is about to faint.

To me, Srila Prabhupada’s translation of tan no ‘nangah pracodayat implies that I should meditate upon the Cupid-like effect Radha has on Syama, an enthusiasm that causes Him to swoon in bewilderment.

Of course, Radha also swoons under the influence of Krsna’s presence or absence. Lalita-devi once described such an event in a letter she wrote to Lord Krsna on behalf of her afflicted friend:

“My dear Krsna! Srimati Radharani has fallen unconscious on the ground, Her mind greatly agitated by Her separation from You. O enemy of Kamsa! You have now become a first-class politician.

“Therefore You are supposed to give relief to everyone. Then please consider the plight of Radha, or very soon You will hear of Her death. Maybe at that time You will lament, although now You are jubilant.”

Now let us hear a pastime in which Krsna fainted under Cupid’s influence. Recalling this pastime will help us be true to Srila Prabhupada’s instruction to meditate on our diksa-mantras.

One day Radha was seated with some dear friends by the scenic banks of the Yamuna, absorbed in thought of Krsna’s glossy form, more charming than a host of Cupids. Trembling in ecstasy, She said:

“O friends! My mind yearns to enjoy pastimes with Krsna. Like a cow dragged by a rope, my heart is forcibly drawn to Him. When will I see His furrowed eyebrows as He tries to frighten Me?

“When will I see His smiling lips as He tries to entice Me? When will I hear His resonant voice as He tries to hypnotise Me?” The gopislistened as Radha sang of Her many desires to please Krsna.

Restless with a longing only Her heart’s conqueror could satisfy, Radha left Her friends and Yamuna’s shore and, on the pretext of collecting flowers for Her beloved, entered Vrndavana forest.

Radha wandered amongst the trees, touching their leaves, caressing their trunks, and kissing their flowers. To a golden creeper climbing a tamala tree, She said, “Bless Me with your good fortune.”

She bowed before an ancient desire tree laden with the fruits of wisdom and begged, “Please be kind and do not forsake Me.” Every so often, She would absent-mindedly place flowers in Her basket.

Meanwhile, Govinda was herding cows nearby. While His weary friends rested beneath a banyan tree and the cows drank water from the Yamuna, Krsna also entered Vrndavana forest, alone.

With no purpose other than the desire to taste love, Krsna feasted His senses upon the natural beauty around Him. Suddenly, He noticed that for no apparent reason the forest had changed colour.

From their roots up, the trees had assumed a golden hue and their trunks, creepers, branches, leaves, flowers, and fruits were blazing with a soft splendour resembling the early morning sunshine.

The earth shimmered, the air glowed, and the clouds sparkled with an alluring radiance. Birds, peacocks, and other fowl sang in ecstasy,their beaks, claws, and plumage tinted gold.

Golden deer chewed golden grass and bumblebees flew wildly about,drinking dazzling golden rays. “What kind of exotic land have I wandered into?” thought Krsna. “Who has coloured Vraja like this?”

Golden shafts of light caressed Krsna’s blackish limbs and transformed His mind, agitating His heart and submerging it in the currents of an extraordinary stream of love.

Like iron filings to a magnet or cakora birds to the moon, Krsna was drawn to the loving feelings that coursed within Him. He murmured,“I must discover the cause of this mysterious state of affairs.”

Krsna followed the golden paths winding through the golden forest until He came to a clearing, where He saw a radiantly beautiful girl with a complexion of molten gold wandering about collecting flowers. Each flower She touched at once turned gold, as did the trees from which She plucked them. Krsna thought, “Here is the beautiful crescent moon born from the ocean of Vrsabhanu’s dynasty.

“The line of red sindura in the part of Her hair is just like a stream of nectar, Her braid is decorated with garlands of jasmine flowers, and Her large lotus eyes are restless like khaniana birds.”

Mesmerised by watching Radharani pluck flowers, Krsna whispered,

**“Her movements are more graceful than a young elephant’s, Her limbs more charming than the petals of a golden campaka flower.**

**“Her arms, tinkling with jewelled bracelets, are more delicate than Vrndavana’s tender creepers. Oh my! Beholding Her form intoxicates Me with the elixir of transcendental passion.”**

**\*\*\*\***

As Radha turned in His direction, the beauty that nourishes the gopis arrested Krsna’s mind. She was dressed in a red sari and beautifully decorated with ankle bells, necklaces,and glittering earrings.

Her broad hips, resounding with strings of jewel-bells, swayed like high-tide waves of ever-fresh nectar. And from the spotless moon of Her gently smiling face flowed a nectar ocean of love for Him.

Where is the one pure enough in heart to describe the pristine quality of Her form? Radha’s glistening red lips filled Krsna’s mind with longing, and Her fragrant smile defeated fields of aromatic flowers.

A dot of musk decorated Her rounded chin like a baby bee sitting on a ketaki petal, and a be jewelled pearl beautified Her stately nose as a dewdrop would a sesame flower. Seeing all this, Krsna reeled.

Intoxicated by divine passion, His mind bewildered by the vision that stood before Him, Krsna murmured to Himself, “Radhika’s form is the reservoir of all auspiciousness, Her limbs a festival of spotless love.

“The spirited cowherd girls are the ornaments in Her hair, the sweet aroma of Her body is like fragrant gold, and Her dancing, jewel-like fingernails are a festival for My eyes and heart.

“May this Radha, whose complexion is like kunkuma-smeared gold,whose beauty is sought after by Laksmi, who is the only shelter for My desolate heart, and who is indeed My very life, be merciful to Me.”

When Radha noticed Krsna on the opposite side of the clearing, the love that had forcefully painted Vrndavana with its hue shot from Her eyes to His, piercing Him with a thousand arrows of Cupid.

Krsna’s peacock-feather crown fell, His yellow garments loosened,and His flute slipped from His hand. Like a haunted statue, Krsna stared wide-eved at Radha. His lips quivered, “Ra, Ra! Ra, Ra— ”

Suddenly, like an uprooted tree, Krsna fell down unconscious. At this, Radha called out, “Visnu! Visnu!” Heart pounding, eyes frantic, She ran to Him and bathed His body with Her tears.

Krsna lay there like a bank of rain clouds run aground. His form glistened with the hue of a monsoon, His cloth shimmered like lightning,and His resonant voice rolled like thunder, “Ra! Ra!”

Radha was devastated. Yet as She gazed at the endless beauty of Her lover, She thought, “O heart! Be at case. Here lies motionless the one who rarely, and then momentarily, crosses the pathway of these eyes.

“Today kind Providence has arranged a private festival in which these eyes can gaze upon My Krsna’s beauty to their full satisfaction.

O heart! Do not hesitate. Grab this once-in-a-lifetime moment.”Radha’s unblinking eyes expanded as if to drink in the elixir of Krsna’s form. There was no one to hinder Her: no superiors, no gopas, no gopis, no modesty — no distracting stare from Krsna.

Nothing but Her eyelids could check Her from seeing His spotless beauty. Indeed, fate had truly smiled upon Her today. Respectful of Her heart’s desire, even the birds and beasts withdrew into the forest.

Only the trees — their rustling leaves echoing Radha’s sweet words —stayed there to shelter the divine couple. Srimati Radharani prayed,“O dark-complexioned Krsna! O abode of transcendental bliss!

“O My dearly beloved whose youthful pastimes embarrass the god of love! O Lord, the radiance of whose blissful smiling face eclipses the autumn harvest moon! O My very own black moon, Krsna.

“O flute player whose songs are an antidote for My pride! O Krsna,who stammers upon hearing the music of My vina! Although sporting in the rising waves of spiritual mellows, You are the origin of all bliss.

“O handsome prince of Vrndavana forest! Your face is like a blossoming lotus flower that has trapped the bee of My mind. I drink the sweetness of Your charming smile, but My thirst for it only grows.

“If I store this liquid sweetness in the honeycomb of My mind, I’ll become deranged, unable to perform My duties. What will become of Me? People will say, ‘How has this nice girl become so demented?’

“The lotus of Your face holds the two reddish petals of Your eyes,which also resemble a pair of restless bumblebees constantly intoxicated by drinking the honey of the gopis’ flower-like faces.

“Your form steals My patience and makes a mockery of My marital vows. The flute, Your lips’ wayward friend, plunders whatever halfhearted restraint I have left. Thus My family’s reputation is ruined.

“O Krsna! You are the incorrigible thief of Gokula and the matchless moon of Vraja! Your broad chest is a sapphire gateway to the palace of love, and Your pillar-like arms maintain the peace of Vrndavana!

“Like love’s playful puppet, You write Your name upon My feet. Your gait is like a baby elephant’s! Your limbs control every fibre of My being, and yet You tell Me, ‘Krsna is Your pet deer.’

“O beautiful dark gem! Your face, chest, waist, thighs, and feet are the pride of the creator, the opulence of Vrndavana forest, the happiness of the gopis, and the life of My life. Oh my! Oh my!

“Who has drawn these colourful designs on Your face with pigments of the gopis' love? Those designs arc an invitation to Your loving pastimes, which decorate Vraja as You adorn Your ornaments.”

Helpless like the sparrow who valiantly tried to empty the ocean to retrieve her eggs, Radha assailed Krsna’s beauty, but no matter how much She drank of His oceanic charms, they just expanded again.

Her hair stood on end, Her body shook, and tears cascaded down Her cheeks. Blinded by these ecstasies, Radha could no longer see the life of Her life, and thus bereft She felt Her heart break into pieces.

Feeling the reassuring warmth of sunshine on Her head, Srimati Radharani rallied Her senses, joined Her palms, raised Her tear streaked face to the sky, and offered prayers to Her worshipable deity.

In a quivering voice, Srimati Radharani said, “O divine eye of the world! O Lord who witnesses the deeds of all living beings! O My worshipable deity! If ever I have worshiped you with faith —

“If ever I have pleased you with an offering, if ever you have considered Radha your servant, then please fulfil just one desire. Let Me again see the beauty of My Syama — just one more time.”By the blessings of the sun god, Radharani ecstasies subsided and She became tranquil. Her mind and senses composed, She could again see Her beloved and savour the wonders of His form.

Who can say how much time passed? Is time privy to such wonders?Srimati Radharani feasted Her eyes upon Her lover, Sri Krsna, untilHis pastime potency gently prompted Her from within:

“O daughter of the king of Varsana! Krsna’s friends will soon arrive in search of Him. Don’t delay!” Radha accepted this advice, but before leaving She gently stroked Krsna with Her nectar-giving hand.

Sri Radha’s touch is prema itself. The influence of that prema entered Krsna’s heart,stirred His consciousness, forced His eyes to open, and revealed to Him Radha’s smiling face hovering just above His.

Radha’s face was like the sudden appearance of hope in a world of despair, the rising of the sun at the end of a winter night. It broughtcomplete joy to Krsna and caused His heart to surge with happiness.

Krsna’s eyes clamoured for an endless vision of Radha. He tried to speak, but His voice failed Him. And although Radha said not a word, Her incomparable smile said everything. Then She disappeared.

Krsna sat up and looked all around. He wondered, “Was I dreaming or did I really see Radha?” He rose to His feet and craned His neck to better search in all directions. “No one is here,” He thought.

As He hunted for Radharani in a nearby grove, without success,Krsna noticed the golden hue that had engulfed the forest was receding, and all things were rapidly regaining their natural colours.

Despondent, Krsna asked a nearby tree, “Where is She?” When He received no reply, Krsna thought, “By great fortune I found the priceless jewel I had been seeking, but now I have lost it again.”

Disheartened by Radha’s disappearance, Krsna’s heart sank into an abyss of sorrow. Could life have meaning without the life of His life?He fell to the ground, rolled about, and cried, “O Radha! O Radha!”

Just as milk is transformed into yogurt by the touch of something acidic, the wonderful happiness that had engulfed Krsna transformed into fearful anguish by the touch of Radha’s sudden departure.

Adrift in sorrow, Krsna tossed upon the earth like a fish on dry land,and His tears — after wetting His clothes and soaking the ground —seemed intent on making a pool in which He might flounder.

The forest creatures, anxious for their Gopala, surrounded Krsna and tried to console Him in their own ways. But they could not pacify Krsna. He remained a pitiful image of unrequited love.

Meanwhile, the cowherd boys awoke from their rest and called out Krsna’s name, but He was nowhere in sight. Distraught, they searched the directions like pirates desperately looking for hidden treasure.

Even the cows frantically searched for Krsna behind bushes and trees, but to no avail. Sridama finally found his friend rolling on the ground, crying out, “Where is She? Where is She?”

Saddened by his dear friend’s condition, Sridama took Krsna on his lap, stroked His face, and comforted Him with sweet words, “Dearest friend! The cows have wandered far away and the day’s end is near.

“We’re hungry, and Your mother is waiting. O Krsna! Our pastimes of herding cows are so full of bliss. But when You secretly leave us,and I find You so full of grief, O friend, I no longer desire to live!”

Krsna is controlled by love. As soon as He heard the emotional plea of His friend, He felt buoyed up, almost pacified. Taking the corner of Sridama’s garment, Krsna wiped the tears from His own face.

Still misty-eyed, Krsna again remembered His beloved Radha. Placing His hand in Sridama’s, He said, “I dreamt of a golden creeper with the most exquisite blossoms. It swayed in the breeze of love.

“Its flowers cast golden beams of nectar in all directions, but when agree dy bumblebee came to savour that creeper, it showered the bee with ambrosia tainted with poison, and then disappeared.”

Overcome by the ecstasy of love, Krsna gazed wild-eyed at His dear Sridama and said, “Friend! This creeper has created a burning, insatiable desire within Me. How can I ever be happy again?”

Sridama tightened his grip on Krsna’s hand to reassure Him and vowed, “Krsna! With friends like me, is anything impossible? I shall surely fulfil Your desire. You need not give it a second thought.”

He stroked Krsna’s hand and then pulled Him to His feet. Sridama consoled his friend, “Don’t worry. The bumblebee will again see that creeper. Have no fear. Come! Now we must go. Mother is waiting.”

**\*\*\*\***

I offer my respectful obeisances to the lover of the gopis, Gopijana-vallabha. He is Kamadeva, the transcendental Cupid, and Puspabana, the archer who shoots flower-arrows of His own personal virtues.

He is Ananga, the all-pervading Cupid, who is visible to the pure followers of Vraja’s gopis led by Sri Radha, and who is worshipped in this world by the kama-bija, the Kama-gayatri, and the maha-mantra.

He is called Krsna because — in addition to attracting the hearts and minds of His devotees, His friends, the gopis, Sri Radha, and even His own expansions like Maha-visnu — He also attracts Himself.

Because of His dark handsomeness He is called Syamasundara, and because a certain gopi’s feminine charms fully complement Him, She is called Radha. Together these two are called Radha and Syama.

May this Syamasundara, who stays always by the side of that Radha, be pleased with us and engage us in His service forever, both in this world and in Goloka Vrndavana — tan no ’nangah pracodayat.

Just as Radha and Krsna stay united in both the kama-bija and the maha-mantra, they stay side by side in New Vraja-dhama. May we always serve the happiness of Their eternal meeting.

By the worship of Radha and Syama we study the Vedas, and by dressing and decorating Them, we become familiar with every cryptic scriptural aphorism, such as *aham brahmasmi* and *tat tvam asi*.

In confirmation, Srila Prabhupada writes, “Even a neophyte devotee who simply engages in the worship of the Deity is understood to be in direct touch with the purport of Vedic knowledge.”

In this way, we fortunate souls who directly serve and worship Radha and Syama come to understand the truths of the Vedas. In so doing,the meanings of mantras like Kama-gayatri are revealed to us.

In addition to the direct meaning of the Kama-gayatri, another meaning is implied by the activities of Krsna, the transcendental flower-arrow-shooting Cupid. Whom is He targeting? The gopis!

Amongst the gopis, He is most attracted to Srimati Radharani. And so the Kama-gayatri glorifies and worships Her as the divine consort of Kamadeva, Anarigi, and the prime target of Puspabana.

Just as a description of the sun includes the sunshine, or a picture of a river includes the riverbed, Krsna mantras always imply the presence of Sri Radha. This holds especially true for Kama-gayatri.

In fact, just as Sri Caitanya-caritāmṛta says that Kama-gayatri is identical with the body of Sri Krsna, the Brhan-naradiya Purana says that the syllables of the mantra constitute Her form as well.

We may question whether interpreting the Kama-gayatri mantra to represent Radharani is truth or fanciful speculation. But a closer look at the mantra itself confirms this understanding to be factual.

Klim is the seed of Kama-gayatri. That seed is the combined form of Radha and Krsna, so how could Radha and Krsna not be manifest in the mantra? The seed is the source of all the attributes of the plant.

The first proclamation of the Kama-gayatri, kamadevaya vidmahe, tells us to learn the truths about Kamadeva. Those truths certainly include the fact that wherever He is, Sri Radha cannot be far away.

When Krsna sent Uddhava to Vrndavana with messages for the Vraja-vasis, Uddhava met Radha and Her friends in a secluded spot by the Yamuna and glorified Radharani with these words —

*Bibhrad vapuh sarva-patir yada yada tada tada tvam viditanurupini*

**“Whenever Lord Krsna, the master of all, assumes any form, You follow Him and assume a corresponding form as His companion.”**

Moreover, Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami explains that Srimati Radharani’s love gives Krsna the ability to taste the sweetness ofthe amorous mellow (*krsnera madhurya-rasa-asvada-karana*).

How, then, can Krsna display His pastimes as Cupid if Radha and the gopis are not present as the objects of His sublime love? How can a king act as a king without the presence of his subjects?

As devotees, we know Kamadeva to be the supreme potent and Radharani to be the supreme potency — two inseparable aspects of the one Absolute Truth. Where one is, the other is automatically present.

Thus, where Krsna is glorified in a mantra, Radha is either directly present, as in the case of Gopijana-vallabha in the Gopala mantra, or indirectly, as She is the shadow of Krsna’s names in Kama-gayatri.

In summary, this conclusion reminds devotees to include Srimati Radharani in their Krsna meditations while chanting Kama-gayatri. That kind of worship is the most complete and the most fruitful.

**\*\*\*\***

Let me now give a brief overview of chanting our initiation mantras, which reveal the truths of Radha and Krsna as They appear to us in different forms like the sun, Sri Guru,and Gauranga.

In this and the preceding chapters, three themes have been repeated in the context of our diksa-mantras: knowing, meditating upon, and dedicating our lives to serving Radha and Krsna and Their followers.

The Vraja-vasis serve the divine couple in five moods; nonetheless,our diksa-mantras direct us, unmistakably and emphatically, to the service of the gopis and to identifying with their loving mood.

This elucidation of our diksa-mantras is consistent with the singular contribution Caitanya Mahaprabhu made to Vaisnava theology, a contribution our acaryas have continued to emphasise to this day.

Amongst the verses that attest to Lord Caitanya’s unique invitation to aspire for amorous love, most significant is one of the Caitanya-caritamrta’s seed verses, composed by Svarupa Damodara Gosvami:

**“May the Supreme Lord who is known as the son of Srimati Saci-devibe transcendentally situated in the innermost chambers of your heart. Resplendent with the radiance of molten gold —“He has appeared in the Age of Kali by His causeless mercy to bestow what no incarnation has ever offered before: the most sublime and radiant mellow of devotional service, the mellow of conjugal love.”**

Indeed, from the perspective of this verse, the “radiance” (bhargah) spoken of in the Brahma-gayatri may be equated with the “radiant mellow” (ujjvala-rasam), the mellow of amorous love.

That the kama-bija prefixes four of the seven mantras we receive at initiation, speaks to that same ultimate goal. Other individual words of the mantras, such as gopijana-vallabha, do the same.

In light of these inherent directives in our mantras, the esoteric meanings of words like om, varenyam, and krsnanandaya, gain strong contextual support, leaving little room for alternate interpretations.

We who have accepted the renounced order of life chant an additional mantra, which explicitly enjoins us to aspire for and take shelter of the loving devotional mood of the cowherd girls of Vraja.

The clear message of our sannyasa-mantra synthesises both the explicit and implicit meanings of our diksa-mantras into an approach that leads us ever onward, towards the service of Sri Radha.

This is not to say, or to promote the idea, that all devotees have the same mood in our original identities in the spiritual world. Obviously,not everyone is a gopi in Goloka. Indeed, Lord Caitanya Himself says:

**“I shall personally inaugurate the religion of the age nama-sankirtana I shall make the world dance in ecstasy, realising the four mellows of loving devotional service.”**

Nevertheless, as has already been stated a few times in this work, all the rasas are nourished by the original (adi) rasa, the amorous rasa, which contains within it all the moods of all the other rasas.

In addition, Srila Prabhupada has also explained that Gaudiya Vaisnavas generally attain either fraternal or amorous perfection and that, of the two, perfection in amorous love is the most common.

We have analysed the diksa-mantras, one by one, but now let us consider them as a sequence. Taken together in that way, they form a cohesive whole that guides the chanter to a single goal.

Just as the verses of the Bhagavad-gita, taken in their entirety, direct us to surrender to Krsna in pure devotional service, the seven diksa-mantras direct us to serve Radha and Krsna as the gopis do.

And just as parts of the Gita, isolated from the whole, can be seen to promote spiritual acts other than pure bhakti, the diksa-mantras, taken on their own, lend themselves to multiple interpretations.

Thus devotees at various levels of Krsna consciousness, and attracted by various mellows, are all fortified by the grace of the Lord, who is present in the varied forms of divine sound in our diksa-mantras.

My purpose in giving detailed explanations of the words of the diksa-mantras was to encourage us to gain a fuller understanding of the gifts we have received so that we may worship them with due reverence.

Once we have developed great faith in our diksa-mantras, the meditations that accompany the explanations in this work may form a basis for our own absorption in this thrice-daily worship of the Lord.

As our bhajana matures, then, our chanting of the diksa-mantras assumes the form of a meditation on our worshipable Deity, our ista-deva, whom Caitanya Mahaprabhu told us we will see everywhere.

Then the seven mantras become as if one, of which the deva is Krsna, the sakti is Radha, and the rsi is the spiritual master. Meditating in this way, we may become absorbed in the bliss of our own aspirations.

At the stage of pure chanting, the truths of the mantras thus far described are supplanted by the mantras’ self-manifesting moods (bhavas), which then reveal Krsna’s qualities, form, and pastimes.

Situated in the mode of goodness and freed of all base qualities, as we practise conscientiously, we realise Krsna’s own words from the Bhagavad-gita:

**“Being freed from attachment, fear and anger**

**“Being fully absorbed in Me and taking refuge in Me, many, many persons in the past became purified by knowledge of Me—and thus they all attained transcendental love for Me.”**

My personal meditations on the diksa-mantras are meditations on Radha-Syamasundara — the source of my knowledge, the objects ofmy devotional practice, and the ultimate goal of my life.

Let me now recount a few ways in which I perceive my diksa-mantras in relation to Radha-Syama who, as my ista-devas, are my source of reference, my guiding light, and my only means of sustenance.

As I chant, I conceive of my diksa-mantras as a seamless continuum,of which Brahma-gayatri is the seed and the Guru mantras the soil,providing knowledge of eternal relationships — sambandha.

To me, the Gaura mantras represent the cultivation of devotional service, abhidheya, and the Gopala and Kama-gayatri mantras together define my life’s ultimate goal, giving the fruit of prayojana.

In this synthesis of mantras, I see the Brahma-gayatri as Radha-Syama’s dress, ornaments, and garlands; the Guru mantras, Their enchanting smiles; and the Gaura mantras, Their merciful glance.

I consider the Gopala mantra as Syamasundara’s selfsame form, and because it is Srimati Radharani’s presence only that causes Krsna to be Cupid, I relate to the Kama-gayatri as Her form.

When Radha is the subject of my meditation, then Brahma-gayatri is Her dress, the Guru mula-mantra Her smile, the Guru-gayatri Her glance, and the Gaura mula-mantra is Her all-embracing compassion.

The Gaura-gayatri is Her complexion and Her love, the Gopalamantra, the constant absorption of Her mind, and the Kama-gayatri is Her pastimes with Krsna in the company of the other gopis.

When I meditate exclusively on Syama, then the Brahma-gayatri is the flute with which He initiated Brahma, the Guru mula-mantra, His nectar-sweet smile, and the Guru-gayatri His eternal companions.

The Gaura mula-mantra is Syama’s concern for the suffering souls, Gaura-gayatri, His submission to Radha, the Gopala mantra, His sublime form, and Kama-gayatri, the fullest and freest show of love.

There is no difference between these diksa-mantras and Radha-Syamasundara Themselves, so I named the chapters dedicated to the study of the mantras after the presiding Deities of New Vraja-dhama.

O Radha! O Syama! Seeing You, serving You, touching You, thinking of You, and chanting Your names are the five airs that sustain my very life. Without these services I would surely die.

For me, every day begins the same — with the hope of securing Your mercy. As night gives way to dawn, dawn to day, and so on into night again, I anxiously wait and think, “Any minute now.”

Until You bestow Your mercy upon me, I will remain shackled by the chains of maya, deprived of divine service to You, far from the transcendental realm free of duality and ignorance, where You reside.

As a child in Motreal I would haunt a downtown Christmas display. Outside the store window, I rubbed and rubbed the glass, but due to the frost, all I could see were dim shadows inside. And I cried.

In the same way I aspire to see Your eternal Vrndavana pastimes,but the modes of nature keep blurring my vision. Try as I might, I see nothing but a few faint shadows within the recesses of my mind.

O Radha! O Syama! Until You lift this curtain of maya from my eyes, I will continue to press my face against the glass, and rub, and rub. And until I can enter Your personal service, I will cry, and cry.

When Krsna was leaving for Dvaraka, Kunti-devi bade Him farewell by offering profound prayers, revealing how Yogamaya conceals Him. She said, “Being beyond the range of limited sense perception —

“You are the eternally irreproachable factor covered by the curtain of deluding energy. You are invisible to the foolish observer, exactly as an actor dressed as a player is not recognised.”

Every day this foolish observer praises the good fortune that he can serve Your Deity forms and chant mantras exalting You. And daily he curses the impurities that prevent him from seeing You as You are.

O beloveds of New Vraja-dhama! If playing hide-and-seek with me gives You pleasure, so be it. Then I am happy. But if one day You are pleased to reveal Yourselves to me, my happiness will increase.

O Radha — Syama! Such are the waves in the ocean of aspiration that pound against the breakwater of my false ego: the flow of my thoughts as I chant my diksa-mantras, and in the time between chanting them.

May those sacred mantras, which are not different from You, and which therefore light up this dark creation, illuminate the hearts of those who repeat them with faith, devotion, and love.

## Appendix 35

**The Glories of Caitanya Mahaprabhu**

(This appendix was inspired by the Gaura mula-mantra and Gaura-gayatri mantra, which are elaborated on in chapter thirty-three.)

**Introduction**

Srila Prabhupada once mentioned that the Srimad-Bhagavatam verse beginning krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam is the most conclusive sastric statement substantiating Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s identity as Sri Krsna. For that reason I have selected it as the basis for this appendix glorifying Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

I have tried to intersperse knowledge of Lord Caitanya’s identity, teachings, and pastimes throughout Nava-vraja-mahima, but these mentions of Lord Caitanya are modest compared to the book’s extensive narrations about Lord Krsna. At one point that sharp contrast was troubling to me; in tact, that concern eventually led me to approach Dayala-Nitai and Vijaya-Gauranga in Their Budapest temple to reveal my heart to Them. In so doing I somehow felt Their reassurance that in spite of the imbalance, the book was going in the right direction. At the same time, I felt a very clear order that, in some part of the book, I should write in more detail about Caitanya Mahaprabhu. The first half of chapter thirty-three and this appendix are both meant to serve that purpose. It is my earnest prayer that these attempts somehow fulfil Their Lordships’ desire.

**The Symptoms of Lord Caitanya**

The verse that begins krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam appears in the Eleventh Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam, wherein we find a conversation between Maharaja Nimi and the nava-yogendras. In that conversation Maharaja Nimi asks, “In what colours and forms does the Supreme Personality of God headappear in each of the different ages, and with what names and by what types of regulative principles is the Lord worshipped in human society?”

The sage Karabhajana answers the king by first describing the incarnation of the Lord in Satya-yuga, the golden age, and the process for worshipping Him. Karabhajana then continues, giving the same information for each age. In reference to Kali-yuga, the age of quarrel, he begins, “In Kali-yuga also people worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead by following various regulations of the revealed scriptures. Now kindly hear of this from me."

According to Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, this verse indicates that in Kali-yuga the Vaisnava scriptures known as the Pancaratras are very important. Why? Because they prescribe Deity worship and the chanting of the Lord’s holy names — the means for deliverance in this age — as alternatives to study of the Vedas, practice of yoga, or performance of sacrifices, which are all beyond the limited abilities of people today.

Then the sage says srnu, “please hear,” indicating that he is aboutto say something important. Karabhajana says:

*krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam sangopangastra-parsadam*  
*yajnaih sankirtana-prayair yajanti hi su-medhasah*

**“In the Age of Kali, intelligent persons perform congregational chanting to worship the incarnation of Godhead who constantly sings the names of Krsna. Although His complexion is not blackish, He is Krsna Himself. He is accompanied by His associates, servants,weapons, and confidential companions.”**

This verse is quoted numerous times throughout Sri Caitanya-caritamrta to authenticate Lord Caitanya’s divinity.

When Lord Caitanya first arrived in Puri, Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya brought the Lord to live with him in his own home. At that time Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya and his nephew Gopinatha Acarya had an argument about the divinity of Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Sarvabhauma pointed out that according to the scriptures there is no incarnation of the Lord in Kali-yuga. Therefore, to consider Lord Caitanya the yuga-avatara was tantamount to wishful thinking. But Gopinatha countered his uncle’s argument by quoting this verse, which declares that the Lord does in fact appear in this age, but in a “covered” incarnation.

Later, after Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya had been transformed into a pure Vaisnava and a stalwart devotee of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, he and Maharaja Prataparudra were once watching the devotees from Navadvipa performing sankirtana. This time it was Sarvabhauma who quoted this verse to substantiate that what the devotees were doing was the yuga-dharma prescribed in scripture.

And in Varanasi, during the two months in which He personally instructed Sanatana Gosvami, Caitanya Mahaprabhu also quoted this verse. While describing the incarnations for the four ages, the Lord cited this verse to describe the incarnation appearing in Kali-yuga. Upon hearing this verse, Sanatana Gosvami-who, as the former prime minister to the king of Bengal, was extremely intelligent — set a trap for Lord Caitanya, hoping that the Lord would disclose His own divinity.

Sanatana Gosvami asked, “How can I understand who is the incarnation for this Age of Kali?”

Caitanya Mahaprabhu replied, “By two symptoms — personal characteristics and marginal characteristics.” The Lord continued, “Bodily features, nature and form are the personal characteristics (svarupa-laksana) and knowledge of His activities provides the marginal characteristics (tatastha-laksana).”

Sanatana Gosvami then tried to ensnare Lord Caitanya. He asked,“The colour of the personality in whom the characteristics of the Lord are found is yellowish and His activities include distributing love of God and chanting the Lord’s names. Is that correct?”

But Caitanya Mahaprabhu recognised the bait Sanatana had set for Him. Unwilling at that time to reveal Himself as the Supreme Absolute Truth, the Lord replied, “O Sanatana! You must give up your intelligent tricks.”

It is apparent from Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s statements that the bodily features of an incarnation, including His complexion, are amongst His distinguishing personal characteristics. In this verse, the words tvisakrsnam and — depending on one’s interpretation — krsna-varnam, describe the Lord’s complexion, while the rest of the verse describes His activities.

**The Meaning of Tvisakrsnam**

The most common interpretation of tvisakrsnam, is “having a lustre (tvisa) that is not black (akrsnam).” But this interpretation requires further explanation because scripture states that the incarnation for Kali-yuga is blackish. Rupa Gosvami confirms this in His Laghu-bhagavatamrta:

*kathyate varna-namabhyam suklah satya-yuge harih*  
*raktah syamah kramat krsnas tretayam dvapare kalau*

**“In Satya-yuga the incarnation is said to be white and to be known by the name Hari. In the Treta-, Dvapara-, and Kali-yuga the incarnations are red, blue, and black respectively.”**

This verse characterises the general convention governing the Lord’s appearance. There are, however, exceptions to the rule. One well-known exception is when greenish Lord Ramacandra appearsin Dvapara-yuga. Another exception is when the Lord appears in Kali-yuga with a golden rather than a blackish complexion, a truth secretly revealed to Nanda Maharaja by the great sage Garga Muni. The verse in which Garga Muni mentions the yellow colour Krsna sometimes assumes in this age is nearly as significant as the krsna-varnam verse. Indeed, Srila Prabhupada almost always quotes these two verses together. Analysis of that verse spoken by Garga Munican therefore be said to be equally important.

After some truly extraordinary events had occurred in the life of his newborn son, the king of Vraja decided that it was time for the baby’s name-giving ceremony. But to avoid Kamsa’s suspicions,the ceremony was performed in secret. Under the shelter of one of Nanda’s barns, Garga Muni performed the appropriate rites and then described Krsna’s past lives. The sage said:

*asan varnas trayo hy asya grhnato nuyugam tanuh*  
*suklo raktas tatha pita idanim krsnatam gatah*

**“Your son Krsna appears as an incarnation in every millennium. In the past, He assumed three different colours — white, red, and yellow— and now He has appeared in a blackish colour, \[In another Dvapara-yuga, He appeared (as Lord Ramacandra) in the colour of suka, a parrot.\] All such incarnations have now assembled in Krsna.”**

According to chronology, the white and red colours in this verse clearly refer to the incarnations in Satya- and Treta-yuga. But the yellow and black incarnations require clarification, which comes from an understanding of the tenses expressed and implied by Garga Muni.

The colours white and red clearly refer to the past, while blackish refers to the present by use of the word idanim, meaning “and now.” But, in that context, placing the yellow incarnation between past and present is confusing. At one time the Hamsa incarnation unravelled this mystery for Lord Brahma by pointing out that, according to the rules of Sanskrit grammar, idanim may also mean the “near future.”

Correlating the tenses to the colours, the verse says,

**“...Just as your son appeared previously in white and red incarnations, He now appears in a blackish form and will appear in Kali-yuga \[in the “near future”\] with a yellow colour.”**

Krsna Himself confirms His appearance as the Supreme Personality of Godhead in both Dvapara- and Kali-yuga when He says, sambhavamiyuge yuge: “I Myself appear, millennium after millennium.” According to Brahma, this should be taken to mean that Krsna appears as Himself in the millennium in which He spoke these words to Arjuna and then as Gauranga in the millennium following that.

Thus the special feature of this Kali-yuga is that the incarnation for this age is not black but yellow, and when Karabhajana says the Lord is akrsna, “not black,” he means that the Lord is pita, “yellow.”Srila Prabhupada confirms, “Akrsna may be red, may be white, but this akrsna means pita, yellow. That is because red and white were in the Satya- and Treta-yuga, therefore here akrsna means the yellow.”

Although krsna-varnam means ‘‘repeating the syllables krs and no” — and therefore refers to someone who is always uttering the name Krsna, a marginal characteristic of Lord Caitanya — there is another meaning. According to Srila Prabhupada, varnam also means “the lustre of one’s original identity.” For the living entities, that lustre is the golden colour of bliss, and for Krsna it is the blackish colour of enjoyment.

Thus the phrase krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam indicates that while Lord Caitanya displays a golden complexion emblematic of Srimati Radharani’s divine love, within Himself He remains blackish Krsna, the enjoyer of the amorous mellow. This is confirmed by Srila Jiva Gosvami as follows:

*antah krsnam bahir gauram darsitangadi-vaibhavam*  
*kalau sankirtanadyaih sma krsna-caitanyam asritah*

**“I take shelter of Lord Sri Krsna Caitanya Mahaprabhu, who is outwardly of a fair complexion but is inwardly Krsna Himself. In this Age of Kali He displays His expansions \[His angas and upangas\] by performing congregational chanting of the holy name of the Lord.”**

Herein Jiva Gosvami implies that Krsna is called “Gaura” because in Kali-yuga He appears with a golden complexion; with that golden complexion He has covered His usual mood of enjoyment with the mood of loving devotional service. This unique situation is the result of Lord Caitanya’s always thinking of Krsna. Or, in Srila Prabhupada’s words, “By thinking of Lord Krsna, He always overlapped Krsna.”

Lord Caitanya’s golden complexion is not just wonderful to look at, it also has a special potency: simply by seeing that unparalleled lustre one’s heart is purified of the tendency to think and act in sinful ways. In place of those unwanted tendencies, one’s heart is filled with the most indescribable treasure- love of Godhead. In the beautiful words of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, tvisa indicatesa transcendental bodily lustre with the potency to distribute Krsna’s names by its beauty alone and to impart Krsna consciousness upon everyone who beholds it.

But despite the evidence given by scriptures and acaryas, some unfaithful persons still raise arguments against Lord Caitanya’s divinity. For instance, Srila Prabhupada says that some members of the Nimbarka sampradaya argue that Lord Caitanya is not God but a great devotee.

Sri Caitanya-caritamrta also recounts how Vallabha Bhatta once insisted directly to Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu that the yuga-dharma could not possibly be the chanting of Sri Krsna’s names. Vallabha’s assertion directly implied that if the process of chanting Hare Krsna could not be the yuga-dharma, Lord Caitanya could not be the yuga-avatara. To support his claim, Vallabha Bhatta argued that it is not befitting for a living entity to call his master by name.

Of course, Caitanya Mahaprabhu soundly defeated Vallabha. The Lord said, “My dear Vallabha Bhatta! You do not know religious principles. Actually, the first duty of a chaste woman is to carry out the order of her husband.

“The order of Krsna is to chant His name incessantly. Therefore one who is chaste and adherent to the husband, Krsna, must chant the Lord’s name, for she cannot deny the husband’s order. Following this religious principle, a pure devotee of Lord Krsna always chants the holy name. As a result of this, he gets the fruit of ecstatic love for Krsna.”

Some people accept the superstition that Krsna will appear in this age in His original blackish form. In fact, when Caitanya Mahaprabhu was visiting Vrndavana, some gullible people mistook the movements of a night fisherman and his lamp on the Yamuna to be Krsna dancing on Kaliya’s hoods.

When the Lord’s own servant was attracted by the spectacle, Caitanya Mahaprabhu said, *krsna kene darasana dibe kali-kale*: “Why would Krsna appear in Kali-yuga?” Thus the Lord implied that,according to scriptures, Krsna appears personally in Dvapara-yuga but never in Kali-yuga. He does appear, however, in a disguised colour and form. That is tvisakrsnam.

And there arc others who argue, on the strength of Garga Muni’s verse asan varnas trayo, that because Gaura and Krsna are listed alongside amsa-avataras, They too are incarnations of the Supreme Lord, not the Supreme Lord Himself. But, because of the non sequential order of the names, this argument must be false.

First the incarnations of the past are listed, followed by those of the future (pita) and the present (krsnatam). The natural sequence —past, present, and then future — is changed here to emphasise a point. The point that the blackish incarnation is most important, and in fact is the source of the other three, is stressed by putting it at the end of the sequence. Moreover, Srimad-Bhagavatam establishes the eternal supremacy of Krsna when Suta Gosvami says:

*ete camsa-kalah pumsah krsnas tu bhagavan svayam*  
*indrari-vyakulam lokam mrdayanti yuge yuge*

**“All of the above-mentioned incarnations are either plenary portions or portions of the plenary portions of the Lord, but Lord Sri Krsna is the original Personality of Godhead. All of them appear on planets whenever there is a disturbance created by the atheists. The Lord incarnates to protect the theists.”**

Garga Muni also intends to emphasise that the Dvapara-yuga avatara is not an amsa; on the contrary, He is svayam-bhagavan, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Still, one may ask, “Although Krsna is certainly unique, is not Lord Caitanya also unique?” Of course He is. But because His identity is a secret and because in this verse He will appear in the future — in other words, He is yet to be established — Lord Caitanya is not glorified here explicitly like Krsna is. As already described in the explanation of the words sambhavami yuge yuge, Krsna Himself establishes the glories of His appearance as Caitanya Mahaprabhuand — in character with His way of expressing secrets, and of Lord Caitanya’s mysterious identity — Krsna speaks of His golden incarnation in a secretive way.

Finally, some people insist that it is not possible for one person to have many colours, especially colours that are so different, like gold and black. Thus, they conclude that a black Krsna could not possibly be gold and that Gaura is therefore not the Supreme Lord.

Srila Prabhupada replies to this materialistic speculation by citing Baladeva Vidyabhusana, who gives the example of a crystal and how it appears to change colour when turned in the light: “Sometimes yellow! Sometimes white! Sometimes red!” Similarly, according to how He is seen through the light of devotion, Krsna sometimes looks black, yellow, or red — indeed any colour.

The aforementioned explanations of tvisa-krsnam were based on dividing the compound into tvisa (“lustre”) and akrsna (“not black”). Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, however, divides it another way: tvisa (“lustre”) and krsna (“black”). In this interpretation, although in Kali-yuga the Lord possesses a golden complexion, to the eyes of advanced devotees like Ramananda Raya, Lord Caitanya’s complexion is blackish. In other words, they see Him as Syamasundara.

And in yet another interpretation, Srila Prabhupada also extracts krsnam from tvisakrsnam and invokes Jiva Gosvami's antah krsnam verse to indicate that Lord Caitanya is of a blackish complexion inside, although golden outside.

In summary, as Caitanya Mahaprabhu told Sanatana Gosvami, tvisakrsnam describes the incarnation for this age by His personal characteristics — a golden complexion concealing a beautiful, blackish (Syamasundara) form that is occasionally visible to His advanced associates. Those personal characteristics unmistakably indicate Lord Caitanya Himself.

**The Meaning of Krsna-varnam**

Whereas tvisakrsnam exclusively delineates Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s personal characteristics, krsna-varnamcan be taken to describe both His personal and marginal characteristics. I shall describe both, starting with the former.

The word varna means “category,” just as it does when Krsna sayscatur-varnyam, “the four divisions \[categories\] of human society.” In the same way, at the beginning of the krsna-varnam verse, sage Karabhajana says that the incarnation for Kali-yuga is in the same category as Krsna: that incarnation is not only visnu-tattva but also svayam-bhagavan, Krsna Himself, the source of all incarnations.

Srila Prabhupada confirms that the words *krsna-varnam*— even if applied to differently coloured forms of the Lord — are synonymous with the phrase “Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

In speaking with Sanatana Gosvami, Caitanya Mahaprabhu personally identifies the Kali-yuga avatara as being Krsna Himself:

*'pita'-varna dhari' tabe kaila pravartana*  
*prema-bhakti dila loke land bhakta-gana*  
*dharma pravartana kare vrajendra-nandana*  
*preme gaya ñṁṣṛloka kare sahkirtana*

**“In the Age of Kali, Lord Krsna assumes a golden colour and, accompanied by His personal devotees, introduces hari-nama-sankirtana, the chanting of the Hare Krsna mantra.
