By this process He delivers love for Krsna to the general populace. Lord Krsna, the son of Nanda Maharaja, personally introduces the occupational duty of the Age of Kali. He personally chants and dances in ecstatic love, and thus the entire world chants congregationally.”**

Another explanation of krsna-varnam is given by Sridhara Svami in His commentary on the same verse. In that commentary varnam istaken to mean “colour.” The acarya says that the Lord’s incarnation appears as bright as a blue sapphire.

Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura elaborates on why Sridhara Svami also describes Lord Caitanya as being of blackish complexion. The Thakura’s answer relates to the Lord’s childhood pastimes in which He played on a pile of rejected cooking pots. Because the pots were black with ash and smoke young Nimai’s limbs were smeared with soot, and so He looked blackish just like Krsna.

The demon Sankhacuda is also described as being krsna-varnam,of a blackish colour. When the gopis saw his hideous features — tall, black, with the face of a tiger — they became afraid and called out to Krsna. This is another example of krsna-varnam describing a blackish complexion.

If one takes varna to mean “colour” in the phrase krsna-varnamtvisakrsnam, an interesting paradox arises: that the yuga-avatara is both blackish and golden. This paradox is easily resolved, however, by the accepted principle that the second statement in such a phrase or thought is given prominence over the first. The resolution, then, is in the fact that the yuga-avatara's complexion is blackish, but that blackish complexion has been covered by a golden one. And so once again we return to Jiva Gosvami's verse, antah krsnam bahirgauram: “Lord Sri Krsna Caitanya Mahaprabhu... is outwardly of a fair complexion but is inwardly Krsna Himself.”

Vaisnava acaryas have frequently asserted and elaborately explained that krsna-varna refers to one of the Lord’s marginal characteristics: He constantly chants Krsna's names. The foremost of these explanations also connects krsna-varna to Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mission — another marginal characteristic of the Lord — with the words, yajnaih satikirtana-prayair: “...intelligent persons performcong regational chanting to worship \[Lord Krsna\]...” This explanation of krsna-varna as a marginal characteristic of Sri Caitanya is also found in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta:

*‘krsna’ ei dui varna sada óanra mukhe*  
*athava, krsnake tinho varne nija sukhe*  
*krsna-varna-sabdera artha dui ta pramana*  
*krsna vinu tanra mukhe nahi dise ana*

**“The two syllables krsna are always in His mouth; or, He constantly describes Krsna with great pleasure. These are two meanings of the word krsna-varna. Indeed, nothing else but Krsna issues from His mouth.”**

Succinctly expressed, this definition of krsna-varnam is found in the words, krsnam varnayati, iti krsna-varnam: “One who always chants ‘Krsna’ is called krsna-varnam."

It should go without saying that when Caitanya Mahaprabhu chants Krsna's names it is not mechanical, hut rather an outpouring of His spiritual ecstasies born of deep meditation on Krsna’s form,qualities, and pastimes. In the words of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, “He personally chants the holy names while remembering His own ecstatic spiritual pastimes.’ Caitanya Mahaprabhu takes the role of a devotee, and as a devotee He is spontaneously attracted to the remembrance of Sri Krsna’s pastimes in Vrndavana. This fact is supported by the numerous references to the Lord’s spiritual absorption found in books like Sri Caitanya-caritamrta.

In the words of Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami cited earlier, “chanting Krsna” means speaking about Krsna as well as chanting His names — both are sankirtana. Lord Caitanya exemplified these two meanings most dynamically while touring South India.

During His travels from village to village the Lord would chant the names of Krsna, and when villagers or fellow travellers saw and heard Him, they would also chant “Hari! Hari!” After nightfall, Caitanya Mahaprabhu would often stay in the houses of brahmanas. After an evening meal, He would speak about Krsna into the early hours of the morning. After a little rest He would again continue His travels. In this way Lord Caitanya displayed the two meanings of krsna-varnam.

Srila Prabhupada adds that krsna-varnam also refers to “one who inspires others to chant the names of Krsna.” In other words,those who are inspired to chant Krsna’s names as Lord Caitanya did are a source of inspiration for others to do likewise; and this process goes on perenially. Thus krsna-varnam refers not only to Caitanya Mahaprabhu but also to His sankirtana mission. Or in Srila Prabhupada’s words, “Krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam indicates that prominence should be given to the name of Krsna. ”What better way to give prominence to Krsna’s names than to induce as many people as possible to chant them? By following this transcendental principle under the guidance of Lord Caitanya, the whole world will be merged in bliss.

**\*\*\*\***

The last explanation for krsna-varnam is “one whose name is Krsna.”

Kesava Bharati selected “Krsna Caitanya” as Nimai Pandita’s sannyasa name because the Lord reminded every-one of Krsna, and in that way awakened (caitanya) them from the dream-like state of material illusion. Krsna-varnam thus indicates “one whose name includes the two syllables krs-na".

In summary, krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam describes Lord Caitanya’s most prominent personal and marginal characteristics, both of which introduce additional characteristics that describe Him further — for example, His associates, the sacrifice by which He is worshipped, and those who worship Him. Again, the inner meaning of the words krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam may be gleaned by studying the aforementioned example of a crystal, which, depending on the angle from which one perceives it, appears to be various colours. It even takes on the colour of nearby objects, but its essence never changes.

In the same way, Krsna assumes various forms and colours in response to the relationships His worshippers desire to have with Him. By doing so, Krsna pleases His devotees, fulfils His own mission,and relishes bliss. And yet, like the crystal, Krsna stays essentially unchanged.

Unlike conditioned souls, the Lord’s body is eternal, so He never changes. He emphasises this fact by reminding Arjuna, “I can remember all My births but you cannot.” But the example of a crystal cannot adequately explain Krsna’s potencies. The crystal only appears to be a different colour as long as it remains in contact with a certain object. But Krsna can assume an unlimited number of characteristics according to His desire and yet simultaneously and inconceivably remain unchanged.

Amongst all these forms, on very rare occasions Krsna forgoes His godly opulences and adopts the characteristics of His own devotee, appearing with a golden complexion and transcendental form that are the very emblems of loving service. And while that form of Krsna,known as Gaura, may potentially display the service mood of any devotee, He is especially attracted to the Vraja-vasis’ unique and spontaneous devotion, the apex of which is found in the amorous love of Srimati Radharani.

Pupa Gosvami writes:

*aparam kasyapi pranayi-jana-vrndasya kutuki*  
*rasa-stomam hrtva madhuram upabhoktum kam api yah*  
*rucam svam avavre dyutim iha tadryam prakatayan*  
*sa devas caitanyakrtir atitaram nah krpayatu*

**“Lord Krsna desired to taste the limitless nectarean mellows of the love of one of His multitude of loving damsels \[Sri Radha\], and so He has assumed the form of Lord Caitanya. He has tasted that love while hiding His own dark complexion with Her effulgent yellow colour. May that Lord Caitanya confer upon us His grace.”**

To grasp what it means that Krsna can assume the mood of His own servant, we must understand something of the dynamics of love between the devotees and the Lord — between the lovers and their beloved. As the object of His devotees’ love (visaya), the Supreme Lord’s mood (bhava) is to perpetually extract from that relationship the taste of transcendental mellows, especially the blackish coloured mellow of amorous love. One’s nature is shown by the permanent mood one displays, and by definition that nature influences one’s entire being. Thus Krsna’s nature as the supreme enjoyer, especially of amorous love, colours His body blackish.

But when Krsna adopts the nature of His devotee, specifically of Srimati Radharani, the golden colour of Her loving devotion tinges His blackish body yellow, and thus He is known as Gaura. In this way the name Gaura stands not only for the colour of the Lord’s complexion but also for the mood within His heart; in fact, the name Gaura also reflects the truth that whoever beholds His beauty shares in His transcendental mood of loving devotion.

What inspires Krsna to take up Srimati Radharani’s mood? One day, while strolling in a garden, Krsna happened to see Himself reflected on the surface of a shiny pillar. Deeply enchanted by His own beauty, Krsna was eager to enjoy Himself just as Srimati Radharani does. But to do so He would have to adopt Her devotional mood (bhava).

How did Krsna, once inspired, assume the mood of Radharani? One night, while They were resting in a grove, Krsna waited until Sri Radha had fallen into deep sleep. He then entered Her heart and, like a thief (hari), borrowed Her devotional nature for His own use. Svarupa Damodara Gosvami describes Krsna’s adopted sentiment and complexion with the words *radha-bhava-dyuti-suvalitam naumi krsna-svarupam*: “I bow down to Him, who has manifested Himself with the sentiment and complexion of Srimati Radharani although He is Krsna Himself.”

These are but a few of the ecstatic truths to be mined from the words krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam. The ocean of transcendental truths and mellows found within Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu knows no shore. To span that ocean with one’s words or intellect is simply not possible. And yet in spite of my limitations the powerful tides that govern the oceanic glories of the Lord impelled me to attempt the impossible. As the author of Sri Caitanya-caritamrta writes:

*gaura-lilamrta-sindhu — apara agadha*  
*ke karite pare tahan avagaha-sadha*  
*tahara madhurya-gandhe lubdha haya mana*  
*ataeva tate rahi' caki eka kana*

**“The ocean of the pastimes of Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu is immeasurable and unfathomable. Who can have the courage to measure that great ocean? It is not possible to dip into that great ocean, hut its sweet mellow fragrance attracts my mind. I therefore stand on the shore of that ocean to try to taste but a drop of it.”**

**The Meaning of Sangopangastra-parsadam**

The second line of the krsna-varnam verse, Sangopangastra-parsadam, describes the entourage of the yuga-avatara. It comprises four words, each of which is described in a number of different ways by our acaryas. Yet those varied meanings have three points of convergence in common: (1) Lord Caitanya’s bodily features, (2) Lord Caitanya’s associates, and (3) Krsna’s holy names.

There are numerous explanations of the words in Sangopangastra-parsadam. These explanations vary not only from one acarya to another, but even a single acarya — like Srila Prabhupada for example— may give multiple meanings for the same word or words.

There is no defect in such variety. Indeed, in a similar way, the practice of referring to Lord Caitanya or Krsna by the names of Their expansions, or the expansions of Their expansions, is amongst the first of many transcendental topics addressed in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta. It is specifically to resolve any confusion this topic may cause that Krsnadasa Kaviraja encourages his readers not to shy away from contemplating transcendental controversies but to speculate responsibly, on the basis of scriptural statements. He says, “A sincere student should not neglect the discussion of such conclusions, considering them controversial, for such discussions strengthen the mind. Thus one’s mind becomes attached to Sri Krsna.”

How can these terms — anga, upanga, and so on — be described in so many different, sometimes apparently contradictory, ways?

The key to understanding this paradox lies in understanding how Lord Caitanya Himself can be seen — and thus described — in so many different ways. The devotional vision of the Lord’s pure devotees that allows for this multiplicity of understanding is described by Rupa Gosvami as follows:

*ata eva puranadau kecin nara-sakhatmatam*  
*mahendranujatam kecit kecit ksirabdhi-sayitam*  
*sahasra-sirsatam kecit kecid vaikuntha-nathatam*  
*bruyuh krsnasya munayas tat-tad-vrtty-anugaminah*

**“According to the intimate relationships between Sri Krsna, the primeval Lord, and His devotees, the Puranas describe Him by various names. Sometimes He is called Narayana; sometimes Upendra (the younger brother of Indra, king of heaven; and sometimes Ksirodakasayi Visnu. Sometimes He is called the thousandhooded Sesa Naga, and sometimes the Lord of Vaikuntha.”**

When, therefore, Lord Caitanya is referred to as svayam bhagavan, His anga is Lord Nityananda. When He is described as Narayana, then His anga is Advaita Acarya. And when He is called Ksirodakasayi Visnu, His anga is Sesa. In the same way, the words upanga, astra, and parsada can be variously identified according to one’s perspective in regard to Lord Caitanya. And while all our acaryas are in full knowledge of Lord Caitanya’s supreme divinity, they invoke the principle of “unity in diversity” by describing the Lord differently according to the manifestations of His transcendental power. By studying the diverse ways in which our acaryas interpret sangopangastra-parsadam, we acquire a broader view of its source, Caitanya Mahaprabhu, and thus better understand a portion of His unlimited glories.

As mentioned earlier, the three main ways in which the members of the Lord’s entourage are described relate to His features, associates,and names. Now we shall begin by describing the Lord’s features.

Jiva Gosvami calls anga the exquisite bodily limbs of Caitanya Mahaprabhu, upanga the sandalwood ornaments and paste that decorate those limbs, astra the weapon of His extraordinary beauty that disarms and subdues even the most ardent of atheists, and parsadas the demigods like Brahma (who appears as Haridasa Thakura) and Siva (who appears as Advaita Acarya) who, attracted by the Lord’s beautiful form, take the opportunity to live with Him.

Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura cites a rule of Sanskrit grammar called karma-dharaya, by which an anga can also be called upanga, astra, and parsada. Thus Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s limbs (anga), can also be called ornaments (upanga), for they are more enchanting than the finest jewellery, indeed they are the supreme ornament of the three worlds. Moreover, like weapons (astra), the sight of the Lord’s limbs defeats the demonic mentality of Mayavadis, and in that capacity they also serve as the Lord’s own associates, parsadam.

These descriptions bring to mind the sage Vaisampayana’s own descriptions of Caitanya Mahaprabhu and the Lord’s personal qualities as they appear in the Visnu-sahasra-nama:

*suvarna'varno hamango varangas candanangadi*  
*sannyasa-krc chamah santo nistha-santi-pardyanak*

**“In His early pastimes He appears as a householder with a golden complexion. His limbs are beautiful, and His body, smeared with the pulp of sandalwood, seems like molten gold. In His later pastimes He accepts the sannyasa order, and He is equipoised and peaceful. He is the highest abode of peace and devotion, for He silences the impersonalist non-devotees.”**

Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s bodily complexion is the colour of mahabhava, which can only be compared to gold that has been melted a hundred thousand times. His singing voice is very sweet, but when He calls out to Krsna in ecstasy His voice is deep and rumbling like thunderclouds. And while these characteristics surely distinguish Him from ordinary living entities, His extraordinary bodily dimensions clearly reveal Him to be in the category of Visnu.

The unique bodily proportions of the Supreme Lord are technically referred to as nyagrodha-parimandala. By these measurements Lord Caitanya’s height and breadth are equal to one another; that distance is four cubits by the measure of His own forearm, roughly seven and a half feet. Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s arms are also said to reach to His knees, and when He raises those arms in êirtana His towering height is further magnified. Although the Lord’s limbs are ornaments in themselves, they are decorated with cooling, aromatic sandalwood bangles and armlets, and anointed with sandalwood pulp.

Lord Caitanya’s facial features are even more unique and attractive than His limbs. The comers of His lotus eyes reach towards His ears, and those eyes are always moist with tears of divine love. His ruby red lips are soft with compassion and affection, for they constantly vibrate the names of Krsna. If one were to compare His face to the moon, then one would have to say that His face is a “spotless moon” because it is both luminous and also free of any mark or imperfection.

Earlier it was said that Lord Caitanya’s bodily beauty is His weapon. But the word “weapon” invokes thoughts of harm or aggression. The Lord’s beauty is neither dangerous nor threatening, however. To the contrary, it is alluringly attractive and thus inspires surrender in everyone who sees it, filling those fortunate souls with the desire to become the Lord’s enthusiastic servants.

Just as Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s beauty subdues devotees and nondevotees alike, Lord Nityananda’s bodily features and Advaita Acarya’s loud shouts of ecstasy are also found in Lord Caitanya’s armoury. And furthermore, besides the supreme brahmastra known as the Lord’s beauty, there is an equally powerful weapon called gaura-daya, Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

**\*\*\*\***

Those brief descriptions of Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s personal characteristics were given in the context of sangopangastra-parsadam. The following section will again examine sahgopangastra-parsadam, but in terms of Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s associates.

In relation to the Lord’s associates, our acaryas generally define the four words contained within sangopangastra-parsadam as indicating the following categories: sa-anga — the Lord’s associates; upanga— servitors; astra — weapons; parsadam— confidential companions.

Srila Prabhupada, in one very significant translation of the krsna-vamam verse, directly equates these four categories contained within the phrase sangopangastra-parsadam with the members of the Panca-tattva. He says, “One should worship the Supreme Personality of Godhead who appears in this Age of Kali with His associates as the Panca-tattva: the Lord Himself and His associates Nityananda Prabhu, Sri Advaita Prabhu, Sri Gadadhara Prabhu, and Srivasa Thakura.”

Identifying the four categories of the Lord’s associates with the members of the Panca-tattva is consistent with the most fundamental ways in which Caitanya Mahaprabhu appears to inaugurate the sankirtana movement. After introducing the fourteen seed verses of his Sri Caitanya-caritamrta and elaborating upon them in six chapters, Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami also describes the Panca-tattva, sometimes referred to as “Lord Caitanya in five features.” Kaviraja Gosvami says,

**“These five tattvas incarnate with Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu, and thus the Lord executes His sankirtana movement with great pleasure.”**

Kaviraja Gosvami then describes Lord Caitanya’s five features in the following famous verse:

*panca-tattvatmakam krsnam bhakta-rupa-svarupakam*  
*bhaktavataram bhaktakhyam namami bhakta-saktikam*

**“Let me offer my obeisances unto Lord Sri Krsna, who has manifested Himself in five as a devotee, expansion of a devotee, incarnation of a devotee, pure devotee, and devotional energy.”**

Correlating the five members of the Panca-tattva as they are identified in this verse with the phrase krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam sangopangastra-parsadam suggests the following synonyms: krsna-varnam tvisakrsnam — Caitanya Mahaprabhu Himself (bhakta-gana); saanga—\[along with\] Nityananda Prabhu (svarupa); upanga— Advaita Acarya (bhaktavataram); astra — Srivasa Thakura (bhakta); parsadam— Gadadhara (bhakta-sakti). In this way the krsna-varnam verse can be understood in terms of the worship of the Panca-tattva by the chanting of Krsna’s holy names. In Srila Prabhupada’s words, “In this age an intelligent person worships the Panca-tattva by the method of chanting the Hare Krsna maha-mantra and, if possible, distributing prasada”

Once, in a lecture in Sridhama Mayapur, Srila Prabhupada identified Sangopangastra-parsadam with the first verse of Sri Caitanya-caritamrta:

*vande gurun isa-bhaktan isam isavatarakan*  
*tat-prakasams ca tac-chaktih krsna-caitanya-samjnakam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto the spiritual masters, the devotees of the Lord, the Lord’s incarnations, His plenary portions, His energies and the primeval Lord Himself, Sri Krsna Caitanya.”**

In the context of sangopangastra-parsadam this verse yields the following synonyms: anga — His plenary portions; upanga — the Lord’s incarnations; astra — the spiritual masters and the devotees of the Lord; and parsadam — His energies. Naturally, the primeval Lord Himself, Sri Krsna Caitanya, is the origin and the controller of these four categories.

Obviously, this interpretation is identical with comparisons to the Panca-tattva already given, and thus the two interpretations helpus to understand how the different categories in Lord Caitanya’s entourage are commonly identified, although those categories are sometimes described differently according to the devotional sentiment of whoever is describing them.

Locana Dasa Thakura gives similar descriptions of Krsna’s entourage and the entourage of Lord Caitanya. The Thakura says that in Krsna’s pastimes His servants, like Balarama, are His angas and thatthose same servants or maidservants become Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s angas or upangas. The Thakura also says that Krsna’s bodily ornaments appear as Lord Caitanya’s upanga servitors. Moreover, Krsna’s supreme weapon (astra), the Sudarsana cakra, appears as Lord Caitanya’s weapon in two forms: directly as the cakra with which He threatened Jagai and Madhai, and in a concealed form as His sonnyasa-danda with which He made everyone bow down before Him. Finally, Krsna’s parsada associates, like Prahlada and Narada, appear in disguised forms as servitors of Lord Caitanya.

There arc numerous other interpretations of Sangopangastra-parsadam given by acaryas like Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami, Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami, Rupa Gosvami, Vrndavana Dasa Thakura, and Srila Prabhupada. Those interpretations show great diversity; in fact, individual acaryas sometimes give multiple interpretations. For example, Gadadhara Pandita is sometimes described as an upanga and a parsada, Advaita Acarya as an anga and an upanga, and Srivasa Thakura as an upanga and a parsada.

In addition, the general picture given by acaryas is that Lord Caitanya’s angas and upangas (limbs and ornaments) are His soldiers in the sankirtana movement and their astras (weapons) are manifest in their preaching. To this Srila Prabhupada adds that all preachers— meaning those devotees spreading Lord Caitanya’s message today — are also weapons of the Lord and are counted as part of His entourage. Finally, the Lord’s parsadas — confidential associates like Jagannatha Misra and Mother Saci — while not as active in His mission, are integral to His loving pastimes.

Most acaryas define astra (weapon) as the chanting of Krsna’s holy name. Clearly this is the activity of the yuga-avatara mentioned in the krsna-varnam verse, and when performed in sacrifice it cleanses and purifies the performers, as well as the place where it is performed.

Unlike Krsna, who killed demons with His weapons, Lord Caitanya kills the demonic propensities in the hearts of conditioned souls through the agency of the Hare Krsna maha-mantra.

In the same way that a weapon is only of value when properly wielded and carefully aimed at its target, the sankirtana of Krsna’s names must be wielded with compassion in order to hit its target — a universal increase in Krsna consciousness. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura advises us to comprehend the essence of the Lord’s mision as jive doya krsna nama: “Chant and distribute the holy names with compassion for all living entities.”

While describing associates like Nityananda Prabhu as the Lord’s weapons, Srila Prabhupada reveals the underlying emotion that makes those associates effective — their compassion upon conditioned souls. “He has not taken any astra. His weapon is His associates. Nityananda Prabhu was going to kill Jagai and Madhai, not by weapon, but by mercy. They were drunkards, woman-hunters, flesh-eaters, and they hurt Him. Still, He delivered them. This is the astra. By argument, by philosophy, by entreating, by flattering, His associates are giving Krsna consciousness.”

And while it is understood that the devotees who appeared with and preached alongside Caitanya Mahaprabhu are the Lord’s eternal associates — Srila Prabhupada asserts, citing Narottama Dasa Thakura — that those devotees who presently preach the message of Lord Caitanya are somehow, even in the conditioned state, elevated to the same eternally liberated platform of the Lord’s associates by the mercy of the holy name. Of course, if devotees conditioned by the modes of nature give up preaching, they lose that extraordinary empowerment. Narottama Dasa Thakura has said:

*gaurangera sangi-gane nitya-siddha korimane*  
*se yaya vrajendra-suta-pasa*

**“Simply by understanding that the associates of Lord Gauranga are eternally free from material contamination, one can immediately be promoted to the transcendental abode of Lord Krsna.”**

Elsewhere Srila Prabhupada explains that devotees achieve such an elevated status through the mercy of Lord Caitanya’s weapons — Lord Nityananda and Krsna’s names:

**“...all the preachers of Krsna consciousness movement, they are maha-bahu, strongly armed by the weapons of Krsna. They cannot be defeated.”**

It should he noted once again that this special protection awarded to conditioned souls engaged in spreading the message of Lord Caitanya will last only as long as they remain under the shelter of the Lord, His associates, and His sankirtana movement. If such devotees think they have become liberated in their own right and leave the Lord’s shelter, they will fall down. Thus Narottama Dasa Thakura advises that we pray to Lord Nityananda, rakho ranga-caranera pasa: “Please keep me in a corner of Your reddish lotus feet.”

Thus the chanting of Krsna’s names — as Gauranga’s astra — has multiple meanings. It refers directly to the power the holy name has to dispel the sinful temperament of people, to bless them with love for Krsna, to empower them to further distribute the holy name themselves, and to protect those devotee preachers from the dangers they encounter while engaged in Lord Caitanya’s mission.

In summary, sangopangastra-parsadam is akin to a king who leaves his palace for a distant land. When the king travels he does not go alone; his ministers, secretaries, bodyguards, royal insignia, and even his family members accompany him. Similarly, when Lord Caitanya leaves Goloka for the material world He comes along with His associates, servants, weapons, and confidential companions.

**The Meaning of Yajnaih Sankirtana-prayair**

The third line of the krsna-varnam verse, yajnaih Sankirtana-prayair, consists of three words: yajnaih — sacrifice; sankirtana — congregational chanting of the holy names;and prayair — mainly.

The entire verse was spoken in reply to King Nimi’s question:

*sri rajovaca*  
*kasmin kale sa bhagavan kim varnah kidrso nrbhih*  
*namna va kena vidhina pujyate tad ihocyatam*

**“In what colours and forms does the Supreme Personality of Godhead appear in each of the different ages, and with what names and bywhat types of regulative principles is the Lord worshipped in human society?”**

In response to the question “By what process is the Lord worshipped?” (*kena vidhina pujyate*), Karabhajana Muni says that in Kali-yuga the Lord is worshipped “mainly by the sacrifice of the congregational chanting of Krsna’s names” (*yajnaih sankirtana-prayair*).

Since the king was enquiring about the type of worship current in each age, the word yajnaih is here synonymous with yuga-dharma. The sage therefore informed him that the yuga-avatara— *krsna-varnamtvisakrsnam* (“Krsna appearing with a golden complexion”)—is worshipped by the *yuga-dharma— sankirtana* (“the congregational chanting of Krsna’s names”).

To naive persons sankirtana may resemble a common festival involving music, singing, and dancing. And so to indicate that sankirtana is just as spiritual as the processes of meditation or fire sacrifice prescribed in previous yugas, Karabhajana precedes mention of sankirtana with the word yajnaih, meaning “by this sacrifice,” and also follows it with the word yajanti, indicating an act of worship. Finally, use of the word su-medhasah (“intelligent people”) eradicatesany lingering doubt that sankirtana is spiritual in nature by insisting that only sober persons participate in it. Thus sankirtana is qualified as the authorised sacrifice with which to worship Lord Caitanya.

In his description of kirtana (“chanting the Lord’s glories”) Jiva Gosvami defines sankirtana:

*yadi saksad eva mahat-krtasya kirtanasya sravana-bhagyam na sampady-ate tadaiva svayam prthak kirtaniyam iti.*  
*gana-sakty-abhave tat srnoti tad-anumodanam bahubhir militva kirtanam sankirtanam.*

**“When one is not engaged in hearing the Lord’s glories from a great soul, one may personally engage in chanting the Lord’s glories. If one has no power to chant the Lord’s glories, then one may simply hear. Chanting the Lord’s glories becomes a source of great pleasure for the chanter. When many are assembled together to hear and chant the Lord’s glories the chanting is called** ***sankirtana*****.”**

Srila Prabhupada, borrowing from Jiva Gosvami’s definition, dubbed *sankirtana* “congregational chanting.”

Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura describes *kirtana* (“chanting”) in connection to *Sravana* (“hearing”). The *acarya* says that *Sravana* entails receiving, through the ears, Krsna’s names and descriptions of His form, qualities, and pastimes. Once received, repeating these transcendental vibrations constitutes *kirtana.*

*Kirtana* is distinguished from *japa*—the silent repetition of Krsna’s names — by being audible to others and also by including repetition of the Lord’s glories. It is said to be a more spiritually effective process for conditioned souls than *japa* because it actively engages the external senses and thus more easily controls the mind.

Although the loud chanting of Krsna’s names is available to everyone, *kirtana* does not immediately deliver its full benefits to those who are unaware of the truths of Krsna’s names, who do not approach those names with due respect, or who commit offences to Sri Krsna’s names.

Serious practitioners therefore apply themselves to understanding the truths that relate to Krsna’s names, especially those truths described by Lord Caitanya in His only recorded work, *Sri Siksastaka*, and expounded upon by realised souls like Srila Prabhupada.

Inspired by their study and hoping to approach Krsna’s names in the proper mood, serious devotees endeavour to cultivate the four limbs of humility as delineated in Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s *Sri Siksastaka:*

*trnad api sunicena taror api sahisnuna*  
*amanina manadena kirtaniyah sada harih*

**“One should chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking himself lower than the straw in the street. One should be more tolerant than the tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige and ready to offer all respects to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly.”**

Those desiring to reap the rewards of kirtana must also avoid the ten offences to the holy names, offences that proliferate like disease in the stagnant water of careless, inattentive chanting. As long as these obnoxious offences persist, the heart remains covered by dirty things and the stage of pure chanting is far away. Without chanting purely,one cannot achieve the result of this sacrifice — love for Krsna.

Sometimes beginners propose that it is better not to chant at all than to chant offensively, but this is faulty logic. No doubt, offenceless chanting is superior to offensive chanting. And it is within our power to avoid offences. But if we cannot immediately free ourselves of offences scriptures still encourage us to continue chanting. As the Padma Purana says, namaparadha-yuktanam namany eva haranty agham/ avisrant-prayuktani tany evartha-karani ca: “Even if in the beginning one chants the Hare Krsna mantra with offences, one will become free from such offences by chanting again and again.”

According to the karma-kanda section of the Vedas, a sinner must perform some form of atonement to counteract his misdeeds. Vaisnava acaryas, however, recommend that those who sin continue their devotional practices in a mood of sincere repentance. In the case of offending the holy name, especially by offending Vaisnavas, the sin is so grievous one can only be absolved of it by obtaining forgiveness directly from the offended Vaisnava. Only then will the holy name forgive the offender. Ritualistic acts of atonement will not bring restitution if the holy name has been offended. To show that only the holy name can forgive an offender, Srila Prabhupada quotes the following verse spoken by Daksa after offending Lord Siva:

*yo ’sau mayavidita-tattva-drsa sabhayam*  
*ksipto durukti-visikhair viganayya tan mam*  
*arvak patantam arhattama-nindayapad*  
*drstyardraya sa bhagavan sva-krtena tusyet*

**“I did not know your full glories. For this reason, I threw arrows of sharp words at you in the open assembly, although you did not take them into account. I was going down to hell because of my disobedience to you, who are the most respectable personality, but you took compassion upon me and saved me by awarding punishment. I request that you be pleased by your own mercy, since I cannot satisfy you by my words.”**

To help free us from the tendency to commit offences and to invoke the mercy of the holy names, before chanting Hare Krsna we chant the Panca-tattva maha-mantra — *sri-krsna-caitanya prabhu-nityananda sri-advaita gadadhara srivasadi-gaura-bhakta-vrnda*. We can also offer prayers to the holy name. For example, Srila Prabhupada suggests the following beautiful verse:

*nikhila-sruti-mauli-ratna-mala-*  
*dyuti-nirajita-pada-pankajanta*  
*ayi mukta-kulair upasyamanam*  
*paritas tvam hari-nama samsrayami*

**“O Hari-nama! The tips of the toes of Your lotus feet are constantly being worshipped by the glowing radiance emanating from the string of gems known as the Upanisads, the crown jewels of all the Vedas. You are eternally adored by liberated souls such as Narada and Sukadeva. O Hari-nama! I take complete shelter of You.”**

Although the chanting of the Hare Krsna maha-mantra is the most exalted spiritual activity, no specific preparation or qualification isrequired for one to take it up. As Caitanya Mahaprabhu says, the process of chanting Hare Krsna is free of any “hard and fast rules” (*tatrarpita niyamitah smarane na kalah*). And, as great authorities like Devahuti have confirmed, chanting the holy names is superior to the performance of austerity, penance, study of the Vedas, or a host of other sacrifices. Indeed, one who has achieved competence in chanting Hare Krsna is considered to have already performed all these ritualistic practices.

Still, we should not receive Krsna’s holy names from just any source. The source must be authorised. For one to be authorised, he or she must possess pure, unadulterated faith in the holy names,unlike the Mayavadis, whose faith is corrupted by the thought that by chanting they can become one with Krsna. We should hear the holy name from a devotee with pure faith. And amongst devotees with pure faith, those who have fully realised the truths and mellows of the name are best.

Kirtana's glories are truly endless. What I have written here can hardly be considered even an introduction to them. Yet it provides us with a basis for a more elaborate discussion on sankirtana, which has already been defined as *bahubhir militva kirtanam sankirtanam*: “When many come together to do kirtana, that is sankirtana."

**\*\*\*\***

Srila Prabhupada explains that the prefix sam in sankirtana means “complete,” implying that sankirtana is the most complete form of kirtana. In fact, scriptures say that sankirtana is a hundred times more potent than silent recitation of the holy names.

The foundation for sankirtana s glories was revealed in a conversation between Caitanya Mahaprabhu and Haridasa Thakura. One day Lord Caitanya asked the Thakura, “How can trees, plants, insects, and other living entities be delivered from material bondage?”

Haridasa replied, “By Your loud sankirtana living entities both moving and non-moving have been delivered. Moreover, when Your followers perform sankirtana all over the world, the living entitiesdance in ecstasy.”

From this conversation it is clear that, in addition to the benefit its direct participants incur, sankirtana is exalted because it brings transcendental sound vibration to others without restriction. Whoever — and whatever — hears the transcendental vibration of sankirtana from devotees’ lips is benefitted. Thus the chanters are doubly blessed by the holy name — first, by chanting it themselves, and second, by sharing it with others. Actually, this is the unique feature of the sacrifice for this age. Sankirtana, in that way marked by selflessness — be it conscious or even inadvertent — is therefore complete and also most conducive for cultivating unmotivated devotion.

Because of that the sankirtana-yajna is indispensable to the lives of serious spiritual seekers, and Srimad-Bhagavatam deems it an integral part of any social or religious function:

*na yatra vaikuntha-katha-sudhapaga*  
*na sadhavo bhagavatas tadasrayah*  
*na yatra yajnes'a-makha mahotsavah*  
*suresa-loko ’pi na vai sa sevyatam*

**“An intelligent person does not take interest in a place, even in the topmost planetary system, if the pure Ganges of topics concerning the Supreme Lord’s activities does not flow there, if there are not devotees engaged in service on the banks of such a river of piety, or if there are no festivals of sankirtana-yajna to satisfy the Lord \[especially since sankirtana-yajna is recommended in this age\].”**

Sankirtana is meant to accompany not only public gatherings, all aspects of devotional service should be performed in conjunction with sankirtana.

Jiva Gosvami writes:

*yadyanya bhaktih kalau kartavya tada kirtanakhya bhakti-samyoga-naivaityuktam. yajnaih sankirtana-prayair yajanti hi su-medhasa iti.*  
*tatra casvatantram eva nama kirtanam-atyanta-prasastam.*

**“Even though in the Age of Kali one may perform the \[other\] eight branches of bhakti, nevertheless, one must perform kirtana as the principal way of advancing in sadhana-bhakti. The Srimad Bhagavatam says, yajnaih sankirtana-prayair yajanti hi su-medhasah, ‘Those who are intelligent in this Age of Kali will worship the Lord through the performance of sankirtana.' On account of this it is determined from an objective point of view that of all processes of devotional servicenama-sankirtana is the best.”**

Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura summarises this by saying,“Therefore, although in the Age of Kali other processes of devotional service are to be performed, they must always be accompanied by the chanting of the holy names." When worshipping the Lord in His Deity form (arcana), for example, devotees are meant to chant Hare Krsna, and so arati is always accompanied by kirtana. The word prayair, meaning “mainly,” implies both this necessity for the other eight limbs of bhakti to be performed in faithful conjunction with sankirtana, and sankirtana's pre-eminence over and above other acts of devotion.

This emphasis on nama-sankirtana is not solely due to its importance as the yuga-dharma. There is another reason: The influence of Kali-yuga is such that even the most qualified souls cannot properly execute austerity, meditation, Deity worship, sacrifice, or other spiritual processes and thus cannot derive benefit from them. But when these acts are accompanied by sankirtana, then the holy name itself compensates for all discrepancies, awarding the benefits one can expect both from performing sankirtana and from performing those spiritual practices that it has accompanied.

Devotees sometimes misguidedly try to perfect activities like Vedic sacrifice or elaborate Deity worship but neglect the serious cultivation of the holy name. Yet Srimad-Bhagavatam leads a myriad other scriptures in proclaiming that the perfections once obtained by sustained and concentrated efforts in meditation, sacrifice, or Deity worship are now available quickly and easily by chanting Hare Krsna:

*krte yad dhyayato visnum tretayam yajato makhaih*  
*dvapare paricaryayam kalau tad dhari-kirtanat*

**“Whatever result was obtained in Satya-yuga by meditating on Visnu, in Treta-yuga by performing sacrifices, and in Dvapara-yuga by serving the Lord’s lotus feet can be obtained in Kali-yuga simply by chanting the Hare Krsna maha-mantra.”**

But sankirtana-yajna, as the yuga-dharma, is not to be equated with the yuga-dharmas of previous ages; that is to say, all yuga-dharmas are not interchangeable, coming to prominence or fading into obscurity because of the forward progress of the ages. In Srila Prabhupada’s words, “Of all the sacrifices mentioned in the Vedic literatures, the performance of sankirtana-yajna is the best.” Moreover, the author of Sri Caitanya-caritamrta gives a stern warning to those who do not recognise the sublime superiority of sankirtana over other Vedic sacrifices:

*koti asvamedha eka krsna nama sama*  
*yei kahe, se pasandi, dande tare yama*

**“One who says that ten million asvamedha sacrifices are equal to the chanting of the holy name of Lord Krsna is undoubtedly an atheist. He is sure to be punished by Yamaraja.”**

The truth of the matter is that nama-sankirtana is not just a religious process: it is the essence of all religion. By just once chanting Krsna’s name without offence the chanter obtains the results of all kinds of charity, vows, austerity, sacrifice, scriptural study, and mantra recitation. Moreover, by chanting Krsna’s name one can attain liberation from this world, and beyond liberation, love for Krsna.

**\*\*\*\***

The names of Krsna deliver one from suffering and place one directly at the lotus feet of the Lord. For that reason they are called taraka-brahma nama, the absolute truth in the form of sound capable of granting deliverance. For the novice, the Lord’s names may be compared to a bud that slowly unfolds into a beautiful flower as the chanter increases in proficiency; they gradually reveal the Lord’s form, qualities, pastimes, and ultimately His abode — Goloka Vrndavana.

The holy names of Krsna are so powerful that even a materialist who chants them — and then even carelessly or in jest — will gradually develop pure devotion to Krsna. If we compare that materialist with a sincere brahmana who, during a fire sacrifice, accidentally mispronounces one syllable of a Vedic mantra and yields the wrong result, then one can see sankirtana’s clear advantage over other spiritual processes.

And whereas sacrifices are generally performed at prescribed times and places and require specific ingredients, the sacrifice of sankirtana can be performed anywhere, at any time, and requires only that one raise one’s voice to praise the Lord.

Because Lord Caitanya and His associates are the source of the benefits offered by the sankirtana-yajna it is advisable to chant before a Deity of Lord Caitanya or a picture of the Panca-tattva. And since sankirtana means to broadcast Krsna’s holy name in public, outside temple walls, devotees often chant on the streets of their own towns and villages. Public chanting fully embodies the spirit of sankirtana, invokes the full mercy of Caitanya Mahaprabhu, and ultimately delivers the entire world.

When Lord Caitanya was in the town of Ramakeli, the Muslimking became curious about the Lord’s sankirtana movement and His influence over others. This curiosity brought concern to the devotees’ hearts, but when Caitanya Mahaprabhu became aware of His servants’ fears He reassured them that nothing and no one could stop His sankirtana movement for it is by His will that it is slowly spreading throughout the world.

The Lord said, “I have descended to this world to begin the sankirtana movement and deliver everyone. I do not care whether one is a Yavana, demon, outcast, untouchable, woman, or sudra. In this age everyone will chant My names and receive devotional service that the demigods, sages, and siddhas yearn to attain.”

The Lord then spoke this famous prophesy:

*prthivite ache yata nagaradi grama*  
*sarvatra pracara haibe moranama*

**“In every town and village, the chanting of My name will be heard.”**

Roughly four-hundred and fifty years after Lord Caitanya made this prediction, Srila Prabhupada took sankirtana beyond the boundaries of India. In order to fulfil the mission of Caitanya Mahaprabhu, he established and empowered a spiritual institution, the International Society for Krsna Consciousness.

We conclude our discussion of the word sankirtana by offering the following meditation: Just as Lord Yajna personally appeared from fire sacrifices during Treta-yuga to accept the oblations of brahmanas, in Kali-yuga, Lord Caitanya appears wherever His devotees perform nama-sankirtana.

In Srimad-Bhagavatam, the Pracetas tell us:

*yatra narayanah saksad bhagavan nyasinam gatih*  
*samstuyate sat-kathasu mukta-sangaih punah punah*

**“The Supreme Lord, Narayana, is present amongst devotees who are engaged in hearing and chanting the holy name of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Lord Narayana is the ultimate goal of sannyasis, those in the renounced order of life, and Narayana is worshipped through this sankirtana movement by those who are liberated from material contamination. Indeed, they recite the holy name again and again. "**

In his purport to this verse, Srila Prabhupada writes,

**“Whenever there is congregational chanting of the holy names of the Lord, Gaura Narayana, the Supreme Personality of Godhead in His incarnation as Lord Caitanya, immediately appears and is worshipped by sankirtana-yajna. ”**

It is interesting to note that in establishing sankirtana as the yuga-dharma, Karabhajana Muni implies — with the word prayair (“mostly”) — that other activities may complement the chanting of Krsna’s names in this age. In previous ages there was no such concession made for combining spiritual activities.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu personally revealed to His mother that the sankirtana movement includes Deity worship alongside the chanting of Sri Krsna’s holy names:

**“Birth after birth you are My mother. Again, in this birth, I have come to begin the sankirtana movement, and so I have become your son. My appearance in this world is twofold: I appear as the arca-murtiand as the holy name. In this way I appear upon the tongue in the form of sound, and as the Deity made from the elements drawn from the earth. In both these forms I am worshipped, and in these two features I have appeared in order to spread the sankirtana movement.”**

But one may wonder why, if Krsna’s names are all-powerful, is there any need for continuing the process of Deity worship practised in Dvapara-yuga? Caitanya Mahaprabhu has included worship of the arca-vigraha as a part of His sankirtana movement in order to help us overcome offences in chanting and thereby enable us to chant purely. Therefore, even though chanting Hare Krsna has prominence over all other spiritual activities, worship of the Deity in the templeis strongly recommended, especially for neophytes. This is one of the meanings of prayair.

Another meaning is that the other seven limbs of devotion— especially hearing devotional literature like Srimad-Bhagavatam — should also be practised as much as possible. Actually, as in the case of Deity worship, regular reading and hearing of transcendental literature is essential in helping devotees to achieve liberaion from material influences. It is well known that Srila Prabhupada repeatedly warned against neglecting this process of hearing: “All the devotees connected with the Krsna consciousness movement must read all the books that have been translated (the Caitanya-Caritamrta, Srimad-Bhagavatam, Bhagavad-gita, and others); otherwise, after sometime, they will simply eat, sleep, and fall down from their position. Thus they will miss the opportunity to attain an eternal, blissful life of transcendental pleasure.”

Another important meaning of prayair— that all limbs of bhakti should be accompanied by sankirtana— has already been explained. In this regard, the following advice of Jiva Gosvami has been repeated in different ways by our previous acaryas: “Even though in the Age of Kali one may perform the above eight branches of bhakti, nevertheless, one must perform kirtana as the principal way of advancing in sadhna-bhakti”

Sankirtana is also performed in public places for the benefit of all. And along with chanting Hare Krsna, conditioned souls may be further attracted to devotional service by receiving krsna-prasada or by hearing public presentations on Bhagavad-gita and Srimad-Bhagavatam. These two practices — prasada distribution and philosophical discourse — are also implied by the word prayair.

Srila Prabhupada was especially fond of large-scale prasada distribution; indeed, he instructed us to “...distribute prasada to millions of hungry people hankering for spiritual emancipation.” He insisted that all public functions include distribution of this sanctified food and that every visitor to every temple be given the opportunity to eat Krsna’s remnants at any time of the day.

In summary, while the adjective prayair confirms that nama-Sankirtana is the means for attaining spiritual perfection in this age, Sankirtana should be accompanied by Deity worship, philosophical discourse, and distribution of sacred food.

**The Meaning of Yajanti Hi Su-medhasah**

The final line of the krsna-varnam verse—yajanti hi su-medhasah — means “intelligent persons certainly worship.”

The word yajanti (“they worship”) specifically addresses Maharaja Nimi’s question, referring back to the object of worship described in the verse’s first and second lines (Caitanya Mahaprabhu), the method of worship described in the third line (nama-sahkirtana), and the “intelligent persons” engaged in worship who are indicated by *hi su-medhasah*.

In Satya-, Treta-, and Dvapara-yugas people were naturally religious, and so Karabhajana does not overtly say, “they worship.” But because King Nimi and others may be doubtful that in this age of sin anyone would take religious practice seriously the sage affirms, “Yes,in Kali-yuga, at least those people who are intelligent will worship the golden Lord and His associates by the sacrifice of chanting the holy names.”

The word yajanti has common and unambiguous usage in scripture. For example, Krsna says, yajanty avidhi-purvakam: “They worshipin the wrong way,” and Akrura says, tvam yogino yajanty addha: “Pure yogis worship You.”

Therefore, yajanti responds affirmatively to these two doubts: Do the denisens of Kali-yuga engage in worship? Does sankirtana-yajnatruly qualify as an act of worship? Yes, and yes.

The last words of the verse — hi su-medhasah — mean “certainly they are intelligent,” clearly referring to those who worship the Lord by sankirtana. The word hi adds emphasis and clarifies that those who chant the Lord’s holy names are truly qualified, a doubt too commonly expressed about the Lord’s devotees. Great scholars like Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya and Prakasananda Sarasvati, for instance, mistook even Caitanya Mahaprabhu to be an uneducated sentimentalist because of His dancing and chanting. Only when they were defeated by the Lord’s superior intelligence did they recognise that Vaisnavas are the most intelligent, su-medhasah.

Elsewhere in Srimad-Bhagavatam the word madrasah means“whose intelligence,” as in hata-medhasah, meaning “deprived of their intelligence,” or krsnasyakuntha-medhasah, “unlimitedly intelligent Krsna.” The prefix su has a variety of concurrent meanings such as “good,” “fine,” “very,” or “most.” In every case, su prefixes medhasah to increase both the quality and the quantity of the Intelligence referred to.

A student of Srimad-Bhagavatam may point out that in the list of Krsna’s incarnations in the First Canto it is mentioned that Vyasadeva divided the Vedas into several branches because he recognised that the people of this age are less intelligent (alpa-medhasah). How then did these less intelligent persons come to be su-medhasah, “the most intelligent”?

The answer is twofold. It is true that only a very few people are intelligent enough spiritually to embrace the yuga-avatara and His sankirtana process. But by submissive association with pure devotees, less intelligent people can become intelligent enough.

Locana Dasa Thakura confirms that only a few people are intelligent enough to accept the process of sankirtana by saying that the common people of this age will neither believe in nor understand the words of Srimad-Bhagavatam and therefore only a few excepttional souls amongst them will delight in sankirtana. And Srila Prabhupada confirms that they may become elevated: “If one is not intelligent? We should take from the sastra the real path. Then we shall become intelligent.” In other words, by submissively hearing from scripture, and from those Vaisnavas who faithfully represent the words of scripture, one can become intelligent.

In either case, it is clear that those who chant Hare Krsna are the most spiritually intelligent persons of Kali-yuga. They may not be great scholars — actually, in some cases they may not even be literate — but because they understand that the purpose of life is to go back to Godhead, and because they pursue that goal through nama-sankirtana, they are the most intelligent (su-medhasah). To know who is truly intelligent, then, one simply has to find those faithful souls who chant Hare Krsna and worship Lord Caitanya.

Thus the phrase yajanti hi su-medhasah can also be understood in these two ways: (1) those of fine intelligence certainly worship Lord Caitanya by sankirtana, and (2) those who worship Lord Caitanyaby sankirtana are certainly of fine intelligence.

Krsna personally explains that only those who know Him are actually the pure devotees. The Lord says, *bhaktya mam abhijanati yavanyas casmi tattvatah*: “One can understand Me as I am, as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, only by devotional service.” By dint oftheir transcendental knowledge, pure devotees command a sort of wisdom that is unknown and incomprehensible to non-devotees.

In two verses Sri Yamunacarya elaborates on this principle, by which fortunate persons are able to recognise Lord Caitanya’s divinity and thus realise the importance of nama-sankirtana. First the acarya explains those who cannot understand the Lord:

*tvam sila-rupa-caritaih parama-prakrstaih*  
*sattvena sattvikataya prabalais ca sastraih*  
*prakhyata-daiva-paramartha-vidam matais ca*  
*naivasura-prakrtayah prabhavanti boddhum*

**“O my Lord, those influenced by demoniac principles cannot realise You, although You are clearly the Supreme by dint of Your exalted activities, forms, character, and uncommon power, which are confirmed by all the revealed scriptures in the quality of goodness and the celebrated transcendentalists in the divine nature.”**

He then explains that no matter how difficult it is to understand the Lord — even if the Lord tries to hide Himself—He is always accessible to pure devotees:

*ullanghita-trividha-sima-samatisayi-*  
*sambhavanam tava parivradhima-svabhavam*  
*maya-balena bhavatapi niguhyamanam*  
*pasyanti kecid anisam tvad-ananya-bhavah*

**“O my Lord, everything within material nature is limited by time, space, and thought. Your characteristics, however, being unequalled and unsurpassed, are always transcendental to such limitations. You sometimes cover such characteristics by Your own energy, hut nevertheless Your unalloyed devotees are always able to sec You under all circumstances.”**

In summary, the last line of the krsna-varnam verse glorifies those who engage in sankirtana, praise not given to worshippers in earlier,more religious ages. It is therefore said that despite Kali-yuga’s many faults, the opportunity to easily attain the topmost perfection bynama-sankirtana makes it an age in which even demigods and great sages desire to take birth. Sukadeva Gosvami thus praises Kalbyuga in this way:

*kaler dosa-nidhe rajann asti hy eko mahan gunah*  
*kirtanad eva krsnasya mukta-sangah param vrajet*

**“My dear King! Although Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, there is still one good quality about this age: Simply by chanting the Hare Krsna maha-mantra, one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom.”**

**\*\*\*\***

Tis was a brief glorification of Caitanya Mahaprabhu through analysis of the verse most often quoted to lend scriptural authority to His appearance and mission. The verse establishes the sacrifice for this age—nama-sankirtana; the beneficiary of that sacrifice — Lord Caitanya; the performing priests — Nityananda Prabhu and others; and the performers of sacrifice — the Lord’s intelligent devotees. The diversity of these transcendental characteristics of Lord Caitanya attain unity through offenceless devotional service, the perfection of unity in diversity Krsna describes to Arjuna:

*brahmarpanam brahma havir*  
*brahmagnau brahmana hutam*  
*brahmaiva tena gantavyam*  
*brahma-karma-samadhina*

**“A person who is fully absorbed in Krsna consciousness is sure to attain the spiritual kingdom because of his full contribution to spiritual activities, in which the consummation is absolute and that which is offered is of the same spiritual nature.”**

In closing, I offer my respectful obeisances unto that golden-complexioned Lord who has appeared along with His associates to benefit the unfortunate souls of this age with the rarest devotional treasure—vraja-bhakti. The eminent Vaisnava scholar Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya noted Lord Caitanya’s unparalleled and incalculably valuable contribution to the conditioned souls of this age:

*kalan nastam bhakti-yogam nijam yah*  
*praduskartum krsna-caitanya-nama*  
*avirbhutas tasya padaravinde*  
*gadham gadham lryatam citta-bhrgah*

**“Let my consciousness, which is like a honeybee, take shelter of the lotus feet of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who has just now appeared as Sri Krsna Caitanya Mahaprabhu to teach the ancient system of devotional service to Himself. This system had almost been lost due to the influence of time.”**

May this attempt at glorying Caitanya Mahaprabhu bring at least some pleasure to that compassionate, golden-complexioned Lord.
